# Comsky Books POS: architecture and build spec (retail first, then pharmacy and restaurant)

> **Status:** design only. Nothing here is built, no source file was changed, no gate (assert, shots, build, perf) was run, and this is the only repo file written. **Revision 2, 2026-10-25:** two adversarial reviews produced 46 findings (4 blockers, 23 majors, 19 minors; six themes were found by both reviews); each was re-checked against the code and the primary pages and applied, and the **Review log** at the end says what each one changed and which did not reproduce as stated. Written against `main` at `5aee765`. **While revision 2 was being written, the custom-fields, reporting-by-field, stock-variants and Back-control work (Phase 30) was fast-forwarded into `main` as `1d22e84` (2026-10-25; NOT deployed, the VM still runs `687c062`), and a docs commit `d959028` followed [C `git log`, CLAUDE.md]**; so every statement that depended on that work being unmerged (the writer's neighbour `bulkPlan.ts`, the carry funnel, `sync/caps.ts`, the P-0b2 gate, the order of shared files) was re-checked at `d959028` and updated, and the `.wt-cf` worktree (now the same commit) is no longer a separate source. **Line numbers cited as [C file:line] without a commit are as read at `5aee765`, except those marked `d959028`; `store.ts`, `types.ts`, `sync.ts`, `conflict.js`, `inventory.ts`, `backupMigrate.ts` and `importMap.ts` moved with the merge, the symbols are stable, and the first build step re-reads them.** This is the build contract for the owner's request of 2026-10-21: *"Tally is not used in POS as it is weak ... a POS function in this, so retailers can be targeted. Check what is good in [Petpooja, Marg, LogicERP and the international tools] and add."*
> **What it is built from.** The five research documents of this workstream, read in full: `docs/research/pos-retail-market.md` **[RT]**, `pos-restaurant-market.md` **[RS]**, `pos-pharma-market.md` **[PH]**, `pos-payments-hardware-compliance.md` **[PY]** and `pos-code-audit.md` **[AU]** (its revision 2, against `5aee765`); the code they cite, re-read where a decision here depends on it; `docs/design/batch-serial-labels-spec.md` **[TS]** (36 decisions T1 to T36, its engines merged as unwired pure files); `.wt-cf/docs/design/custom-fields-spec.md` **[CF]** with its build notes; and the repo brief `CLAUDE.md` (Phase 20 to 29 and MCP gotchas). **`docs/design/mobile-app-spec.md` exists** (created 2026-10-25 20:59, seven minutes before revision 1 of this file, which wrongly said it did not): its sections 0.3, 2.3, 2.5.1, 2.5.6, 2.6 and 3.2 to 3.6 were read for revision 2 and the POS identity, numbering and engine-lock plans were reconciled with them (PD40); the mobile research (`mobile-backend-readiness.md`, `mobile-personas-and-jobs.md`, `mobile-tech-options.md`) is cited as before.
> **Evidence tags** (the repo's, kept so a reader can tell fact from opinion): **[C file:line]** read in this repo at `HEAD`; **[CF ...]** read in the `.wt-cf` worktree (whose files are on `main` since `1d22e84`); **[AU n]**, **[RT n]**, **[RS n]**, **[PH n]**, **[PY n]**, **[TS n]** stated in that document at that section, **not re-checked here unless a line says so**; **[W]** a page or file fetched and read in revision 2 (Appendix C item 1 lists them); **[M]** a number I computed in this session (Appendix B); **[J]** my judgement; **[unverified]** could not be checked. External facts are paraphrased.
> **What this document did not do, said first.** (1) **Web research was limited in revision 2.** The web-search budget of the turn was already exhausted, so only direct page fetches were possible; the ones that worked are listed in Appendix C item 1 (Chrome's durability post, Petpooja's pricing page, Electron's timelines page, CBIC Circulars 164 and 167, the Packaged Commodities Rules, FSSAI's order, the CDSCO Drugs Rules, Notification 10/2023-CT) and the CBIC Rule 46 page failed its certificate check again. The section 34(2) credit-note cut-off, Rule 46 and 46A after 2021, the Rs 2,500 apparel wording and the portal tables for 9(5) supplies therefore stay as the siblings left them: **dated data with a source note, never a constant in code** (decision PD26). Every other statutory or vendor fact is carried from the sibling documents with their tags. (2) **No hardware, no live product, no running app**: every statement about a printer, scanner, drawer, scale, terminal, soundbox or a competitor's behaviour is documentation or marketing as the siblings read it. (3) **No legal or tax advice**; section 11.2 lists what a CA, an advocate and a pharmacist must sign before each vertical ships. (4) The numbers marked **[M]** come from small scripts run in the session scratchpad (not committed): the integer-arithmetic vectors of Appendix B.1 to B.4 and, in revision 2, the cess residual, the allocation comparison, the apparel band and the pull-check timing of B.5 to B.8; they prove the algorithms on the cases shown, not the whole suite.
> **How to read it.** Section 0 is the decision table (each decision can be vetoed; the default is the one written). Sections 1 to 9 are the design. Section 10 is the slice plan with exit gates, the order against every other build, file lanes, the assertion plan and the harness recipes. Section 11 is what needs a human. Appendix A lists the code anchors, Appendix B the worked vectors, Appendix C what was not verified, Appendix D the sources, and the **Review log** at the end what each of the 46 review findings changed (and which did not reproduce as stated).

---

## 0. The decisions, in one table

"Why" cites the evidence. "Veto" marks the ones where I expect the owner to have a view (all are listed again, with their defaults, in section 11).

| # | Decision | Why | Veto |
|---|---|---|---|
| PD1 | **Order of the verticals: general retail and supermarket first (slices P-1 to P-5), pharmacy second (P-6), restaurant third (P-7), aggregators last (P-8).** Swap rule: if the tracking lane's T-2 has not landed when P-5 ends, P-7 (restaurant counter service, which depends on nothing outside the POS lanes) is built before P-6 with no rework; the two touch disjoint files | Retail needs the shared foundation and nothing else, and is where books-in-the-box is a reason to switch [RT 0.3 D1, D6]. Pharmacy's increment (about 60 to 80 builder-days) rides engines (batch cells, FEFO, scan, MRP guard) that the tracking lane is building anyway for retail v2 [PH 6.4; TS 15.2]. Restaurant's increment (about 75 to 105 builder-days) is almost entirely new surface that nothing else exercises, plus a tax class and a charge law that need a CA first [RS 7.2, 4.10]. **Revision 2:** the v1 retail segment is named, not implied (1.3.1): barcoded general retail, stationery, hardware, gifts, mobile accessories without serials, and a supermarket whose items carry no expiry batches until slice P-2b; restaurant v1 is sold as counter service (bakery, sweet shop, QSR, canteen) only, never as dine-in or aggregator-heavy until captain devices and the aggregator middleware exist (RV32, RV33) | yes |
| PD2 | **The POS is its own root and its own writer on the same `Voucher`, not a mode of the voucher screen.** `PosRoot` replaces the shell on a terminal in POS mode and is mounted full-bleed over the shell for a person who opens it from the menu; the writer is `core/pos/bill.ts`, built beside CF's `core/bulkPlan.ts` | The Sales screen is 4,118 lines, takes 115 key actions for a 10-line invoice and asks four questions before the first item [AU 0.2]; three other builds edit it [AU 12]; Bulk Entry is the precedent for a second writer [AU 1.1] | no |
| PD3 | **Voucher-per-bill is the system of record. Summary posting (the audit's lane S) is not built in this spec, and the Rs 200 daily consolidated invoice is not offered.** Every bill is a numbered invoice | The law makes nearly every bill a numbered invoice (s.31(3)(b) and the Rule 46 proviso allow a consolidated day invoice only below Rs 200, to an unregistered buyer who does not ask) [AU 3.4; PY 3.1]; summary posting breaks Table 13, the unit chain, cancel and returns [AU 3.4]; the wall is the O(N) work per bill, which lane A removes **within the supported envelope of PD38** (above about 1,500 bills a day per GSTIN v1 does not sell the POS, and the owner's numbers decide whether lane S or a hybrid is then designed) | yes (needs the owner's numbers, 11.1) |
| PD4 | **Tender shapes: S1 for a single-tender bill to an unregistered buyer** (the party leg IS the tender ledger; one voucher) **and S2 for everything else** (a Sales voucher to the buyer, or to a per-outlet "Walk-in customers" debtor, then **one Receipt voucher with a Dr line per tender**, in the same transaction). S3 (tender legs inside the Sales voucher), S4 and S5 are not used | S3 corrupts GST by adding a debit leg to taxable value (`gst.ts:161-177`) [AU 0.4, 2.1]; S2 with a clearing debtor keeps every Receipt an ordinary Dr cash/bank Cr party document and the cash ledger exact at every moment; S1 halves the voucher count on the large majority of bills that are single-tender [J, unverified: the audit says most Indian shops have few mixed-tender bills] | no |
| PD5 | **All money inside `core/pos` is integer paise. Tax is never re-implemented: the POS calls the existing `computeItemGst` (through one pseudo-line per rate bucket) so its rounding is the Sales screen's rounding.** MRP-inclusive pricing derives, per rate bucket, the largest taxable base whose base plus CGST plus SGST plus cess does not exceed the bucket's inclusive total; the 0 to 2 paise left over go to Round Off | The engine rounds CGST and SGST each as `round2(base * rate / 200)` on the bucket sum [C `gst.ts:61-83`]; GSTR-1 reads the posted legs and tolerates about Re 1 between a bucket's tax and rate times taxable [C `gst.ts` `RATE_TOLERANCE`]; a basket of MRP goods must total the sum of the MRPs, which a 2-decimal tax-exclusive rate cannot do [AU 1.3]. Over T = 1 to 200,000 paise the leftover was 0, 1 or 2 paise at every slab **without cess** and **0 to 3 paise with an ad valorem cess** (28% with 22% cess: 2,344 of 200,000 totals leave 3; 28% with 60%: 10,639) [M, Appendix B.1 and B.5]; the Round Off leg absorbs it (RV16) | no |
| PD6 | **A bill-level discount is allocated across the lines before tax (largest remainder, in paise) and is stored in the line amounts; a discount after the sale is only a credit note** | s.15(3) and Circular 92/11/2019: an invoice-recorded discount leaves the value of supply, a later one only under conditions [AU 1.4; RT 6 row 5; both [W]]; a Discount ledger in the extras sits outside taxable value and overcharges tax [AU 1.4] | no |
| PD7 | **Round-off is a company setting. Default: nearest rupee, half up, for a bill of tax-exclusive goods (what the voucher screen does today); down only (or none) when the bill holds any item with an MRP and the MRP guard on.** The 0 to 2 paise of PD5 are always absorbed by the same Round Off leg, never added to a customer's total | Round-up on MRP goods can exceed the sum of the retail prices, which Rule 18(2) of the Packaged Commodities Rules bars; whether it is judged per pack or per bill is unread [PY 3.7, 6 item 4]; Rule 18(2) itself was read this revision [W]: no sale of a packed commodity at a price above its retail sale price. Round-down gives away about 50 paise a bill against round-to-nearest if the fractions are uniform (about Rs 1.46 lakh a year per terminal at 800 bills a day [M]), so the terminal wizard and the Z report's round-off line **show that estimate and the reason**, and the CA's per-pack-or-per-bill answer sets the default (RV46) | yes (CA) |
| PD8 | **Numbering: one POS voucher type per terminal for each of Sales, Credit Note, Receipt, Payment and Contra; each series is device-owned (the terminal numbers locally and never asks the cloud on the Accept path), and the terminal CLAIMS what it has issued in batches of at most 50, off the Accept path, before each push, through the same server function the mobile app's `/m/v1/numbers/claim` uses (mobile-app-spec 2.5.6).** A template validator keeps every printed number inside 16 characters of upper-case letters, digits, `/` and `-` and refuses a Monthly restart unless the template carries `{mm}`; `posSeries.next` is `max(counter, one scan of the type per window, carriedNumber, burnedNumber, the server's last answer) + 1`; a **replacement terminal starts above the issue ledger's maximum and above the last number the manager declares was issued**, and a range of bills that never synced is **never** filled with cancelled shells (2.4 case 18, 7.1). POS types are written by `commitPosBill` and refused by every other writer | Rule 46(b): consecutive, at most 16 characters, unique for a financial year, several series allowed [AU 4; PY 3.3]; the cloud round trip is up to 4 s and a claim storm after an offline day (`vchNumbering.ts:20`, `sync/numbering.ts:153`) [AU 4]; the mobile spec's device-owned series with a batched claim (at most 50 numbers a request, `taken` flags a clone, the server returns the next number at enrolment) is the same design, so there is one protocol [mobile-app-spec 2.5.6; C `backend/src/numbering.js:137-149`]; no other device writes a terminal's type, so there is nothing to clash [AU 4]; shells would have removed real sales' tax and made their cash surface as "cash over" (RV6) | yes (cloud numbering as an option) |
| PD9 | **The bill date is the device clock (Asia/Kolkata), shown on screen and on every bill, never carried from the last voucher.** A guard compares the clock with the terminal's last bill and with the server's time when online: skew over 5 minutes warns, over 1 day blocks until a manager acknowledges with a reason | `loadInto` and every save carry the last voucher's date; a terminal opened on Monday would post Saturday [AU 4 last bullet; C `store.ts:357`, `:702`] | no |
| PD10 | **A POS bill is never altered in a voucher screen.** Allowed: a cancel that keeps the number (a shell), only while the bill's shift is open and with the right and a reason; after that a credit note. A cashier has no Delete, no Undo of a printed bill and no Alter | Undo deletes and leaves a hole; Delete leaves a gap; an altered paid bill is an audit finding; the stored rate is 4 decimals and a re-save through the grid's 2-decimal input would re-price the line [AU 4, 5; C `undo.ts:118-119`] | no |
| PD11 | **A return carries `pos.originalId` and prints the original's number and date; it is limited to what was sold less what was returned; an exchange is a Credit Note plus a Sales voucher in one transaction with one net tender. The credit note's tax comes from the original lines' own snapshot (rate, cess, taxability and HSN copied through `pos.originalId`), never from today's snapshot and never from a re-run rate rule; a cash refund credits the refunding terminal's own cash ledger; a return of a bill made on another terminal is online-only (PD41).** The credit note's stock line is booked at the cost the goods left at (a new optional `InventoryLine.costValue`, slice P-4b), because the engine otherwise books an inward line at its amount and a return at the sale price inflates average cost | A cash bill has no bill reference today, so a return cannot be tied to its bill [AU 5]; `inventory.ts` books an inward line at `amount` [C `inventory.ts:129`]; Rule 53(1A) names the original's serial and date [PY 1.7] | yes (P-4b is an engine edit) |
| PD12 | **Cash: one cash ledger per terminal ("Cash - T1"), zero-based per shift.** Open = a Contra from the safe ledger for the float; close = blind count, over or short posted as a Payment (short) or Receipt (over) to "Cash short and excess", then a Contra back to the safe. UPI, card and wallet ledgers are per outlet, not per terminal, and each is a Collections ledger that must return to zero through a **settlement voucher** (PD42) | A per-terminal ledger gives drawer-level books with no new engine [AU 2.4]; no Journal with a cash ledger, as in Tally; per-terminal UPI ledgers would clutter the balance sheet for no reconciliation gain (the bank statement is per account) [J] | no |
| PD13 | **Customers are a light `customers` table keyed on the phone number with a consent record; a party ledger exists only for credit customers. Loyalty points, store credit and gift vouchers are derived from an append-only `custEvents` table; consent and its withdrawal are events too, and the current consent is their fold (RV17)** | 100,000 ledgers ruin the picker and the Day Book [AU 1.6]; a balance edited by several terminals is a conflict magnet, an event log is not [AU 1.6]; a phone number is personal data from the DPDP duties of about May 2027 [PY 4.5] | no |
| PD14 | **A held bill is a device-local `posDrafts` row: no number, no stock reservation, not synced, not in the company backup; the cart itself is written to it on every change (relaxed durability is acceptable for a draft: at worst the last second of edits; a saved BILL is strict, PD39)** | Optional vouchers burn a number and are left out of Table 13; Sales Orders are numbered documents [AU 5]; a power cut must return the cart [RT 5 row 10] | no |
| PD15 | **Stock policy default: allow a sale below zero silently and report it** (company setting: allow, warn or block); tracked items follow the tracking rules per item. Two counters selling the last unit is detected, not prevented, and listed in an Oversell report | A prompt per bill is the wrong default at a counter; sync raises no conflict between two different bills; the tracking design checks units across devices only with its optional reserve [AU 6; TS 10.1] | yes |
| PD16 | **A `posEvents` table is the detective log**: void, cancel, discount over cap, price override, open item, return, refund, no-sale, cash in or out, reprint, drawer open, approval granted or refused, login, lock, shift open and close, clock warning. It is append-only and cannot be switched off; a tender's confirmation after the bill is saved is a `tender` event, never a write to the voucher (RV13) | Rights are client-side only; theft prevention is detective until the server enforces (AU 9); the Companies (Accounts) Rules audit trail may not be disabled [PY 3.8, [Ws]] | no |
| PD17 | **Rights: a new area `pos`, a `Role.pos` block of limits (discount cap, override, void, refund, no-sale, reprint, close, open item, who may approve what), approval by a different person's full PIN, and a fast per-device counter PIN only for switching cashier on an unlocked terminal.** Honestly detective until lane F | The role model has no cancel, discount, override, refund, reprint or no-sale right and a cashier needs the Accounting area, which opens the cash book (`access.ts:129-133`) [AU 9]; a PBKDF2 check is 260 ms or more [AU 9] | no |
| PD18 | **One additive Dexie version for P-0 (five tables), numbered after or merged with the tracking lane's optional `labelTemplates`; later slices add tables in their own additive versions. Backup schema 5, written only when POS data exists** | One editor of `db.ts`; an older tab tolerates a newer database; an older app must refuse a backup it would damage [AU 12; TS 3.7] | no |
| PD19 | **`Company.pos` holds the company policy (tenders, caps, rounding, stock policy, charge rules, outlets, identity lines) and gets the Phase 27 older-client duty from day one: a revision, a per-device mirror, a restore path and a server keep rule, built on the machinery of `Company.entryPolicy`** | A synced company field is erased by an older client's re-save unless defended [C CLAUDE.md Phase 27; AU 10.3; CF CF-14] | no |
| PD20 | **`Voucher.pos` is one optional object carried through CF's carry funnel and classified in `voucher-resave`; the server keeps it for an older client by a capability marker `pos1` and, from P-0c, refuses a put or delete that changes the financial content of a stored voucher carrying `pos` (7.5, RV12)** | The Sales save rebuilds the voucher as a literal, field by field, so any new field is erased on alteration [AU 0.3, 12] | no |
| PD21 | **Lane A (the hot path) is a precondition of the counter UI, and it is split by who it can hurt.** **POS-only, in P-0a:** `commitPosBill` (a binary insert on `(date, number, id)`, one strict-durability transaction, PD39), the O(1) series counter, and a running index fed from ONE Dexie-level choke point and checked against the engines at shift open and close (PD43). **Global, in separate slices P-0g1 to P-0g4, each with an equivalence suite and a HEAD golden, shipped first only for a POS company and scheduled after the CF and tracking merges unless the pilot's numbers force them earlier:** a pull that applies pages without a full reload plus an incremental duplicate-number map (replaces the O(N) `checkDuplicates` and its 5,000-voucher cap), bounded outbox reads, a windowed month of GSTR-1, a streamed backup | An Accept is about 0.5 s at 250,000 bills and 2.3 s at 500,000 on a loaded M4; one terminal makes 292,000 bills a year at 800 a day [AU 3.2, 3.1]; the pull duplicate check alone measured 59 ms at 250,000 and 182 ms at 500,000 vouchers on this machine at load 8 [M, Appendix B.8]; the shared paths every company runs need their own proof that a company without POS reads the same bytes (RV8, RV9, RV10, RV35) | no |
| PD22 | **One device bridge, `electron/deviceBridge.cjs`, for receipts, the drawer, the customer display and the scale, sharing the printer list, the network allow-list, the one-job-at-a-time rule and the Windows RAW helper with the tracking lane's label bridge; `labelLang.cjs` and a new `escposLang.cjs` are its emitters.** A job is structured, never a command; the answer says "sent to the printer"; **printing never blocks Accept, and never starts before the strict commit of the bill has completed** (PD39). **One owner for the file name:** the tracking spec still names it `labelBridge.cjs` (its lines 884, 1048 and 1065 [C]), so its T-7a is amended to `deviceBridge.cjs` before P-5 or T-7a starts (RV34) | The audit calls it `deviceBridge.cjs`, the tracking spec `labelBridge.cjs`: two names for one discipline [RS D10]; a receipt, unlike a label, prints on every bill [PY 2.6] | yes (name) |
| PD23 | **Platform order: the counter is the Windows Electron app.** Android and iPad devices are lookup, order-taking and owner devices until HTTPS and a native shell exist (a Windows 7 or 8 box, a 32-bit PC or an Android-only counter is **not a v1 customer** and the pitch says so; Petpooja's pages list Windows, Android and iOS and do not state offline billing [W petpooja.com/pricing], while the restaurant study read an Android tablet with up to five days of offline billing [RS 2.6]). **The runtime floor** is 64-bit Windows 10 or 11, 4 GB of memory (8 GB recommended) and an SSD recommended; the repo's `^31.4.0` Electron is outside the project's support policy of the latest three majors [W electronjs.org timelines] and is upgraded before P-5's packaging gate with every bridge check re-run (slice P-5p, RV29). **TLS and a hostname come before the first PSP, WhatsApp provider, aggregator webhook or hosted bill** | Only the Windows Electron app reaches every device without extra software; the live site is HTTP only [PY 0 items 11, 14; AU 7] | yes |
| PD24 | **A tender has a confirmation state and a source; v1 ships the manual provider (typed reference, attested by the cashier) and a bank-statement match; the app never stores a card number, CVV or payer VPA; a UPI tender can never carry a surcharge. The v1 UPI dialog defaults to "the customer scans the shop's own QR, the cashier attests", shows the attested amount and an optional UTR tail before it is recorded, and has its own keystroke budget (1.4 Flow A2, RV36).** The UPI link gains optional `tr`, `mc`, `mid`, `msid`, `mtid`, `url` only when configured | A QR proves nothing; the repo's link has no reference [PY 0 items 1, 2]; RBI: only issuers and networks store card data [PY 1.5]; NPCI's 2026 FAQ bars pass-on of the merchant discount [PY 0 item 3] | no |
| PD25 | **E-bill v1 (sold as "share by WhatsApp", never as a "WhatsApp bill": RV44): print, a `wa.me` link that OPENS the cashier's WhatsApp with an itemised text, `mailto:`, and a PDF the person attaches. A hosted bill page waits for TLS; a provider-based WhatsApp bill waits for an account and an opt-in** | `wa.me` cannot attach a file and sends nothing; a provider needs templates and a per-message price [AU 8; PY 4.1, 4.3] | yes |
| PD26 | **Counter GST gates:** the buyer block at Rs 50,000; **a GSTIN on a bill of an e-invoice-liable seller is a hand-off, not a dead end: "Continue as invoice" prepares an Optional Sales voucher (ordinary Sales type, the buyer, the lines, the discount, the place of supply) for the back office's existing e-invoice path and says so; no number of a POS series is spent (RV22)**; a Bill of Supply for a composition company; an invoice-cum-bill of supply for a mixed basket; **the apparel and footwear rate rule ships OFF: it needs the CA's recorded basis, and a price in the band where the answer flips is refused with the three results shown (2.3 step 4, RV16, RV26)**; **a single cash tender at or above the cash cap needs a manager and a reason whoever the customer is (RV27)**; every statutory figure is dated data with a source, never a constant, and the unreadable ones (the s.34(2) cut-off, the Rule 46 amendments after 2021) carry `SCHEMA?` | [AU 1.5, 16; PY 3.1 to 3.5; RT 6] | no |
| PD27 | **Item master gets POS tail fields (short code, favourite, tile colour, price mode, open-item and weighed flags) and a `noStock` flag the stock engine honours.** Barcodes are the tracking lane's `ItemBarcode` list; until it lands the alias and part number carry them (a Tally retailer's alias barcodes keep working). **P-1 does not wait for the scan layer (T-5b):** the POS scan field is an uncontrolled input resolved by the merged `resolveScan` on Enter, with no bare-letter hotkey on the screen. **Bare digits never open a quantity edit: they always go to the scan field and resolve as a code first; quantity comes only from `*` or the stepper (1.5, 3.2, RV24)**; the item gets an optional `localName` for the receipt and kitchen ticket (RV38) | The scan layer needs `keys.ts` after Back; a POS with no bare-letter hotkeys and an uncontrolled field gets the same safety for the common Enter-terminated wedge scanner [AU 7; TS 14.3] | no |
| PD28 | **Promotions are data with a fixed set of shapes** (percent or amount off, price at, slab, bill-level, time window, coupon) that emit discount allocations before tax, **built in Phase 2 as P-3a so the first pilot can run a scheme; the bundle (buy X get Y priced once) and the combo ship in the same family but default OFF until the owner's CA has attested the GST reading (a company flag, P-3b); loyalty, store credit and gift vouchers wait for the CA and the DPDP notice (P-3c)**. No scripting language; a free line needs a reason and is flagged for the credit-block note | [RT 4.7, 10.4; AU 1.4; RV32] | no |
| PD29 | **A new or replacement terminal joins by the ordinary pull (lane A), never by restoring a backup file over a company.** The existing restore is a REPLACE: it queues an outbox put for every row in the file and a delete for every row the device holds that the file lacks, and its writes skip the cloud's cancelled-voucher guard [C `db.ts:505-571`, `backupGuard.ts:77-97`, `conflict.js:68`]. For a POS company a restore that would delete or replace any voucher carrying `pos` is **refused** on the client and on the server. A backup is rehearsed on a scratch machine that never signs in (10.4). A **seed-from-backup** import (tables only, no outbox rows, a refused device with unsent rows, the pull cursor and baselines carried in new header fields) is specified in 7.4 and built only if the measured lane-A pull at the envelope's ceiling is too slow on the low-end PC (P-2c, optional); a "Year roll" or "Period roll" (PD38) starts the next period's company with the POS configuration and customers carried | Restoring a 250,000-voucher backup onto a new terminal would push about 310 MB (834 batches of 300) as new revisions, replace every document edited since the backup with yesterday's version on the cloud and on every device, bring a cancelled bill back to life, and, on a device that already holds today's bills, delete them everywhere [C; M arithmetic]; the backup carries no pull cursor, so the next pull would replay everything anyway [C `db.ts:449-472`] (RV1) | yes |
| PD30 | **Restaurant: the order is an append-only stream of kitchen-ticket events and takes no voucher number until tender; charges are service lines, the service charge is off by default and needs the owner's dated attestation to turn on, a tip is a Receipt to a liability ledger, dishes are stockless, the aggregator class (ECO95, "tax paid by the operator under 9(5)") applies per line to restaurant service only: goods on the same order (packaged items, manufactured ice cream) post as the restaurant's own taxable supply at their own rate with their own invoice and the operator's TCS flagged, and an aggregator order is its own voucher type outside the restaurant's invoice series (4.5, RV11, RV20).** v1 is one counter device per outlet **and is sold as counter service only (RV33)** | [RS 0.2 items 1, 14, 16; 4.3, 4.4, 7.3; W Circulars 164 and 167] | yes |
| PD31 | **Pharmacy: the capture gate keys on a schedule snapshot on the line; the prescription record lives on the voucher (`pos.rx`) and the registers are reports over vouchers; the schedule list is dated data loaded at enablement and shown with its source and date; the product never says "compliant".** | [PH 3.8, 3.9, 6.3] | no |
| PD32 | **Aggregators: a cloud webhook receiver writes a non-posting `pendingOrders` table (the external order id is the idempotency key); the counter accepts, rejects or holds; only then does the POS writer make the bill.** Middleware (UrbanPiper-style) first, direct partner APIs and ONDC later | Unsigned, time-limited webhooks need a public HTTPS endpoint that answers in seconds; the REST API writes raw vouchers with no numbering [RS 3.7, 0.2 items 6, 15] | yes |
| PD33 | **Seams:** every new `Voucher` or table key is classified for the MCP bundle (the PII-bearing ones dropped), the REST API cannot write a POS type or a POS table, and every edit of a file in a lock ends with the drift run of **each** package whose lock holds it: `cd mcp && npm run drift` and the mobile engine drift (mobile-app-spec 2.3); the lock map in 10.2 lists, file by file, which lock holds it and which POS slice edits it | [AU 12; C CLAUDE.md MCP gotchas; C `mcp/engine-lock.json` (52 files); mobile-app-spec 2.3] | no |
| PD34 | **A training mode runs the whole counter and writes nothing**; its receipts print "TRAINING - NOT A BILL" | A supermarket without proper training is a recurring complaint [RT 7] | no |
| PD35 | **One company per GSTIN per financial period; outlets in one state share it** (a godown, a terminal series and a cash ledger each). Revision 1 said "a company per financial year per outlet" and, in the same row, that outlets share a company: the second is the rule. The bound on N is the **envelope of PD38** (a ceiling per active company and a Period roll), not the financial year | [AU 3.3, 10.1; RT 4.16; RV23] | no |
| PD36 | **Server enforcement is a four-tier plan (credential allow-list, immutability, value policy, second-identity approval). v1 ships only the cheapest slice of tier 2 (a POS voucher's financial content cannot be changed or deleted, P-0c, 7.5 layer 5) and the rest waits for lane F.** v1 says "detective" on screen. **Tier 1 does not invent a second identity: a terminal credential is a `device_session` of the mobile spec's BE-2 with a `terminal` kind and a `counter` role (RV28).** The invites and member-accounts work (assessed, not built) is a prerequisite of tiers 1 and 4 | [AU 9; HANDOFF "Team members and invites"; mobile-app-spec 2.6, 3.2 BE-2] | yes |
| PD37 | **Parity and brand:** the POS is a `beyond` capability row (never in the parity percentage); no "AI-powered" claim; no "Tally" in any product string except truthful migration copy; every compliance sentence says what the software prints or checks and never that the shop "is compliant" | [C CLAUDE.md "Phases 18-21", "Branding"; PH 6.3] | no |
| PD38 | **Supported envelope and the Period roll.** v1 certifies a company whose **active period holds at most C vouchers** (default **C = 300,000, a [J] start value that the memory spike S-env replaces with a measured one**; a banner at 80% of C) and is sold only to a GSTIN whose bills are about **1,500 a day or fewer, all counters together** (two rolls a year at most; Appendix B.9). Past 80% of C a **Period roll** (a one-sided Split Company Data, 2.15 and 7.8) starts the next period at a month start (a quarter start for a quarterly filer); **billing is never blocked**. A customer above the envelope is **not sold the POS in v1**: the owner's Q2 numbers decide whether lane S or a hybrid is designed, before P-1 begins, not after | At the stated 3,000 bills a day a company passes 500,000 vouchers on day 167 and holds about 1.1 million a year; the audit measured 722 MB of heap at 500,000 bills and about 1.6 GB extrapolated at 1.1 million, and judges that a 4 GB PC cannot hold it comfortably [C audit 3.1, 3.3; J]; `loadCompanyData` reads every voucher of the company into memory at open and its IndexedDB read is unmeasured [C `db.ts:573-598`; C `PERF.md:325-330`]; revision 1 stated no heap, no cold start and no envelope (RV3, RV23) | yes |
| PD39 | **A saved POS bill is committed with strict IndexedDB durability, and nothing prints, opens the drawer or hands over goods until that commit has completed.** The default for a `readwrite` transaction has been relaxed since Chrome 121 (the `complete` event fires when the data is in the operating system's buffer, which is flushed about every couple of seconds) [W developer.chrome.com] and the app sets no durability [C `db.ts:111`, `dexie.js:3032`; grep]. A DBCore middleware makes the bill's transaction (and the series high-water mark written in it) strict and leaves every other write as it is; an Electron-only journal in the main process (fsync'd: bill id, number, total) written before the print job reconciles against IndexedDB at start-up and lists a printed-but-missing bill as a **recovered bill**. The pilot test is a real power pull on the bench kit, not a process kill | Electron 31 is Chromium 126, past 121 [J]; a bill that completed, printed and was handed over could be missing after a power cut a second later, and a series rebuilt from vouchers would issue its number a second time: two bills with one number, one of them in no book (RV2, RV21). Whether the fsync fits the 300 ms Accept gate on a hard disk and on eMMC is **unmeasured**: slice S-dur measures it first | no |
| PD40 | **One identity, one numbering protocol and one engine lock with the mobile track.** A terminal credential is a `device_session` of the mobile spec's BE-2 (kind `terminal`, role `counter`) and the POS adds only the columns that spec's tables lack; the claim is **one function in `backend/src/numbering.js`** called by both `POST /m/v1/numbers/claim` and a terminal route; the POS and mobile specs allocate SQL migration numbers together at build; a file in both packages' locks trips both drift checks, so a slice that edits one names both | The mobile spec exists and specifies all three (device model D-15 and D-16, series with a batched claim 2.5.6, an engine entry with its own `engine-lock.json` 2.3); revision 1 said it did not exist and would have built each twice (RV6, RV28) | no |
| PD41 | **Returns and stored value across terminals.** Returnable quantity is the fold, across every terminal, of the `pos.originalId` credit notes; a return of a bill made on **another terminal** is allowed only online (or when no credit note for that bill is pending anywhere this device can see), says so on the return screen, and a pull that finds credit notes beyond the sold quantity writes an **Over-returned bill** row (Exceptions, the Z close list, the Sync Mismatch Report). A gift voucher or store-credit redemption is an online check, or an offline redemption capped per terminal per day and re-checked at sync; loyalty burn follows the same rule | Two offline terminals can each return the same Rs 5,000 bill and pay out twice; nothing in revision 1 detected it (RV5) | yes |
| PD42 | **UPI, card and wallet Collections ledgers are settled by a settlement voucher, and an unmatched tender has a lawful end.** A batch picker over unsettled tenders posts two vouchers in one transaction (2.4 case 16): a Contra (Dr Bank for the net credit, Cr the Collections ledger) and a Payment (Dr Merchant discount, Dr Input GST on it where the owner's CA says credit is available, Cr the Collections ledger), with the gross, discount, GST and net typed from the provider's settlement report and checked to add up; the bank-statement matcher stays one-to-one and matches the single bank entry. A tender still unmatched after the owner's wait (default 7 days) is written off to a named loss ledger or converted to a receivable from the cashier, with approver and reason (case 17); a suite asserts every Collections ledger returns to zero after a simulated statement cycle | Revision 1 posted Collections as received and never designed how they reach the bank ledger, the merchant discount or its GST, or what happens to a tender that never arrives; the matcher is one statement line to one entry [C `brsMatch.ts` rule 4] (RV7) | no |
| PD43 | **The running index has one choke point, an oracle and a deterministic order.** It is fed by Dexie `creating`, `updating` and `deleting` hooks on `vouchers` (the repo already hooks the same table for undo [C `undo.ts:211-219`]) so every write path (save, delete, pull apply, restore, undo, Bulk Entry, cancel, split) reaches it, and a rebuild follows a company open, a restore and a split; it is checked against the engines at shift open and close and fails closed to the engine's answer; the shift's expected cash is computed by the oracle from the shift's own vouchers, never read from the index. Vouchers are ordered by `(date, number, id)` wherever lane A inserts, which equals what a reload gives (a stable sort over id-ordered rows), so live order equals reloaded order and a FIFO or average-cost sequence cannot differ between a live device and a restarted one | `sortVouchers` is stable and `saveVoucher` appends the new voucher last among equal `(date, number)`, while a reload returns them in id order; `stockMovements` numbers moves by array position, so cross-type ties (`T1/000123` and `T2/000123` on one day) are ordered differently on two devices [C `store.ts:313-314, 717-726`, `inventory.ts:45-74`] (RV8) | no |
| PD44 | **Operations are part of v1:** a signed installer; an update channel that applies a new version only when the shift is closed and enforces a minimum version through the capability handshake; a diagnostics page with an exportable support bundle (versions, last sync, printer test results, queue depth, last backup, series state, the journal); and one written **lapse contract** (billing, Z close, local backup and reprint are never gated; sync-dependent tools say why they are off) | There is no auto-updater anywhere in the repo (grep), the Windows installer is unsigned and was never run on Windows [C CLAUDE.md], switching POS on is refused while any device lacks `pos1`, and the field's top complaint is support during a rush [RT 7] (RV30) | yes |
| PD45 | **Day-one data: a retail item and opening-data importer is part of v1 (P-1i), and the v1 segment is named (1.3.1).** Barcodes, alias list, MRP, selling and purchase price, pack, short code, tile colour, HSN, rate, opening stock by godown and customer opening balances load through the wave-3 mapped importer with a preview, a duplicate-barcode report and saved column mappings for the common exports; "a real store's masters loaded in under half a day" is a pilot gate | The shipped item importer maps only name, unit, group, HSN, GST rate, taxability, cess, opening quantity and rate [C `importMap.ts:64-74`]; a 4,000-item store arriving with a spreadsheet of barcodes and MRPs could not load it (RV31) | no |

---

## 1. Product

### 1.1 Positioning, in one paragraph

**"The counter that posts to the same books."** A POS bill is the accounting voucher, so GST, stock, cash, credit and the CA's reports agree by construction; there is no day-end summary to chase, no sync to a ledger and no second GST engine [RT 10.1, 3.3 item 3]. The proof points the field skips or gets wrong are the ones this spec makes testable: GST correct on discounts, schemes and price slabs; MRP-inclusive billing that totals the sum of the MRPs; a shift close with a blind count and a maximum difference; detective controls on by default; keyboard-first speed that also works by touch; Tally XML in and out so the CA loses nothing [RT 10.1]. The target customer for v1 is a **small multi-counter store**: one to three counters, a few thousand items, an owner or manager and two to six cashiers, a CA who uses Tally or Comsky, **inside the envelope of PD38 (about 1,500 bills a day per GSTIN, all counters together) and the screening sheet of 1.3.1**; sold through the owner's Tally partner channel, whose local support answers the loudest complaint about Marg and GoFrugal [RT 0.2 items 3 and 12; 7]. One published price per company, no per-terminal or per-user fee, every counter feature included; add-ons only where a third party charges (WhatsApp messages, a payment partner) [RT 10.1]. The honest pitch for a Tally retailer is "keep your CA's Tally if you need to; bill in Comsky; the books are the same books" (Tally XML export and import have never met a live TallyPrime file, so a pilot with a real Tally company is part of the exit gate, 10.4) [RT 8].

### 1.2 Personas

| Persona | Job at the counter | What the software must give | What it must never let them do |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Counter cashier** (retail; also the QSR cashier) | Scan or tap items, take the tender, hand over the bill, hold a bill for the next customer, take back a return with a manager | Zero questions before the first item; the cursor never leaves the scan field; one key per tender; an exact-cash key; print and drawer as side effects of the tender; a visible offline contract; a PIN switch in about 2 seconds when staff change | See the expected cash before counting; open the Day Book or cash book; edit or delete a saved bill; discount above the cap; sell above MRP; refund cash above the cap without a manager |
| **Store manager** | Approve a discount, void, return or no-sale; close shifts; read the exceptions; correct a mistake the lawful way; replace a terminal | A one-key approval prompt that records who and why; the Z report and the exceptions list per cashier against peers; a remote close of a shift left open by a crashed device; the oversell list; the unmatched-tenders list | Approve their own exception beyond their own cap; hide an event |
| **Owner** | Know today's takings by tender, margin, what was voided or discounted and by whom, whether the cash tallies, whether the UPI arrived | Dashboard widgets and reports that equal the books to the paisa; a cost-per-tender view; the CA's reports without re-keying | Be unable to explain a figure: every number drills to a report and the report to the vouchers |
| **Restaurant captain** (v1: the counter person; v2: a tablet) | Open a table, add items with modifiers, send to the kitchen, move or merge a table, ask for the bill | A table map with derived status; a kitchen ticket per station that cannot be silently altered; change and cancel tickets that print with a banner | Delete a sent line without a reason and an approver; discount without a cap |
| **Restaurant cashier** | Print the provisional bill, split it, take the tender, issue the tax invoice | Split by item or equally with charges apportioned in paise; the tax invoice issued at settlement; token numbers for counter service | Add a service charge by default; print an invoice on an aggregator order |
| **Kitchen** (v1: a printer; v2: a screen) | Read tickets by station, see changes and cancels | Immutable tickets; ADD, EDIT, CANCEL and TRANSFER banners; DUPLICATE on a reprint | None (no write access) |
| **Pharmacist** | Find a medicine by brand or salt, pick the batch, serve a scheduled drug on a prescription, sell a strip or a loose tablet, record what was out of stock | One search box; the earliest sellable batch pre-selected; expired batches refused; the gate asks for prescriber and patient only on a scheduled line; a neutral, price-sorted substitute panel that is off on a scheduled prescription | Sell expired stock; supply a substitute on a scheduled prescription; skip the register entry; be told the shop "is compliant" |

### 1.3 Vertical modes: the order, the v1 scope of each, and the evidence

A **terminal profile** (a device fact, 1.6) picks one of four modes: `retail`, `pharmacy`, `restaurant-counter` (QSR, cafe, bakery, sweet shop: pay first, token) and `restaurant-table` (dine-in: open check, pay later). A company may enable several (a supermarket with a pharmacy counter), each terminal runs one. A mode is a registered pack of hooks (item search extras, pre-tender gates, a post-commit writer, receipt sections, report rows), so the three share the root, the tender, the shift, the rights and the events and differ only where the research says they differ [RS 7.3 table; AU 14 lane H].

| | General retail and supermarket (first) | Pharmacy (second) | Restaurant (third) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Evidence for the position in the order** | The floor of an Indian counter POS is a shift with a cash close, hold and recall, linked returns, schemes, loyalty, role-limited approvals and device support; Comsky has the engine under all of it and none of the counter [RT 0.1 item 1, 0.2 items 1, 2]. The loudest complaints are support, renewals and the price of changing a rule, which published prices and owner-editable policy answer [RT 7]. Pilot with two or three of the owner's own Tally retail customers [RT 0.1 item 5] | Pharmacy is where an accounting-first product lost to a specialist because of the statute (Rule 65 makes batch, manufacturer and licence number part of the record) [PH 0 item 1]; no page read describes a keyboard-first, touch-friendly pharmacy product with a real double-entry ledger and a synced audit trail underneath [PH 2.8]. Its engines are the tracking lane's, which retail v2 (variants, serials) needs as well [RT 10.3 V2.4] | Petpooja wins on price and breadth, not accounting; the opening is books in the same box with correct 5% no-credit, composition and the operator rule [RS 0.2 item 2]. Single-device offline is the market norm; multi-device offline is a premium on-premises tier [RS 2.6] |
| **v1 scope** | Scan-first cart; exact-code lookup with "not found: create or attach"; favourites and tiles; MRP-inclusive pricing with the MRP guard; line and bill discount allocated before tax with caps and approvals; value-slab rate; hold and recall; single and split tender with confirmation states; credit (khata); returns and exchanges linked to the bill; shift, blind close, Z report; receipts at 58 and 80 mm with the drawer; `wa.me` bill; roles and the event log; reports and a golden that equals the Sales screen's returns; customers with consent (no points); promotions (P-3a: a scheme the cashier never keys by hand); the item and opening-data importer (P-1i); the operations kit (PD44) | The batch pick with FEFO (a no-stop pick when unambiguous); expired and near-expiry rules; per-batch MRP with the printed and effective prices apart; strip and loose with its own label; the schedule gate with `rx` capture; the prescription and Schedule H1 registers as reports; licence numbers and the pharmacist on the memo; free-quantity schemes; neutral substitutes; the expiry-return worksheet; batch holds from a regulator's list | **Sold as counter service only** (bakery, sweet shop, QSR, canteen; token numbers, pay first); the dine-in, table and manual-aggregator pieces below are built but not sold to a dine-in or aggregator-heavy outlet until captain devices and the aggregator middleware exist (RV33). Menu with variants, option groups and simple combos on stockless item lines; four order types (dine-in, takeaway with a token, own delivery, manual aggregator order); floors and tables with derived status, move and merge; immutable kitchen tickets per station from the counter PC; settlement into Sales vouchers (S1 and S2), split by item or equally; charges as lines (service charge off); tip as a Receipt; owner-configured reasons and approver; day close with a voids-and-comps review and KOT-versus-bill reconciliation; the 5%, composition, premises-class and 9(5) profile; FSSAI per outlet |
| **Not in v1** | Loyalty points, store credit and gift vouchers (P-3c, after the CA); the bundle and combo promotions until the owner's CA attests (P-3b); batch and expiry at the counter until slice P-2b and the tracking lane (the v1 segment of 1.3.1 excludes them); several outlets with transfers; variants and serials at the counter (the tracking lane, then a P-6-style pack); a payment-terminal integration; a provider WhatsApp bill; B2B bills with an IRN; Android shell | Scheduled-drug register for Schedule X; an interaction checker; incentive-ranked substitutes; doctor commission; refill reminders on scheduled drugs; TPA and ABHA; distributor features (salesman app, route, e-invoice through a GSP) | KDS, courses and fire, the outlet hub and captain tablets, aggregator APIs, QR ordering, recipes with ingredient stock, loyalty and wallet, liquor, reservations, happy-hour pricing |
| **Depends on (outside the POS lanes)** | Nothing for P-1 beyond lane A. Multiple barcodes, packs and weighed labels use the tracking lane's registry when it lands | The tracking lane through T-2 and T-4 (52 to 64 builder-days by its own table); the F1 and F2 fixes to it; a dated schedule list signed off by a pharmacist and an advocate | The operator-pays tax class (P-7a) and a CA's reading of Table 14(b); the stockless rule (PD27) |

### 1.3.1 Who v1 is for: the pilot screening sheet (said at the first call, not at the first complaint)

| Question | v1 serves | v1 does not serve |
|---|---|---|
| Business | a barcoded general store, stationery, hardware, gifts, mobile accessories without serial or IMEI tracking, a supermarket whose items carry no expiry batches (until P-2b), a counter-service restaurant (bakery, sweet shop, QSR, canteen) | a dine-in or aggregator-heavy restaurant (Petpooja's Base plan advertises unlimited users and terminals and aggregator integrations at Rs 12,000 ex tax, and its captain app, KDS and Zomato or Swiggy reconciliation sit in an Operations Manager tier from Rs 20,000 ex tax [W petpooja.com/pricing, indicative, period not stated]); a chemist until P-6; a serial or IMEI seller; batch and expiry at the counter until P-2b |
| Volume | about 1,500 bills a day or fewer per GSTIN, all counters together, inside a company ceiling and a Period roll (PD38) | above that: not sold in v1 (lane S or a hybrid is the owner's decision with Q2's numbers) |
| Machine | 64-bit Windows 10 or 11, 4 GB (8 GB recommended), an SSD recommended (PD23) | Windows 7 or 8, a 32-bit PC, an Android-only counter (v2 with the mobile track) |
| E-invoice | a seller below Rs 5 crore turnover, or one whose B2B bills at the counter are rare enough to hand off (PD26) | a seller above Rs 5 crore with many GSTIN bills at the counter: every such bill is a hand-off to the back office's e-invoice path because the app makes no IRP call. One terminal at 800 bills a day passes Rs 5 crore at an average bill of Rs 171 and three counters at Rs 57 [M: 5,00,00,000 over 292,000 and over 876,000 bills], so a multi-counter store is usually liable by construction. The threshold is Rs 5 crore from 1 August 2023 [W Notification 10/2023-CT through taxheal] |
| Network | patchy internet is fine (offline for cash, credit and recorded UPI); a replaced terminal needs the cloud | a counter that must bill on a brand-new PC that has never reached the cloud |
| Support | the partner answers a counter during a rush (11.1 Q20) | a shop with no support path |
| Hardware | an ESC/POS receipt printer, a wedge scanner, an optional drawer, from the tested list of 6.6 | anything outside the tested-hardware list |

### 1.4 The north-star flows, screen by screen

**Timing budget.** A trained cashier scans about one item every 1.2 s; a 5-item bill is about 6 s of scanning, 2 s to tender and 1 s from Accept to the printer: 9 s, of which **the software's share must stay under about 1 s for the whole bill: 150 ms from scan to the line on screen, 300 ms from Accept to the first bytes at the spooler** [RT 5, J, unverified assumptions]. These are gates measured on the perf harness at 250,000 and 500,000 synthetic bills (10.5), not promises about a shop.

#### Flow A: the 10-second repeat-customer bill by barcode scan (retail)

| # | Screen and state | Action (real keys) | The system does | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | **Idle**: shift open since 09:10, bill date and time in the header, cart empty, the scan field focused, no modal, sync badge "online, 0 waiting" | none | Nothing is asked before the first item: party, sales ledger, godown, series, tax rows, place of supply are terminal defaults | n/a |
| 1 | **Customer (optional)**: the customer chip reads "Walk-in" | scan the loyalty or membership card, or type the last 4 to 10 digits of the phone and Enter (F6 opens the panel) | `customers` found by exact card code or phone suffix (at most 5 candidates, newest first); chip shows name, points, credit due; **no customer is created without the shopper's say-so** | 0.5 s by card, 3 s typed |
| 2 | **"Repeat last basket"** (F6 panel action, only with a customer) | one key | The customer's last bill's lines are added, **re-priced at today's price, stock checked, every change shown** (ghost recall keyed on `pos.customerId`, never on the Cash party) | 0.2 s |
| 3 | **Scan** | scan each item; a second scan of the same item adds 1; `3*` then a scan sets the quantity; a weighed label sets the weight | `resolveScan` (exact: unit, batch, item, pack or variant, scale code, GS1, alias) then the line appears with price, tax and the MRP chip; the cursor stays in the field; a short tone on error only | 150 ms per scan |
| 4 | **Tender** | F2 (cash) or F3 (UPI) | F2 opens the cash dialog with the **Received** box focused: Enter on an empty box is exact cash, a typed amount shows the change. With the terminal setting `exactCashOnKey` (off by default, on for an express counter) **F2 itself commits exact cash**: two actions for a one-item bill (scan, F2), three without it (scan, F2, Enter). F3 opens the UPI dialog with the QR; the dialogs of 1.5 handle everything else | 0.3 s |
| 5 | **Commit** | none | One Dexie transaction with **strict durability** (PD39) writes the voucher(s), outbox rows, events and the series high-water mark; number taken **here**, after every check; **only when that commit has completed** is the print queued, the drawer pulsed on a cash tender and the WhatsApp link offered (only if a phone was given) | Accept to spool 300 ms **including the strict commit** (slice S-dur measures it) |
| 6 | **Done** | none (auto after 2 s, or the next scan) | A one-line toast "T1/000123 saved, Rs 1,614 cash, change 0"; Undo is NOT offered (PD10); the next bill's cart is empty and the scan field focused | n/a |

**Flow A2: the UPI bill (the commonest tender at an Indian counter), with its own budget.** Scan, **F3** (the UPI dialog opens on the exact amount with the shop's own QR, and the customer display shows the same QR), the customer pays, the cashier reads the amount on the soundbox or the customer's screen, the **Received** box (focused, pre-filled with the due amount) takes it and an optional UTR tail, and **Enter** records the tender as `unconfirmed`, source `cashier`: **scan, F3, Enter is three actions**, and the next customer's first scan is accepted while the previous dialog is still closing. The attested amount and the UTR tail are shown on the dialog before Enter and on the receipt after it; an amount different from the due amount becomes a split with cash or a second UPI, **never silently the due amount**, because a payer's app may let the payer edit the amount in an unsigned link [unverified]. The recipe `pos-tender-upi-flow` drives it with real keys (RV36).
States that must exist, each with its words and its keys:
- **Offline.** Banner "Offline: selling works; UPI and card are recorded as unconfirmed; 14 bills waiting to send". Cash, credit and recorded tenders work; numbering is local; stock and prices are the terminal's own copy. A return of a bill made on **another** counter is refused while offline ("this bill was made on counter T2: connect to return it", PD41); the return screen says "last sync 10:42" and, if the bill is not found, "not on this machine yet" (never "no such bill").
- **Hold.** F7 holds (label: customer or time); the cart clears; F8 lists holds (age, cashier, total, items); recall re-prices and flags changes; **open holds are listed at shift close and must be discarded or carried by name** (10.4 test).
- **Error: unknown code.** The field keeps focus and shows "Code 8901234567890 is not in your items", with Create item (Masters right) and Attach to an item; for a cashier without the right, "Ask the manager". **Never silent and never appended to the next scan** (the silent failure of the Sales screen [AU 1.3]).
- **Error: price missing, above MRP, expired batch, stock policy block, limit exceeded.** Each is a banner on the line it concerns with the rule's name and the key to ask a manager; none opens a modal that steals the scan field except the approval prompt.
- **Error: the save fails** (disk full, a write refused). The bill is not handed over: "Could not save: do not give the goods. Retry (Enter) or Hold (F7)". The cart is intact.
- **Print fails or the printer is off.** The bill is saved first; a lane message "Receipt not printed: Retry (Enter), Reprint later (F11)"; the drawer still opens on a cash tender only if the pulse went to a printer that answered (otherwise a manager key opens it with an event).
- **Power cut.** The cart returns from `posDrafts` (relaxed durability, so the last second of cart edits may be missing: the screen shows the restored time); a saved bill is strict-durable (PD39) and, if the Electron journal lists a printed number that IndexedDB lacks, start-up shows "Recovered bills: T1/000123 was printed but is missing" and the manager re-enters it from the customer's copy or posts the adjustment of 2.4 case 18.
- **Return (F10).** The return screen finds the original by scanning the bill number, then the rules of 2.6 apply: returnable quantity only, the refund to the original tender, a cap and a manager above it, the cut-off date as data. Offline it works only on bills this terminal made itself (a bill made on another counter needs the cloud, PD41); a bill not found says "not on this machine yet", never "no such bill".

#### Flow B: a restaurant table to the kitchen to the bill (restaurant-table; v1 single counter device)

| # | Screen | Action | The system does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Table map** (floors, tables coloured by derived status: free, running, bill printed, paid-pending) | tap or type a table name | Opens an order (`posOrders` row: outlet, channel dine-in, table, guests, captain); no voucher number |
| 2 | **Order** (menu grid, search by name, short code or variant name; modifiers in a side panel) | add items; variant, option groups (minimum and maximum picks enforced), notes | A line's identity is item + variant + chosen options + course + station + note; option price deltas fold into the line's price (PD30); mixed-tax combos are refused in v1 |
| 3 | **Send** | F2 (or the Send button) | One immutable kitchen-ticket event (`kind: add`) per station is written and printed to that station's printer from the counter PC; lines turn "sent" and are struck-through, never removed, if cancelled later |
| 4 | **Change** | add more, edit a sent line, cancel a sent line, move or merge a table | A new event per change (`add`, `edit`, `cancel`, `transfer`) that prints with a banner; a cancel after the grace window needs a reason and an approver and, if cooked, is tagged comp or wastage; the check never edits a ticket |
| 5 | **Bill** | F2 "Bill" | A **provisional bill** (no tax-invoice number; "Bill (not yet paid)", the order or table number, totals, charges that are on) prints; the table turns "bill printed" |
| 6 | **Settle** | F3 to F5 tenders; split by item or equally; add charge lines; tip | Each paid bill is its own tax invoice: the number is taken **at settlement**; discount, charges and round-off apportioned in paise across split bills; an abandoned check burns no number |
| 7 | **Close the table** | automatic after the last settlement | Status free; the order folds into its settlements; KOT-versus-bill reconciliation row written (every ticket line billed, voided with a reason, or flagged) |

Restaurant states: network down (single device keeps working; a second tablet "only while online" in v1 and the screen says so); printer off (the ticket is queued, a visible "kitchen not notified" banner per station, never a silent drop); a ticket line cancelled after the kitchen cooked it (comp or wastage tag, approver recorded, never the beneficiary); an aggregator order (Flow D in 4.5).

#### Flow C: a pharmacy bill, FEFO, with loose tablets (pharmacy)

| # | Screen | Action | The system does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Counter**, pharmacist on duty named in the header (chosen at shift open) | type "para 500" or scan a strip | One search over brand, salt, alias, barcode and rack; each row shows maker, pack, MRP, stock and, for a batch item, the earliest sellable batch and its expiry |
| 2 | **Batch** | Enter | When one batch covers the need and is neither short-dated nor in a warning tier, the FEFO proposal is taken **without a stop** and the line's chip shows batch, expiry, MRP; otherwise a one-key panel lists the live batches (expired ones cannot be picked) |
| 3 | **Quantity speaks the pack** | `2` (strips) or `5t` (tablets) | A compound unit box > strip > unit; the unit rate is the strip price over the units per strip, never above the pro-rata MRP; the stock report shows whole strips plus loose per batch |
| 4 | **Scheduled line** | add an H or H1 item | The gate appears **only now** (an OTC bill has no extra step): prescriber, patient, prescription reference, times allowed, the pharmacist on duty (already known); the substitute panel is replaced by "No substitution on this prescription"; the register serial is assigned at commit |
| 5 | **Loose sale** | a loose quantity | Needs the pharmacist on duty; a loose-supply label (drug, quantity, dealer name and address, plus batch, expiry and unit MRP as the shop's own additions) is queued with the bill |
| 6 | **Tender and commit** | as retail | The bill prints the dealer's drug licence number (only if the owner entered it), a serial, batch and expiry per line, manufacturer on a scheduled line; the register line is a report over the voucher |
| 7 | **Short-list** | one key on an unavailable medicine | The item and the customer's phone (with consent) go to a short-list that feeds reorder |

### 1.5 The counter screen

**One root, two input modes, one state.** `PosRoot` is a full-window layout of three zones plus a header and a tender bar, tuned for 1280 x 800, 1024 x 768 and a 1280 x 800 tablet; below 900 px wide it stacks (cart above, scan and tiles below) for a phone. Every target a finger touches is at least 44 px; light and dark, compact and comfortable density, 150% text size are in the recipe matrix (10.6). It takes the app's tokens (`styles/pos.css`, no new colour outside the accent family), not a second visual system.

```
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| T1 | Anita (cashier) | Shift open 09:10 | Bills 143 | Sat 25-Oct-2026 14:02 | online 0 |
+------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------+
| CART                                      | [ scan or search ______________ ]     |
|  1 Biscuits 100g   3 x 30.00   90.00      |  customer: Walk-in  [F6]              |
|  2 Rice 5kg        1 x 310.00  310.00     |  results / tiles (favourites,         |
|  3 T-shirt         1 x 1,299.00 ...       |  categories, recent today)            |
|                                           |  [Biscuits] [Rice 5kg] [Milk] [Tea]   |
|                                           |  [ 1 ][ 2 ][ 3 ]  (touch numpad)      |
| Items 3 | Discount 84.95 | Tax 85.82      |                                       |
| Round-off -0.05          PAYABLE 1,614.00 |                                       |
+------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------+
| F2 Cash  F3 UPI  F4 Card  F5 Split  F6 Customer  F7 Hold  F8 Recall  F9 Discount  |
| F10 Return  F11 More  F12 Shift                                                  |
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

**Cart.** One row per line: name with the chip (batch, expiry, MRP, weight), quantity, unit price, line discount badge, line total. The selected line is changed with Up and Down; **bare digits never open a quantity edit**: they always go to the scan field and resolve as a code first (a 3-digit PLU is a PLU and a 13-digit EAN is an EAN; RV24). A line is selected by Up and Down or by **tapping it**, and a touch stepper appears on the row (minus, plus, remove, discount; each target 44 px); **`*` pressed on an empty scan field opens the selected line's quantity edit** (Enter sets it; eight or more bare digits there are refused as "a barcode, not a quantity"; a quantity of 100 units or more, or a line of Rs 50,000 or more, asks "Is this right?" with Enter to confirm and Esc to revert, the thresholds being owner data); `+` and `-` on the numpad change by one; Delete removes the line (a toast with an Undo key; after the tender dialog has been opened a removal needs the right). **The selection clears on any scan, on Enter, when the tender dialog opens and after 2 seconds with no key**, so a scan after a glance up the cart can never land in a quantity box. The recipes `pos-select-then-scan`, `pos-select-then-plu` and `pos-touch-only` (add, edit, remove and tender with no keyboard, RV37) hold it. A line shows why its price is what it is (price level, scheme, override, open item) in one word under the name. The total panel always shows items, discount, tax by head, round-off and the payable amount in the largest type on the screen.

**Search versus grid.** The scan field is the default focus and the only text field on the counter screen. Typed text filters in this order, stopping when a stage yields results: exact code (registry, alias, part number, short code); name tokens by prefix; substring; each stage ranked by items sold on this terminal today, then recency, then name; at most 50 rows, rendered without per-key work on the book (the filter is debounced 40 ms so a scanner burst updates once). With the field empty the right zone shows **tiles**: favourites (a per-terminal list), category chips, "sold recently here". Tiles carry a name and an optional colour or small image (stored in the item, 20 KB at most); a tile tap adds one unit. On a touch device the field has `inputMode="none"` until the person taps the keyboard icon, so the on-screen keyboard does not cover the cart each time focus returns.

**Keys (screen-scoped, registered in `core/shortcutRegistry.ts` in a new block, rebindable from Settings except the reserved set, shown by `keyLabel`).** Only F-keys, Alt chords and the keys above; **no bare-letter hotkey exists on the counter screen**, so a scanner burst can never fire one [AU 7; TS 14.3]. Esc is layered: close the open dialog, then clear the scan field, then ask "Clear this bill?" (No armed). Go To (`Ctrl+K`), the `?` sheet and Settings (`Ctrl+,`) keep working. Typing `3*` in the scan field sets the next item's quantity (a multiplier prefix, the one printable character the field interprets; it is read only as 1 to 4 digits, an optional decimal part and then `*`, so a Code 39 start character at the front of a scan is never taken for a multiplier).

**Customer panel (F6).** Find by card code or by phone (exact or last 4 digits). Not found: a short form (phone, optional name, two separate consent ticks: "send me this bill", "send me offers", both off unless the shopper says yes, each with the purpose in plain words); saving it writes a `customers` row and `consent` events in `custEvents` (who, when, on which terminal, for what, with the notice words shown). With no consent the phone is used for the one WhatsApp link and not stored. The panel shows the customer's last bill (for "Repeat"), credit due and limit, points (P-3c) and notes.

**Payment dialog (F2 to F5).** Large due amount; a tender list that adds up to it. **Cash**: tendered amount with quick buttons (exact, then the next 50, 100, 500, 2,000), change in large type. **UPI**: by default the customer scans the shop's own QR and the cashier attests (an amount QR for micro-merchants is an option; the owner's own VPA link, with `tr` and merchant fields only when configured, PD24); the dialog shows the **attested amount** (pre-filled with the due amount, editable) and an optional UTR tail before Enter records it as `unconfirmed`, source `cashier` (Flow A2); with a PSP later the state flips to `confirmed` by itself. **Card**: the approval code and the last four digits typed from the machine's slip; any 13 to 19 digit run that passes the Luhn check is refused in the typed reference, narration and note fields (never in a scan target or a serial or IMEI field, 6.7), with the reason in the message. **Credit** (a customer is required): the credit check runs (the same engine as the voucher screen), an override needs the manager. **Gift voucher or store credit**: a code scan or entry shows the balance (an online check or, offline, a redemption capped per terminal per day and re-checked at sync, PD41). **Split**: repeat tenders until the balance is zero; every non-cash tender is capped at the balance; only cash gives change. Enter on a complete dialog commits; Esc returns to the cart with every tender kept.

**Customer-facing display.** A second window on the second display (Electron only, P-5): the item just scanned large, the running list, the payable amount, the UPI QR when UPI is chosen, and "Thank you" with the bill number after commit. It shows no customer data other than what the customer typed or scanned themselves.

### 1.6 How it fits the app, and why a company that does not enable it sees nothing new

| Concern | Decision |
|---|---|
| **Root** | `App.tsx` renders `PosRoot` instead of `AppShell` when the device is a POS terminal (`TW_DEVICE.pos = { terminalCode }`: the terminal's CODE, which survives a Period roll, resolved to the `posTerminals` row of the active company; a per-device key beside the tracking spec's Devices tab, cleared by `?uireset=1`). From the shell, **Open POS** (menu row, Go To, Alt chord from the registry) pushes a screen `pos` that mounts the same component as a fixed full-window layer; Esc at the root returns to the shell. A cart is typed work, so `PosRoot` registers a `state/dirty.ts` probe (non-empty cart, open dialog, open shift with unsent bills) and every dialog takes `onClose` (Back rules B5 and B12 [CF back-nav-spec]) |
| **State** | A separate store `state/posStore.ts` (cart, terminal, shift, dialogs, device status). The main store's `reloadActive` (a full company reload per pulled page) must not clear a cart: the cart lives in the POS store and in `posDrafts` |
| **Kiosk** | Electron flags owned by the main process, set by a launch argument or `userData/terminal.json`, never sent by the renderer: kiosk or fullscreen, `requestSingleInstanceLock()` (two windows on one machine each hold their own copy of the book and the stock rules are guaranteed per window [TS 10.1]), start at login, `powerSaveBlocker`, devtools off in production. Closing the window with an open shift asks first; the existing backup-on-close stays. Kiosk is ergonomics, not a security boundary |
| **Menu and Go To** | New rows (Open POS, POS reports) are added to `navTree.ts` with their `featureOf` gate, and to the literal row table in `scripts/assert/sidenav.assert.ts`; Go To commands are **late** commands (they rank behind every ordinary match) and hidden unless `features.pos`; the golden Go To capture of 103 queries must not move [C CLAUDE.md Phase 27] |
| **Quick create** | Rows "POS terminal", "Promotion", "Customer" in `ui/quickCreate.ts`, gated by the feature and by the operator's rights; nowhere else |
| **Settings** | A "POS" tab from the registry `ui/settings/tabs.ts` (company policy in the Workspace part, administrator only, written only by `writeCompanyPos`; terminal profile, printers and scanner per device, in the tracking spec's Devices tab); the tab is absent without the feature |
| **Company switch** | `CompanyFeatures.pos?: boolean` (F11: "Point of sale counters"). **F11 rebuilds the whole `features` object from its nine keys on accept** [C `CompanyCreate.tsx:356-360`], so `pos` joins that literal in the same hunk as the tracking lane's `tracking` and `trackingOffline` (one writer). Configuration lives in `Company.pos`, never in `features` |
| **Rights** | `SCREEN_AREA` rows (`pos: 'pos'`, `posReports: 'reports'`, `posShift: 'pos'`, `posSetup: 'masters'`), `TABLE_AREA` rows for every new table, the `Role.pos` block (9.2). `screenDenial` and `voucherDenial` get one added rule for a voucher carrying `pos` (9.2) |
| **Parity board** | One `beyond` row `b-pos` (and later `b-pos-pharmacy`, `b-pos-restaurant`), marked "built, not deployed" until a deploy; the parity count (250 / 58 / 73) does not move, and the rows `report-export`, `credit-limit` and the like are not touched [C CLAUDE.md "Phases 18-21"] |
| **Identity when POS is off (asserted, not hoped)** | For a company without `features.pos`: `Company` has no `pos` field; the five tables are empty and absent from its backup; backup schema number unchanged; the visible menu rows, quick-create list and Settings tabs are the same arrays by deep equality; the Go To golden is unchanged; no POS voucher type exists, so the Sales screen's type picker is the same list; the F11 object has no `pos` key; the key dispatcher hands every handler the same event object; the `Voucher` literal of the Sales save is unchanged byte for byte (`voucher-resave` with `pos` removed); `mcp` drift is green. **For the shared paths lane A changes (pull apply, the duplicate check, the outbox reads, a GSTR-1 month, the backup stream), the same company without POS is held byte-equal to HEAD by goldens (10.5 `pos-global-golden`): GSTR-1 and Table 13 JSON on the sample and a 50,000-voucher book, the Audit Log and Cancelled register row sets with a "load more" path, a backup the HEAD restore reads, and the existing `syncconflict` and pull suites unchanged (RV9).** A suite named `pos-identity` holds all of this (10.5) |

---

## 2. Accounting design

### 2.1 Voucher per bill or summary posting: the decision

**Decision PD3: voucher per bill.** The audit's comparison [AU 3.4] stands, and I add what decides it for the build:

| Question | Voucher per bill | Summary posting (a day voucher per terminal plus detail documents) |
|---|---|---|
| Does every existing engine, report, saved view, dashboard and statutory return work unchanged? | Yes | Yes for the books, **no for every statement about a bill**: Table 13 would show one document for 300, the unit chain cannot be summarised, cancel and returns need a detail table and a reopen path that do not exist [AU 3.4] |
| What does the law require? | Every bill of Rs 200 or more, every bill to a registered buyer and every bill a buyer asks for needs its own number from a consecutive series (Rule 46(b)); only a supply under Rs 200 to an unregistered buyer who does not ask may be covered by one consolidated tax invoice at the close of each day (s.31(3)(b) and the Rule 46 proviso) [AU 3.4; PY 3.1; read in the Act copy of 1 Aug 2021 and a reproduction of the Rule, the amended text unread] | Fits only that slice |
| Where is the cost? | An O(N) cost per bill and per keystroke in the voucher screen's save path, which the POS root does not use; the POS save is the lane A hunk (below) | A close that can fail, books that lag the counter, a detail store with its own index and a new Table 13 source [AU 3.4] |
| What would change the position? | | A customer at 3,000 or more bills a day per company with no wish to shard by terminal; a statutory reading that lets all B2C bills of a day be consolidated; or rebuilding the books engines on incremental indexes, which is lane A taken to its end [AU 3.5] |

**The wall and the way around it.** One terminal makes 109,500, 292,000 or 1,095,000 bills a year at 300, 800 or 3,000 a day; an Accept in today's voucher path is about 0.5 s at 250,000 bills and about 2.3 s at 500,000 on a busy M4 and 3 to 5 times slower on a low-end PC [AU 3.1, 3.2, 0.5]. So: **(a) the POS root never calls `saveVoucher`**; it calls `commitPosBill`, a new store method (lane A, POS-only) that inserts the new vouchers into the in-memory array by a binary insert on `(date, number, id)` (the order a reload gives, PD43), writes the documents, their outbox rows, the events and the series high-water mark in **one strict-durability Dexie transaction** (PD39, 2.13), and updates a running index (item and cell quantities, ledger balances, open bills) that is fed from one Dexie-level choke point and checked against the engines (PD43, 2.14); **(b)** the per-type number is an O(1) counter (`posSeries`), never `nextVchNo`'s scan, and takes the maximum of the counter, one scan per window on first use, the carried and burned numbers and the server's last answer (2.5); **(c)** a **supported envelope and a Period roll** (PD38, 2.15): a ceiling per active company, a one-sided Split Company Data at a month start, and a GSTIN sold only inside the envelope; **(d)** the global lane A items (P-0g1 to P-0g4 in 10.1), each proven equal to HEAD for a company without POS. The exit gate is flat bill-save and scan-to-line time within noise at 250,000 and 500,000 synthetic POS bills **and, at the ceiling C, renderer heap on a 4 GB test PC, cold start (open company to first scan) on the low-end PC, and the existing reports and the default dashboard** (10.5); a figure above 500,000 is not claimed because `stockPositions` and `buildGstr1` grow faster than the book and nobody has found why [AU 3.2].

### 2.2 Money, and which engines the writer calls
