**Integer paise inside `core/pos`.** Every amount in the cart, pricing, tender and shift engines is an integer number of paise; conversion to the rupee floats the existing `Voucher` uses happens once, at the boundary of `bill.ts`, by `p / 100` (an exact two-decimal float), and the new `Voucher.pos` object stores paise (`*P` fields) so it can never drift. `allocateP(totalP, weights)` is the largest-remainder split of `gst.ts allocate` on integers; **the exact integer split is the oracle, not the engine's float one.** The engine divides floats (`(cents * w) / sum`) and sorts the fractional parts, so a near-tie can order the other way; a seeded run of 1,000,000 random cases (2 to 7 weights up to Rs 5,000, totals up to Rs 4,000) against an exact BigInt largest remainder with ties by index gave 4 cases that differ by one paisa in two parts, e.g. total 117,621 over weights 402,493, 412,092, 74,624, 218,175, 16,728 and 166,114: engine 36,692 and 19,890, exact 36,693 and 19,889 [M, reproduced in revision 2, Appendix B.6; a reviewer's run found the same four]. So `allocateP` is exact and is **the authority for a POS bill**; the suite `pos-money` asserts that `allocateP` equals the BigInt oracle always, that it and `gst.ts allocate` agree on the sum and never differ by more than one paisa in any part, and that they agree except at flagged near-ties; the Sales-screen golden (10.5) states that tolerance explicitly (RV15).

**What the writer calls and never copies.** Tax: `computeItemGst` (`gst.ts:93-108`), through one pseudo-line per rate bucket, so CGST and SGST are `round2(base x rate / 200)` each on the bucket's taxable sum, exactly as the Sales screen computes them; the per-line snapshot: `snapshotOf` (`gstRates.ts:246`) for rate, taxability, HSN and cess on the voucher's date; intra or inter: `isIntraState`; the tax ledger by head and rate and the Round Off ledger by name, found as CF's `bulkPlan.ts` finds them (the writer sits beside it and shares what it can [CF `bulkPlan.ts:189-221`, `:296-302`]); the credit check: `creditCheck` (`creditLimit.ts:96-101`); number formatting: `formatVchNo`; cancel: `voucherCancel.ts`. **A second GST engine is a defect** (a rule of the assertion plan, 10.5: a source scan fails the build if `core/pos` contains a literal tax rate, a `* rate / 200` or a `round2` over tax).

### 2.3 From a cart to line values: the algorithm

Inputs per line: `qty` (decimals by unit, up to 4), `unitP` (the price per unit in paise, from the item's price level, the batch's price or the typed price for an open item), `mode` (`incl` or `excl`), `lineDisc` (percent or paise), the tax snapshot (`rate`, `cess`, `taxability`) after the **rate rule** (below). Bill inputs: a bill discount (percent or paise), the round-off policy, the intra or inter result.

1. **Gross.** `G_i = round(qty_i x unitP_i)` in paise, half up.
2. **Line discount.** `G'_i = G_i - d_i` (`d_i` = round(G_i x pct) or the typed paise; never above G_i; a line may reach 0 only through the gift path with a reason, 3.4).
3. **Bill discount.** `D` is allocated over the eligible lines with weights `G'_i`: `D_i = allocateP(D, G')`; `G''_i = G'_i - D_i`. Eligible = every line not marked "no discount" (an item flag; fixed-price and MRP-protected items may still take a discount below MRP, never above). Loyalty and coupon value enter here as further sources of `D` (the sum is allocated once, so rounding happens once), each recorded in `pos.discount.parts`.
4. **The rate rule (ships OFF).** For an item with `rateRule = { kind: 'unitPrice', basis, tiers: [{ upTo?: paise, rate }] }` (apparel and footwear from 22 Sep 2025: 5% up to Rs 2,500 a piece, 18% above [RT 6 row 7, PIB release read by the sibling]), the rate is chosen **after** step 3 from the net unit price `G''_i / qty_i`. **The rule is OFF until the company records its basis, and the basis is the one thing a CA must sign (11.2):** `net-exclusive-at-lowest-tier`, `net-inclusive`, or `net-exclusive-at-each-tier-rate`; with no basis recorded, a line of a rule item is **refused** ("rate rule: ask your CA, basis not set"). **The default I first wrote does not close for a tax-inclusive tag between Rs 2,626 and Rs 2,950** [M, Appendix B.7]: at 5% the exclusive value is above Rs 2,500 (2,799 / 1.05 = 2,665.71), so the line goes to 18%, where its exclusive value is 2,372.03 and would qualify for 5%. **For any rule item whose inclusive price falls in that band the line is refused with the three results shown** (5%: GST 133 on 2,666; 18%: GST 427 on 2,372; and the basis in force) until the recorded basis resolves it; the band edges are computed from the tiers and rates in force, never typed. The rule's tiers carry `effectiveFrom`, so a back-dated bill takes the old rate; a bill in which a line's net price crosses a tier because of a discount **shows the rate and the reason on the line**. **A return never re-runs the rule: the credit note copies the original line's rate (2.6).** A grep of `src/core` finds no such rule today [AU 1.5, Appendix B] (RV16, RV26).
5. **Tax split per rate bucket.** Group lines by (rate, cess). For a bucket:
   - **Exclusive** (`mode = excl`): `A_i = G''_i`; the bucket base is `B = sum A_i`; CGST and SGST (or IGST) and cess come from `computeItemGst` on `B`.
   - **Inclusive** (`mode = incl`, the MRP case): `T = sum G''_i` is what the customer pays for the bucket. Find the **largest integer `B` (paise) with `B + cgst(B) + sgst(B) + cess(B) <= T`**, where the three taxes are the engine's for a base of `B` paise (start at `floor(T x 100 / (100 + rate + cessRate))`, step up while the next base still fits, then step down until it fits; at most six steps in either direction). The leftover `T - (B + taxes)` is 0, 1 or 2 paise without cess and **0 to 3 paise with an ad valorem cess** (28% with 22% cess: 2,344 of 200,000 totals leave 3; 28% with 60%: 10,639) [M, Appendix B.1 and B.5]; the search never needed more than 2 steps up or 1 down over every case run [M], so six is a safe bound. Then `A_i = allocateP(B, G''_i over the bucket)`, so the lines sum to `B` exactly, and `rate_i = A_i / qty_i` is stored to **4 decimals** (a display value; `amount` is what is booked and what every engine reads). Inclusive and exclusive lines of one rate are split as two sub-buckets; the posted tax is their sum, and the engine's per-rate regrouping on read tolerates the paisa this can leave (its tolerance is about Re 1 per bucket).
6. **Totals and the party leg.** `sumA` = the sales credit; the tax legs are the bucket sums; `raw = sumA + sum of tax`; the customer's total before rounding is `Traw = sum of T_bucket` for inclusive buckets plus `sumA + tax` for exclusive ones. The **party leg `P`** is `Traw` rounded by the policy (`none`, Re 1, 50 paise, 10 paise or 5 paise; `nearest` or `down`; PD7 forces `down` or none when the bill has an item with an MRP and the guard on). **The Round Off leg is `P - raw`** (a debit when negative, as the voucher screen's round-down bills are): it carries the rounding **and** the 0 to 2 paise per inclusive bucket, so the voucher always balances to the paisa and `voucherGstSummary` reads taxable and tax exactly as posted (the Round Off ledger is excluded from taxable value by name, `gst.ts:176`).
7. **Guards before commit** (each a named, tested rule): MRP guard (the tax-inclusive net unit price may not exceed the line's MRP; `warn` or `block` per item, an override needs the approval right and a reason; the tracking engine's `mrpCeiling` is the arithmetic [C `batches.ts:175`]); price below zero or missing; quantity at or above eight bare digits; stock policy; credit limit; buyer block at Rs 50,000; e-invoice gate; expired or held batch (the tracking rules R11, R13); cash cap: **a single cash tender at or above the cap needs a manager and a reason for any customer, known or not**, and a warning at 75% of the cap for a known customer's day total (the section's three limbs are one person in a day, one transaction, one event or occasion, as commentary reads them [PY 1.6; not the Act text]; whether a PAN must also be quoted on a large cash sale is [unverified], a CA question; the figure and the Income-tax Act 2025 section number are dated data) (RV27).

**Worked vector [M].** Biscuits (MRP 30.00, 18%) x3 = 90.00, Rice 5 kg (MRP 310.00, 5%) = 310.00, T-shirt (tag 1,299.00, 5%, under the slab) = 1,299.00; gross 1,699.00; a 5% bill discount = 84.95, allocated 4.50 / 15.50 / 64.95, net 85.50 / 294.50 / 1,234.05. Bucket 5%: inclusive 1,528.55, base 1,455.77, CGST 36.39, SGST 36.39, leftover 0 (line amounts 280.48 and 1,175.29). Bucket 18%: inclusive 85.50, base 72.46, CGST 6.52, SGST 6.52, leftover 0. Sum: base 1,528.23, tax 85.82, total 1,614.05; party leg rounded down 1,614.00; Round Off 0.05 (debit). The voucher: Dr party 1,614.00, Dr Round Off 0.05, Cr Sales 1,528.23, Cr CGST 42.91, Cr SGST 42.91. Appendix B repeats it with the S2 and return variants.

### 2.4 The exact posting, case by case

Ledgers named below are created by the Add terminal wizard (7.1) through the ordinary master path, one undo step, with the reserved groups; none is hard-coded by name except Round Off (found by name, as today).

| # | Case | Vouchers | Entries | GST effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Cash, one tender, unregistered buyer** (S1) | one Sales voucher, type "POS Sales T1" | Dr **Cash - T1** P; Cr Sales (the terminal's sales ledger, or one per stock group, see the paragraph after this table); Cr CGST, Cr SGST (or IGST, cess) per bucket; Dr or Cr Round Off. Tender detail in `pos.tenders` | B2CS row by place of supply (the company state), intra, rate; HSN B2C half; Table 13 row of this terminal's type |
| 2 | **UPI or card, one tender** (S1) | one Sales voucher | as 1 with the party **UPI Collections - outlet** (or Card Collections), a Bank Accounts ledger; the tender's `state` (`confirmed`, `unconfirmed`) and `ref` live in `pos.tenders`. **A PSP wallet** is posted the same way to a per-outlet `Wallet Collections` ledger. **Every Collections ledger is cleared by a settlement voucher (case 16), never left to grow** | as 1 |
| 3 | **Mixed tender** (S2) | one Sales voucher + **one Receipt voucher** ("POS Receipt T1", one Dr line per tender) | Sales: Dr **Walk-in customers - outlet** P, Cr sales and tax as 1. Receipt: Dr Cash - T1 c, Dr UPI Collections u, Cr Walk-in customers P (`c + u = P`); each Dr line carries `instrumentType` and `instrument` (the UTR or approval code) | unchanged (receipts are not GST documents [C `gstr1.ts:359-370`, `:385-387`]); the clearing debtor nets to zero after every bill (asserted) |
| 4 | **B2B bill** (a GSTIN given) | Sales to the customer's ledger; Receipt(s) if paid at the counter | Dr customer P; Cr sales and tax; place of supply = the customer's state (or the delivery state typed); bill-wise New Ref named after the bill number if the ledger is bill-wise; Receipt Cr customer with Agst Ref | B2B table 4A; HSN B2B half. **If the company is e-invoice-liable the bill is not completed at the counter, and it is not a dead end:** "Continue as invoice" carries the cart (buyer, lines, discount, place of supply) into an **Optional Sales voucher of the ordinary Sales type** saved through the existing store path, tells the cashier "the invoice will be prepared for the office; no bill number is used", and the back office completes it through the existing e-invoice flow (JSON export, IRN import, QR print), because an invoice without an IRN is not treated as an invoice and this app never generates an IRN [PY 3.5; C CLAUDE.md]. No number of a POS series is spent (RV22) |
| 5 | **Credit sale** (udhaar) | one Sales voucher | Dr customer ledger P (bill-wise New Ref, due date from the customer's credit days); no Receipt | as 4 or B2CS by the customer's registration |
| 6 | **Credit plus part payment** | Sales + Receipt | Receipt Dr tender, Cr customer, Agst Ref = this bill, amount = the part paid; the New Ref's balance is the remainder | as 4 |
| 7 | **Loyalty points or coupon applied** | as the base case | the value is part of the bill discount `D` (step 3), so it reduces taxable value before tax; **it is never a tender** | discount recorded on the invoice (s.15(3)(a)); whether points are a discount or a payment is unsettled [PY 1.7, unverified], a CA decides before P-3c ships |
| 8 | **Gift voucher redeemed** | as 1 or 3 | a tender whose ledger is **Gift vouchers payable** (a Current Liabilities ledger): Dr Gift vouchers payable | the underlying supply is taxed at redemption (Circular 243/37/2024 as read through commentary) [PY 1.7, CA to confirm] |
| 9 | **Gift voucher or store credit issued** | one **Receipt** (not a Sales voucher), `pos.role = 'voucher-issue'` | Dr tender ledger, Cr Gift vouchers payable (or Store credit payable); a `custEvents` row | none at issue (CA to confirm) |
| 10 | **Tip** (restaurant) | a Receipt in the same transaction, `pos.role = 'tip'` | Dr the tender ledger, Cr **Tips payable**; **never a leg of the bill** (an extra Cr leg on a Sales voucher is added to taxable value, `gst.ts:161-177`) | none |
| 11 | **Service charge, packaging, delivery** | lines of the Sales voucher | **service items** (stockless, with SAC and a dated taxability snapshot), not extra ledger legs; the service charge is off by default and carries no GST treatment until the owner has chosen one (a Non-GST line is possible; the engine leaves Non-GST lines out of taxable value) | in the value of supply at the supply's rate (s.15(2)(c)) for packaging and delivery on the restaurant's own bill; the service charge is the contested one [RS 4.4, 4.5, CA] |
| 12 | **Aggregator order** (restaurant, P-7a and P-8) | one Sales voucher **per class of line**, in the aggregator's own voucher type ("POS Aggregator T1", outside the restaurant's invoice series) | **Restaurant-service lines:** Dr Aggregator receivable (value), Cr Sales (value), **no tax legs**; the lines carry the new taxability `ECO95` ("tax paid by the operator under 9(5)"); the restaurant prints no tax invoice for them. **Goods and any other supply on the same order** (a packaged soft drink, a chocolate, manufactured ice cream, which Circular 164 treats as goods) **are the restaurant's own taxable supply at their own rate**: the manual order screen splits the order or asks per line, posts them as an ordinary tax invoice on the terminal's own POS Sales series with the operator's TCS flagged, and item classes default packaged goods and ice cream to goods rates. The payout later clears the receivable bill-wise; commission and its GST are the restaurant's expense | restaurant-service lines are reported where the circular says (**GSTR-1 Table 8 and GSTR-3B 3.1(c), "for the time being"** [W Circular 167, Sl. 11]; the portal's Table 14(b) and 3.1.1(ii) per search summaries stay **unconfirmed**) and count in turnover; the goods lines are in the B2C tables and the restaurant's own HSN summary; **the operator issues the invoice for restaurant service (Sl. 10) while for goods the supplier keeps the invoice duty and the operator collects TCS under section 52 (Sl. 8 and 9)** [W Circular 167]; ice cream from a parlour is goods, not restaurant service [W Circular 164 s.4.3: 18% in 2021; the rate in force after 22 Sep 2025 is dated data, not read here] |
| 13 | **Free line, BOGO, gift** | lines of the same voucher | "buy one get one" is a **bundle priced once**: three units, the amount of two, a per-unit net by allocation, never a zero-rate line; a gift with no consideration is a zero-amount line with a reason `gift`, flagged for the credit-block note | circular 92/11/2019 as read [RT 6 row 5, [W]]: BOGO is two supplies at one price; a gift is not a supply and its credit is blocked |
| 14 | **Composition company** | as 1 to 3 with no tax legs | lines carry no tax, the receipt is titled **Bill of Supply** with the legend "composition taxable person, not eligible to collect tax on supplies" | the existing Bill of Supply path's treatment of a composition company is **read at P-2's start** (not re-derived here) [PY 3.1] |
| 15 | **Mixed taxable and exempt basket** | as 1 to 3 | per-line taxability; the printed title is "Invoice-cum-Bill of Supply" for an unregistered buyer | Rule 46A as the sibling read it [PY 3.1; Rule 46A not read here] |
| 16 | **Settlement of a Collections ledger** (UPI, card, wallet) | two vouchers in one transaction, `pos.role = 'settle-tenders'`: a **Contra** and a **Payment** (the types of the terminal that runs the settlement, 2.5) | Contra: Dr Bank (the net credit on the statement), Cr the Collections ledger. Payment: Dr **Merchant discount** (Indirect Expenses), Dr Input GST on it where the owner's CA says credit is available, Cr the Collections ledger. The gross, discount, GST and net come from the provider's settlement report (typed or imported) and must add up; each tender in the batch gets a `tender` event `settled` | none on the Sales side; the discount's GST is the shop's input; the bank-statement matcher matches the one Dr Bank entry one-to-one against the statement line [C `brsMatch.ts` rule 4] |
| 17 | **A tender that never arrives** (a faked screen, a failed payment) | a Payment: Dr the owner's named loss ledger (or the cashier's receivable ledger), Cr the Collections ledger | approver and reason recorded; a `tender` event `written-off`; the bill itself is untouched and stays a complete, printed bill | none (the sale stands; the loss is the shop's) |
| 18 | **Lost bills** (a terminal died with unsynced bills) | the bills **re-entered** from the customers' or duplicate copies (a "recovered bill": an ordinary POS Sales voucher in the retired terminal's type with its original number and date, a manager's approval, `pos.recovered`), **or** one dated **adjustment**: a Sales voucher in a separate type ("POS Adjustment") with one stockless line per rate (service-style items made on first use) at the manager's reconstructed totals, tendered by the remote close's cash count | the adjustment shows the tax by rate, so B2CS and the tax payable are right; **the declared range stays in the integrity report as "documents issued, records lost"**; Table 13 counts those numbers as cancelled (holes in the series, [C `gstr1.ts docsIssued`]), so the Table 13 screen says "ask your CA before filing" and nothing is invented (11.2) | as the case: B2CS rows by rate |

**One sales ledger per bill is the default, and a reader audit is the price of changing it.** The Sales screen credits one ledger for the whole bill (`itemLedgerId`), so profit and loss shows one sales line [AU 1.1, 1.3]. A retailer wanting "Sales: Grocery 5%" and "Sales: Gifts 18%" needs several Cr legs; `voucherGstSummary` already adds every non-tax non-party leg to taxable value, so GST is safe, but any reader of `itemLedgerId` would see only one. POS vouchers are alteration-locked (PD10), so the writer may post one Cr leg **per stock group's sales ledger** when `Company.pos.salesByGroup` is on (default off); P-2 starts with a grep of every `itemLedgerId` reader and ships the option only if each is safe or fixed.

### 2.5 The stamp, the tender, the types, the series

```ts
// src/types.ts (lane C; every member optional; a Voucher without `pos` is every voucher that exists today)
export type PosMode = 'retail' | 'pharmacy' | 'restaurant-counter' | 'restaurant-table';
export type PosRole = 'sale' | 'settle' | 'return' | 'refund' | 'tip' | 'voucher-issue' | 'float' | 'drop' | 'payin' | 'payout'
                    | 'overshort' | 'settle-tenders' | 'writeoff' | 'adjust' | 'close';
export type TenderKind = 'cash' | 'upi' | 'card' | 'wallet' | 'voucher' | 'credit' | 'store-credit' | 'other';
export type TenderState = 'pending' | 'confirmed' | 'unconfirmed' | 'expired' | 'failed';   // the state AT ACCEPT; every later change is a `tender` event and the current state is their fold (RV13)
export type TenderSource = 'cashier' | 'psp' | 'terminal' | 'statement';
export interface PosTender {
  id: string; kind: TenderKind; ledgerId: string;
  amountP: number;                       // what the ledger received (never the cash handed over)
  tenderedP?: number; changeP?: number;  // cash only
  state: TenderState; source: TenderSource; at: number;
  ref?: string;                          // UTR, RRN or approval code AS the provider or the cashier gave it
  tr?: string;                           // our reference: the draft id, then the bill number
  cardLast4?: string; issuer?: string; approval?: string; rrn?: string; terminalRef?: string; provider?: string;
  voucherCode?: string; mdrP?: number;   // mdrP from a dated rate table, or the provider's report
}
export interface PosApproval { kind: 'discount' | 'override' | 'void' | 'refund' | 'noSale' | 'reprint' | 'creditLimit' | 'mrp' | 'stock' | 'clock'; by: string; reason: string; at: number; amountP?: number; self?: true }
export interface PosLineMeta { listP: number; discP?: number; why?: 'level' | 'batch' | 'scheme' | 'override' | 'open' | 'gift' | 'bundle'; promo?: string; mrpP?: number; opts?: string; sched?: string; eco95?: true; src?: { billId: string; lineKey: string } }
export interface PosStamp {
  v: 1; billId: string;                  // groups the Sales, its Receipt and any tip Receipt
  role: PosRole; mode: PosMode; terminalId: string; shiftId?: string; at: number;   // epoch ms of Accept, device clock
  cashierId: string; salesperson?: string;
  tenders?: PosTender[];                 // on the sale: every tender, whichever voucher carries the Receipt
  lines?: PosLineMeta[];                 // same length and order as `inventory`
  discount?: { totalP: number; parts: { src: 'manual' | 'line' | 'promo' | 'loyalty' | 'coupon'; id?: string; amountP: number }[]; reason?: string };
  approvals?: PosApproval[];
  buyer?: { name?: string; phone?: string; address?: string; state?: string; gstin?: string };   // Rule 46(e), (f); the one place a shopper's data lives on a bill
  customerId?: string;
  originalId?: string;                   // return, refund, credit note: the sale voucher
  settles?: string;                      // a Receipt: the Sales voucher it settles
  tip?: { amountP: number; tender: TenderKind };
  rx?: PosRx;                            // pharmacy, section 5
  order?: { id: string; channel: string; table?: string; token?: number; ext?: { platform: string; id: string } };   // restaurant, section 4
  eco95?: true;                          // restaurant: set when any line is ECO95; the class itself is per line (PosLineMeta.eco95)
  recovered?: { by: string; reason: string; source: 'duplicate' | 'customer-copy' };   // a lost bill re-entered with its original number (2.4 case 18)
}
// Voucher.pos?: PosStamp   InventoryLine gains: sched?, mfr?, freeQty?, costValue? (5, 2.6)   StockItem gains: pos?: { shortCode?, fav?, tile?, priceMode?, open?, weighed?, noDisc?, rateRule?, localName? }, noStock?
```

A reprint count, a drawer open, an approval refused and every other thing that is **not a change to the document** is a `posEvents` row, never a write to the voucher, so a saved bill stays byte-identical for ever and cannot be "edited after print" (PD10, PD16). **That includes a tender's confirmation:** a UPI or card tender that moves from `unconfirmed` to `confirmed` (a statement match, a provider callback), `settled` (case 16) or `written-off` (case 17) is a **`tender` event** `{ billId, tenderId, state, source, ref?, settlementId?, at, by }` keyed to the bill; the current state is the fold of the bill's events through the transition machine of 6.5 (the latest legal event by time, ties by id), so two statement imports on two PCs cannot conflict and an offline terminal's older copy of the bill is never rewritten (RV13).

**Types and series.** The Add terminal wizard creates **five** `VoucherType`s per terminal (Sales, Credit Note, Receipt, Payment, Contra) and, on first use, a sixth and a seventh (an aggregator order for a restaurant, the lost-bills adjustment of case 18), all marked by a new optional `VoucherType.pos?: { terminalId: string; role: 'sale' | 'return' | 'receipt' | 'payment' | 'contra' | 'aggregator' | 'adjust'; docs?: 'issued' | 'not-issued' }`:

| Type | Base | Template (default) | Restart | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| POS Sales T1 | Sales | `{code}/{seq6}` -> `T1/000123` | Yearly | one pure series per terminal, so Table 13 gives a clean row per terminal and the cancelled count is shells plus holes of a pure series [C `gstr1.ts:459-462`; AU 4] |
| POS Return T1 | CreditNote | `CN-{code}/{seq6}` | Yearly | its own series |
| POS Receipt T1 | Receipt | `RC-{code}/{seq6}` | Yearly | not a GST document, but numbered |
| POS Payment T1 | Payment | `PY-{code}/{seq6}` | Yearly | pay-outs, short cash, goodwill refunds, settlement charges and write-offs (2.4, 2.7); without its own type these would take the default Payment type and either ask the cloud on the Accept path or scan the book (395 ms at 500,000 [AU 3.2]) |
| POS Contra T1 | Contra | `CT-{code}/{seq6}` | Yearly | float, drop, settlement credits |
| POS Aggregator T1 (restaurant) and POS Adjustment T1 | Sales | `AG-{code}/{seq6}` and `AJ-{code}/{seq6}` | Yearly | the aggregator type is `docs: 'not-issued'`: an order whose invoice the operator issues is not one of the restaurant's own tax invoices and must not use the printed series or count in its Table 13 unless the CA says so (a flag read by the Table 13 builder, P-7a); the adjustment type holds the one document of 2.4 case 18 |

**The template validator** (`core/pos/numbering.ts`, a pure function the wizard and a Data Check both call): tokens `{code}` (the terminal's 2 to 6 upper-case letters or digits), `{seq}` with a width of 4 to 8, `{fy}` (`26-27`, 5 characters), `{yy}`, `{mm}`; the printed number is checked **at the largest sequence it can reach** (width plus one digit at overflow, and 7 digits for a terminal expecting 3,000 bills a day) against `^[A-Z0-9/-]{1,16}$`; it must start with a letter or a non-zero digit (the e-invoice portal question the siblings left open [PY 3.3]); a **Monthly restart is refused unless the template carries `{mm}`** (without it April and May repeat a number inside one financial year, which Rule 46(b) forbids and nothing warns about today [AU 4]); upper case only (the portal upper-cases a number, so `t1/1` and `T1/1` are one document [PY 3.3]). The 16-character budget [PY 3.3]: `T1/26-27/000123` is 15; `MUM01/T3/000123` is 15 with no financial year in the text; `MUM01/T3/26-27/0001` is 19 and refused. The financial year need not be printed (the series window is the year in the data).

**Writers.** A POS type is written by `commitPosBill` and by nothing else: the Sales and voucher-type pickers hide it; Bulk Entry, Excel and Tally XML import, the REST path and the MCP planner refuse it; `writeDenial` refuses a voucher whose type is a POS type and which carries no `pos` stamp (client), and the server's rule of 7.5 refuses it from a writer without `pos1`. Without this an imported Sales voucher named "POS Sales T1" could take number 400 that the counter hands out again (RV14).

**Where a number comes from, and where a hole can come from.** `posSeries.next(typeId, date)` returns `max(counter, one scan of the type's vouchers in the window on first use, carriedNumber, burnedNumber, the server's last answer) + 1` (the counter alone ignores a split's `numberFloor` and an IRP-held number, which `nextVchNo` folds [C `store.ts:742-757`]); the high-water mark is written **in the bill's own strict transaction** (PD39); `commitPosBill` never calls `stampIssuedLocally`, which would flag every bill provisional on a signed-in device and send each to the single-claim loop [C `vchNumbering.ts:104`]; the number is taken **at commit, after every check**, so an abandoned cart burns nothing. **Claim:** before each push the terminal claims the numbers in `(claimedUpTo, issued]` of each series in one batch of at most 50 (the same function as the mobile spec's `/m/v1/numbers/claim`, PD40); the answer carries the series' next number, which lifts the counter (a claim with an empty batch is the **series check** that the preflight and the Replace terminal wizard use), so a restored backup or a reset PC that is online is lifted before its first bill by the series check of the shift-open preflight (3.12); `taken` for a number written by a different voucher flags the device, stops it issuing and writes a Sync Mismatch Report row, and nothing is renumbered silently. The causes of a gap and the guard of each [AU 4]:

| Cause | Guard in POS mode |
|---|---|
| Undo of a just-saved voucher deletes it (`undo.ts:118-119`) | no Undo toast on a POS bill; "take back" is Cancel |
| Delete (Alt+D) | the Sales screen refuses a voucher with `pos`; a cashier role has no delete action |
| An Optional voucher used as a hold | holds are `posDrafts`, not vouchers |
| A carried date | PD9 |
| A server wait or a claim storm | the writer never asks the cloud (PD8) |
| A terminal lost or reset | the **Replace terminal** wizard (7.1): the new device pulls the history before it bills, asks the server for each series' next number (the issue ledger's maximum plus one) and starts above **both that and the last number the manager says was issued**; a declared range of bills that never synced is **not** filled with cancelled shells (they were real sales: shelling them would remove their tax and Table 13 would call them cancelled, and their cash would surface as "cash over"): it is re-entered from the customers' or duplicate copies, or posted as one dated adjustment (2.4 case 18), and listed in the integrity report as "documents issued, records lost" |
| Two machines sharing one terminal code, a restored backup, a cloned install | the batched claim answers `taken` and the device stops issuing; the series check in the preflight lifts a stale counter when online; `checkDuplicates` (incremental from P-0g1, with no 5,000-voucher cap) and the Series integrity Data Check (duplicates, gaps, a date running backwards within a series) flag the rest [C `sync.ts:678-680`] |

**What PD8 gives up, said plainly.** A cloud-numbered series reserves each number at Accept and records it in the server's issue ledger at once; a device-owned series records it at the next claim, so a number issued and never claimed (a lost terminal) is unknown to the cloud until the manager declares it. The cost is the Replace terminal procedure, the claim and the Data Check. **A claim can only protect numbers it has seen:** two machines sharing a code are caught at the first claim, not at the second bill. Cloud numbering for a POS type is **not offered in v1**; it can be added as a per-terminal setting (`numbering: 'device' | 'cloud'`) without touching the writer, because `needsNumber` is the one place that decides (`vchNumbering.ts:79-82`).

### 2.6 Returns, exchanges and cancellations

**Return (F10).** The original is found by scanning the bill number (printed as a Code 128 and a QR on every receipt; the encoders are merged [C `src/core/barcode/*`]), by typing it, or by customer, phone and date. The screen lists the original's lines with **returnable quantity = sold less returned so far** (a per-voucher lookup by `pos.originalId`, indexed once in the running index), the reason code (a list the owner edits), and the refund tender (default: the original tender; cash refunds above `refundCashMaxP` need the approval right; a confirmed UPI payment is refunded by a typed reference; store credit needs a customer). Rules:

- **Per line, per unit, in paise.** A return of `q` of `Q` units of line `i` returns `T_ret = allocateP(T_i, [q, Q - q])[0]`, where `T_i` is the line's net inclusive value after discounts; the **last return of a line takes the remainder**, so the returns of a line add up to the sale to the paisa. The credit note's tax is recomputed on the returned value by the same bucket algorithm (2.3 step 5) **using each line's own snapshot copied from the original line** (`gstRate`, `cessRate`, `taxability`, `hsn`, found through `pos.originalId` and `PosLineMeta.src`): `computeItemGst` uses a line's own rate when it has one and otherwise the item's record on the voucher's date [C `gst.ts:93-108`], and a credit note's date is the return date, so a rate that changed between sale and return (12% to 5% on 22 Sep 2025) would otherwise be reversed at the new rate while 12% was charged and remitted, and GSTR-1 would net a 5% row against a 12% sale. **The rate rule is not re-run on a return**: the original's rate decides the bucket, so a line that crossed a tier by a discount returns at the rate it was sold at. A partial return can differ from the original's tax share by a paisa, which the Round Off leg absorbs; **a return of a whole bill copies its legs exactly** (no recomputation). The suite `pos-returns` carries a rate change between sale and return, a tier crossed by a discount, and a golden that GSTR-1 rows by rate net to zero for a full return (RV4).
- **The credit note** is a CreditNote voucher in the terminal's return series, `pos.originalId` set, `refNo` = the original's printed number, the original's date printed; its party is the refund tender ledger (S1; for cash, always this terminal's own cash ledger), the customer's ledger (a credit customer: the New Ref reduces by an Agst Ref) or **Store credit payable**.
- **The cut-off.** Section 34(2) ties a credit note's declaration to a date after the financial year of the supply. The audit read the Act as consolidated on 1 Aug 2021, which says September; the payments study reports 30 November from 1 Oct 2022 by the Finance Act 2022, which neither could read at source [AU 1.4; PY 1.7]; I tried to settle it this session and could not (Appendix C). **The date is `Company.pos.policy.creditNoteCutoff`, a dated data row with `SCHEMA?` and its source**, shipped as "30 November following the year" and shown on screen; a return that would land after it is refused with the reason, and the manager may instead record a **goodwill refund** (a Payment to a "Goodwill refunds" expense, no credit note, no tax adjustment).
- **A return window** (days, default 7, owner-edited) warns and, past it, needs the approval right.
- **No bill, no return** in v1, except through the right `returnsWithoutBill`, a reason and **store credit only**, at the item's current selling price; each is an event and an exceptions row.
- **Across terminals (PD41).** Returnable quantity is the fold of every credit note carrying the bill's `originalId`, from every terminal this device has synced. A return of a bill made on **another** terminal is allowed **only online** (or when this device's last sync is under a minute old and no credit note for that bill is pending in any outbox it can see), and the screen says so ("this bill was made on counter T2: connect to return it"). A pull that finds credit notes beyond the sold quantity writes an **Over-returned bill** row (Exceptions, the Z close list) and a Sync Mismatch Report record naming both credit notes; nothing is cancelled silently. A gift voucher or store credit follows the same rule: redemption is an online check, or an offline redemption capped per terminal per day and re-checked at sync, and loyalty burn follows it (3.6) (RV5).
- **The refund tender.** A **cash refund always credits the refunding terminal's own cash ledger**, whichever terminal sold the bill (the money leaves this drawer; crediting the seller's would show cash over on one close and short on the other); only a non-cash refund follows the original tender (RV19).
- **Stock and cost.** The credit note's inward line books at the cost the goods left at, through `InventoryLine.costValue` (slice P-4b: a one-hunk change in the valuation engine's `bookIn` reading, `inValue = line.costValue ?? <today's>`; the writer snapshots it from the original line's `dValue` observed by `stockPositions` run to the sale, once per return, about 0.2 s at 250,000 bills). Without it a return at the sale price inflates average cost on every return [C `inventory.ts:129`; AU 5]. `inventory.ts` and `valuation.ts` are hash-locked for the MCP and one-writer files for the CF and tracking lanes (10.2).
- **The tracking rules apply:** a returned batch goes back to its own batch (R5, R6), a serial unit's last event must be a sale **by the sale's own bill** (the Cash party makes "to this party" vacuous, so the POS adds `originalId` as the proof) [AU 5; TS 5, 7.3].

**Exchange.** One transaction writes the Credit Note and the new Sales voucher; the net is one tender or a refund; if both vouchers' lines are the same item in another size the exchange screen pairs them, and the receipt prints both numbers.

**Cancel (the lawful void of the whole bill).** Alt+X's machinery (`voucherCancel.ts`) extended by `cancelPosBill(billId)`: **one transaction shells the Sales voucher and its Receipt(s) together** (a shell keeps its number and date, posts nothing, puts stock back [C `voucherCancel.ts:180-214`]), writes one event, and needs the `void` right (a cashier needs a manager) and a reason of at least three letters. **Allowed only while the bill's shift is open;** after the close a bill is reversed by a credit note (Petpooja's own glossary draws the same line [RS 3.4]). A confirmed UPI or card tender must first be refunded and the reference typed. A cancel is an alter in the write guard today (`access.ts:359-360`), so the POS cancel is its own right and never "alter any bill".

### 2.7 Shift, float, drawer, over and short, the Z report

```ts
export interface PosShift {
  id: string; cid: string; terminalId: string; status: 'open' | 'closing' | 'closed';
  openedAt: number; openedBy: string; floatP: number; floatVoucherId?: string;
  pharmacist?: { id: string; name: string; regNo?: string };
  closedAt?: number; closedBy?: string; approvedBy?: string;
  countedCashP?: number; denominations?: Record<string, number>;    // denomination (rupees) -> count
  expectedCashP?: number; diffP?: number; diffVoucherId?: string; dropVoucherId?: string;
  tenderTotalsP?: Record<TenderKind, number>;                       // the snapshot the Z report prints
  firstNo?: string; lastNo?: string; billCount?: number;
  note?: string;
}
```

1. **Open.** The manager (or the cashier, if the policy allows) enters the float; one Contra voucher (`pos.role = 'float'`) debits **Cash - T1** and credits the safe ledger (Cash in hand), so the drawer ledger is **zero before the float and equals the drawer after it**. A terminal with an unclosed shift from an earlier day opens that shift's close first.
2. **During.** Cash sales, cash refunds, **pay-in** (a Receipt, `pos.role = 'payin'`) and **pay-out** (a Payment, `pos.role = 'payout'`) each take a reason from the owner's list mapped to a ledger (a pay-out has a cap and an approval); a **no-sale** drawer open is an event with a reason and the `noSale` right.
3. **Close** (F12). The **blind count**: the screen asks for the count (by denomination, or one figure) **before** it shows anything; then it shows expected = the **oracle's** figure for Cash - T1 (the float plus the movements of the shift's own vouchers, computed by the books engine from the vouchers carrying this `shiftId`, never read from the running index; 3,000 bills is cheap) (RV8), the difference, and, if `|diff|` is within `maxDifferenceP`, closes; above it, the manager approves with a full PIN and a reason (Odoo's maximum-difference rule [RT 2.9]). **Over or short is posted**: a short is a Payment, Dr "Cash short and excess" (an indirect expense), Cr Cash - T1; an over is a Receipt the other way; both `pos.role = 'overshort'`. Then the **drop**: a Contra, Dr safe, Cr Cash - T1 for the counted cash less the float to keep (`closeCarry: 'all-to-safe' | 'keep-float'`), leaving the ledger at the float or zero. Open holds are listed and must be discarded or carried by name; unconfirmed tenders are listed (the bank-statement match clears them later); the day's voids, discounts, returns, reprints and no-sales are shown to the manager **before** the Z report is accepted [RS 3.4].
4. **Remote close.** A shift left open by a crashed device is closed by the manager from another device with the counted figure typed by the manager (`approvedBy`), recorded as an event (bills the dead device never synced are not in the books: the lost-range handling of 2.4 case 18 follows, and the cash they brought is in the drawer count, not an invented "cash over"); the terminal, if it returns, finds the shift closed and refuses new bills until it opens another.
5. **The Z report** (and an **X report**, the same figures without closing and without the drawer count; **for a role without `seeExpected` the X omits expected cash and over or short, and the Z prints them only after the blind count: a cashier cannot print an X, pocket Rs 400 and count to match**, RV43): terminal, shift, cashier(s), first and last bill number of each series and the count (the series' Table 13 row for the window), gross, line discounts, bill discounts by source, net, tax by rate, round-off, takings by tender with the confirmation state of each UPI and card line, refunds and credit notes, cancelled shells (numbers kept), voids, no-sales, reprints, pay-ins and pay-outs, opening float, expected cash, counted cash, over or short, drops. **Its ties (the Phase 28 rule applied to a till, asserted by the suite to the paisa):** the tender totals equal the sum of the bills' tender amounts; the cash tender total equals the movement of Cash - T1 from sales and refunds; the by-rate taxable and tax figures equal the GSTR-1 B2CS rows the same bills would give; the bill count and number range equal the terminal's Table 13 row; every figure equals the report its drill opens.

### 2.8 Customers, credit, bill-wise

- **Walk-in** is the terminal's tender ledger (S1) or the outlet's Walk-in debtor (S2); no walk-in is ever a `customers` row.
- **Credit customers** are party ledgers (Sundry Debtors, bill-wise, credit days, credit limit), created from the customer panel through the existing ledger master; `customers.ledgerId` links the two. The credit check runs at tender with the same engine; **a Ctrl+A-style save that skips the bill panel cannot happen** because the POS writer always writes the New Ref [AU 1.2].
- **Collect dues** (F11): pick the customer, the open bills (`openBillsFor`, one full `outstandingBills` for one party, about 170 ms at 250,000 vouchers [AU 1.6]), the tender, and a Receipt with Agst Ref entries; a reminder is a `wa.me` text that the cashier sends.
- **Quick add by phone** is the `customers` form of 1.5, not the 15-field ledger form (`masters.tsx:354-459`).

### 2.9 GSTR-1, GSTR-3B, HSN and Table 13: why a POS bill reads correctly, and the edits that make it so

| Return part | How a POS bill reaches it | What must be true | Edit needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B2CS** (Table 7), small B2C | one row per place of supply, intra or inter and rate (keyed place of supply, INTRA or INTER, rate) [C `gstr1.ts:546-559`]; on 500,000 synthetic bills the year is 4 rows [AU 0.1, M] | the party is not a registered buyer; place of supply = the company's state for a walk-in (`isIntraState` returns true when either state is blank) [C `gst.ts:18`]; the posted tax equals the rate times taxable within the tolerance | none |
| **B2B** (4A), B2CL | a bill to a ledger with a GSTIN and a customer inter-state | the POS writes `placeOfSupply` explicitly for every bill | none |
| **CDNR, CDNUR, and a credit note netting into B2CS** | `gstr1.ts:573-577`: a credit note to an unregistered party nets into B2CS with sign -1 unless it is against a B2C large invoice, which the engine finds only through a bill-wise Agst Ref [C `gstr1.ts:522-541`, `:736-744`] | a cash or UPI bill has no bill reference | **`originalInvoice` also reads `v.pos?.originalId`** (one hunk in a hash-locked file, with the MCP re-lock, 10.2) |
| **HSN summary** (Table 12, B2B and B2C halves) | per line snapshot (`hsn`, `gstRate`, `cessRate`) [C `gstr1.ts:601-650`] | every line carries an HSN of the digits the company's turnover band needs (4 up to Rs 5 crore, 6 above; a small taxpayer may omit them on B2C bills but the table still wants them) [PY 3.2] | none; a Data Check lists items with no HSN |
| **Documents issued** (Table 13) | `docsIssued` groups by voucher type and numbering window and counts shells and holes of a pure series [C `gstr1.ts:424-477`] | one **pure** type per terminal; Receipts and tip receipts are not GST documents; a cancelled shell counts as cancelled, and the numbers of a lost range are holes that count as cancelled too (2.4 case 18: the Table 13 screen says so) | none |
| **GSTR-3B** 3.1(a), nil and exempt, non-GST | read from the same posted legs and line taxability [C `gstNil.ts`, `gstr3b.ts`] | exempt, nil and Non-GST lines carry the right taxability | the ECO95 class (restaurant) is a builder change in `gstRates.ts`, `gstNil.ts`, `gstr1.ts`, `gstr3b.ts` (P-7a, CA first) |
| **Cess** | `Heads.cess` flows through the posted legs [C Phase 16] | the cess ledger by head | none |
| **e-invoice** | none for B2C; B2B above the threshold is a hand-off to the back office's e-invoice path (case 4) | | none |

**What the golden proves (10.5):** for 10,000 random baskets and tender mixes, GSTR-1 (B2CS, B2B, HSN, Table 13), GSTR-3B 3.1 and the trial balance computed from the POS vouchers equal the same bills entered through the Sales-screen builder (BulkEntry's planner) when the bill is exclusive-priced, and equal an **independent integer oracle** when it is MRP-inclusive (the Sales screen cannot express an inclusive price); every voucher balances; a discount never moves a tax figure outside the lines; an MRP basket prints exactly the sum of its MRPs less discounts, rounded down.

### 2.10 Stock, batch and serial, valuation, the stockless rule

- **Stock out is the voucher's lines.** A Sales voucher's `inventory` lines reduce stock at the item's costing method with no POS-specific code; the terminal's `godownId` is the line's godown; **a retail terminal defaults to the outlet's single godown** (two counters serving one shelf would otherwise sell the same stock from godowns nothing transfers between, RV18); a counter godown is offered only where the stock is physically separate (a restaurant station) and then with an explicit transfer step.
- **Stockless lines.** `StockItem.noStock` (a new tail field, P-0c) makes `stockMovements` skip the item's lines. Charges (packaging, delivery, a service charge, an alteration fee), dishes and the seeded **Open item** use it. **Today the engine never reads `isService`** (only the e-invoice, e-way bill and inward-kind code do), so a dish sold from zero stock would raise the negative-stock prompt on every bill [RS 0.2 item 10; C `types.ts:525`]; the new flag is a separate field so no existing book changes meaning. It is a one-hunk change in a hash-locked engine file (10.2).
- **Open item.** A seeded stockless item "Open item (POS)": the cashier types a description, a price and picks a GST rate from the company's allowed list (default HSN from the company); it needs the `openItem` right and a reason, prints "OPEN ITEM" on the receipt and is an events row, because otherwise it is a way round a price list and a tax rate [RT 4.3].
- **Batch and serial.** The POS **uses** the tracking lane's pure engines and never forks them [AU 0.15]: `pickCellsOf`, `proposeAllocation`, `splitToLines` for the FEFO pick, `mrpCeiling` for the guard, `unitChain` and `unitId` for a scanned IMEI, `scanResolve` for codes; the allocation step is the POS's own component over those functions (the tracking spec builds its panel inside the voucher screen) [PH 6.4 A2]. Until the tracking lane's T-0b has landed, tracked items sell as plain items and the POS says so; **wiring any of it before T-0b forks the stock key** (10.3).
- **Costing.** Per item as today (Avg. Cost by default); `Specific` per unit only where the tracking lane offers it; a return books at issue cost (2.6).
- **Variants** (size and colour) are the custom-fields branch's `(item, variant)` positions (CF-9); a counter sells a variant by a variant barcode through the tracking registry; the POS slice for it is v2 (retail V2.4): CF-9 is merged (`1d22e84`), and the counter's variant barcode needs the tracking lane's registry (T-0b and T-1 to T-4, not built).

### 2.11 Performance: from 300 to 3,000 bills a day, and what v1 certifies

| Item | Today [AU 3] | What this design does | Gate (10.5) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Per keystroke in the item field | 61 ms at 120,000 bills and 160 ms at 250,000 in `nextVchNo`, plus a re-render of the voucher screen | the POS never calls it; the field is uncontrolled; the search index is rebuilt only when masters change | scan to line <= 150 ms and flat from 120,000 to 500,000 bills |
| Accept | about 0.5 s at 250,000 bills (copy, filter, sort; balances and positions rescans) | `commitPosBill` (binary insert, running index, one transaction) | Accept to spool <= 300 ms at 250,000 and flat to 500,000 on the perf harness; a low-end PC is 3 to 5 times slower [AU 3.2] and the gate is also run at that multiple |
| Pull | a full reload per 500-row page; a new terminal replays 502 pages | lane A applies a page without a reload; a new terminal joins by the ordinary pull (PD29) | a pull of 100 pages leaves the cart and the scan field responsive |
| Outbox | never pruned; two screens read all of it (`AuditLog.tsx:101`, `cancelled.tsx:48`) | bounded reads for those two screens (lane A); `posEvents` holds only exceptions (not a row per bill); `posDrafts` is local and unsynced | Audit Log and Cancelled register open in under 1 s at 1,000,000 outbox rows |
| Backup | one pretty-printed JSON string, refused above 512 MiB (`main.cjs:35`), reached at about 292,000 to 370,000 bills | streamed backup (P-0g4) and the envelope of PD38 | a backup is restored on a **scratch machine that never signs in** and its trial balance, stock and GSTR-1 totals equal the live company's as of the backup (never tested today [C HANDOFF]); a restore is never run over a live POS company (PD29) |
| Day Book | one row per voucher, no virtualisation | the POS Z and shift reports read the shift's own vouchers; Day Book windowing is P-9a's, not left to a grid nobody owns | a Z close for 3,000 bills in under 1 s |
| GSTR-1 | a month costs 2.4 s at 500,000 bills | lane A windows the credit-note index and Table 13 | a month is proportional to the month |
| Memory and cold start | the whole company is read into the renderer heap at open: 177 MB at 120,000 bills, 370 MB at 250,000, 722 MB at 500,000 (about 1.45 KB a bill), and the IndexedDB read is unmeasured [AU 3.1; C `PERF.md:325-330`] | the ceiling C and the Period roll (PD38, 2.15) | renderer heap at C on a 4 GB test PC; **cold start (open company to first scan) on the low-end PC**; both measured by spike S-env before P-1 |
| Pull duplicate check | `checkDuplicates` builds a map over every voucher on each pull that brings one, on the main thread, and gives up above 5,000 arriving vouchers [C `syncGuard.ts:311-344`, `sync.ts:678-680`]: 59 ms at 250,000 and 182 ms at 500,000 vouchers, 586 ms at 1,000,000, on this machine at load 8 [M, Appendix B.8] | an incremental map keyed (company, type, window, number string) updated per applied voucher, so a pull checks only its arrivals and the cap goes (P-0g1) | `pos.pull.dupcheck` at 250,000 and 500,000 |
| Registers a CA opens | Day Book, Sales Register and Ledger Vouchers draw one row per voucher with no windowing: a month of one terminal is about 24,000 rows, a year about 292,000 [C `PERF.md:334-337`] | **volume views** (P-9a): day-wise summary rows for a ledger or register above a size with a drill into one day, and row windowing **taken from the report-grid build (`.wt-grid`, not read here) rather than built a second time**; print and export still read the full set | Day Book (one day), Sales Register and Ledger Vouchers (one month), an Excel export, the Balance Sheet, Stock Summary and the default dashboard at C, each measured on the dev machine and on the low-end PC |

One terminal at 3,000 bills a day writes about 1,116 bytes of voucher and 1,238 bytes of change-log row per bill [AU 3.1], so about 3.3 MB and 3.7 MB a day before Receipts (S2 bills add one more voucher, about 700 bytes, for the minority that are mixed or credit [J]); a festival day at five times the average is a bill every two seconds on one terminal, so a **second billing device in the same store** is the queue-breaker (a second terminal with its own series; a handheld is v3) [RT 4.18].

### 2.12 Offline multi-terminal stock races, negative stock, batch conflicts

- **The facts.** Stock is derived from the log and never reserved; two terminals offline each see the last unit and each sell it; two different bills raise **no sync conflict at all** [AU 6]. The tracking design detects a unit sold twice at sync (T-9a) and stops two online devices only with its optional reserve (T-9b); **nothing checks two counters selling the last strip of one batch** [TS 10.1].
- **The policy (PD15).** `Company.pos.stockPolicy`: `allow` (default: sell, never prompt, report), `warn` (a banner on the line, no stop), `block` (only for an item the owner marks strict). A tracked item follows its own rules (strict: a cell below zero blocks unless a person with the Inventory right overrides with a reason; upgraded legacy items warn) [TS 2.3, 5 R10]. A running index per terminal shows "in stock N" under each line from the terminal's own copy.
- **What happens after sync.** The **Oversell report** lists every item or cell that is below zero at the end of a day or after a pull, with the last sales that took it there and their terminals; the manager's action is a Stock Journal receipt (a suggested re-balancing voucher) or a purchase entry that was late. A duplicate serial is the tracking lane's Mismatch Report row. The report is a Data Check kind and a day-close line, not a prompt at the counter.
- **Two windows on one machine** each hold their own copy of the book; the single-instance lock (1.6) prevents it, and `ensureFresh()` at Accept (check the outbox for rows this window did not write and reload first) is the tracking lane's guard that the POS root calls [TS 10.1].
- **Two terminals return the same bill, or redeem the same code.** Detected after sync and prevented where online: 2.6 and PD41.

### 2.13 The saved bill survives a power cut (PD39)

**The gap.** Since Chrome 121 the default durability of a `readwrite` IndexedDB transaction is *relaxed*: the `complete` event fires when the data is in the operating system's buffer, which is flushed about every couple of seconds; *strict* waits for the flush [W developer.chrome.com, IndexedDB durability]. `db.ts` builds Dexie with no options and Dexie 4.4.4 passes `durability: this.chromeTransactionDurability`, which is undefined [C `db.ts:111`; `node_modules/dexie/dist/dexie.js:3032`; a grep of `src` and `electron` finds no durability setting], so every transaction in the app takes the browser default; Electron 31 is Chromium 126 [J: the known mapping; Electron's timelines page names none]. A bill that completed, printed and was handed over can be missing after a power cut a second later, and a series counter rebuilt from stored vouchers then issues its number again. Revision 1's race simulation ("a power cut inside the transaction: none or all") tests atomicity, not this (RV2, RV21).

**The design.**
1. **Strict for the bill, relaxed for everything else.** `commitPosBill`'s transaction asks for `durability: 'strict'` through a **Dexie DBCore middleware** (`src/data/posDurability.ts`, installed by one line in `db.ts` in P-0c): the DBCore `transaction(stores, mode, options)` already receives `{ durability }` [C `dexie.d.ts:565, 714`], so the middleware sets `strict` for a transaction opened inside the commit's scope and leaves every other call untouched. At the public layer Dexie exposes only a database-wide option (`DexieOptions.chromeTransactionDurability` [C `dexie.d.ts:1121`]), and a database-wide strict setting would put an fsync on every cart edit in `posDrafts`. On a device with no POS terminal flag the middleware is not installed (`pos-identity`: the transaction options are unchanged).
2. **The series high-water mark is in the same transaction** (a `meta` row `posSeries:<typeId>:<window>` holding the last number), so a restart can never rebuild a counter below a committed bill's number.
3. **Nothing leaves the building before the commit resolves:** the print job, the drawer pulse, the customer display's "Thank you" and the WhatsApp link are started by the promise the strict commit returns, never in parallel with it.
4. **A belt in the main process (Electron only, slice P-5j).** `receipt.print` appends `{ billId, number, totalP, at }` to `userData/bills.journal` and **fsyncs** it before the spooler write; at start-up the renderer asks main for the journal's tail, and any number IndexedDB lacks is shown as a **recovered bill** (the manager re-enters it from the customer's copy, or posts the adjustment of 2.4 case 18) and raises `posSeries` above it. The journal holds no customer data. It protects against a disk that lied about a flush and against IndexedDB's own loss modes; it is not a second book.
5. **Measure, then gate.** Slice S-dur measures the cost of a strict commit on a hard disk and on eMMC against the 300 ms Accept gate before P-0a is built. If it does not fit, the options are a narrower strict set (the voucher rows and the high-water mark strict, events relaxed), an SSD in the floor of PD23, or letting the journal's fsync be the durability promise with IndexedDB the lazy copy; the choice is made from the numbers, not here.
6. **The test is a real power pull** on the bench kit in the middle of a shift (10.4 item 3): hard-reset a VM, or pull the PC's plug two seconds after a receipt prints. A process kill does not lose OS-buffered data and proves nothing.

### 2.14 The running index: one choke point, an oracle, one order (PD43)

**Feed.** Every write to `vouchers` reaches the index through Dexie `creating`, `updating` and `deleting` hooks on that table, installed once (the repo already hooks the same tables for undo, `core/undo.ts:211-219`, with a wrapper that cannot break a mutation). The hook is O(lines of the voucher), which respects the Phase 24 rule that work inside a Dexie hook must be O(1) per row. A suite proves the hooks fire for `put`, `bulkPut`, `bulkDelete`, a restore, an undo, a pull apply, Bulk Entry, a cancel and a split. A company open, a restore and a split **rebuild** the index from the array; nothing else may write it.

**Check.** At shift open and at shift close the index is compared with the engines (`closingBalances` for the terminal's ledgers, `stockPositions` for the sold items); a difference opens a red preflight item and the screens use the engine's answer for that figure until the index is rebuilt (fail closed). The shift's **expected cash is computed by the oracle** from the shift's own vouchers (2.7), never read from the index, because it becomes a posting (the over or short voucher): a pulled cancel shell or a back-office entry that never reached the index would otherwise post a wrong "cash over" (RV8).

**Order.** Live inserts and pull applies use the comparator `(date, number, id)`. A reload returns rows in id order from the `cid` index and `sortVouchers` is stable, so a reload is also `(date, number, id)`; but `saveVoucher` appends the new voucher last among equal `(date, number)`, and `stockMovements` numbers moves by array position [C `store.ts:313-314, 717-726`; `inventory.ts:45-74`], so two vouchers of different types that share a number on one date (`T1/000123` and `T2/000123`) are ordered by arrival on a live device and by id on a restarted one, and a FIFO or average-cost sequence can differ between two devices. A suite asserts live order equals reloaded order over random arrival orders for a POS company.

**What is still O(N) after lane A, each with an owner and a gate:** `settledBy` on a cancel (`voucherCancel.ts:144-161`; P-4 replaces it for a POS bill with the `pos.settles` link in the index; gate: a cancel under 200 ms at 250,000), ghost recall `findRecall` (P-1: keyed on `pos.customerId` in the index), `exposureOf` for a credit sale (about 76 ms at 250,000 [AU 3.2]; P-2: the index's per-ledger balance), `outstandingBills` for Collect dues (about 170 ms at 250,000, accepted for an interactive call that is not per bill), the pull duplicate check (P-0g1) and `nextVchNo` (never called by the POS).

### 2.15 The supported envelope and the Period roll (PD38)

**State it, then test it.** v1 is sold to a GSTIN whose **active period** holds at most **C vouchers** in the company the terminals load. **C starts at 300,000** [J] and is replaced by the largest figure at which the gates below pass on the 4 GB test PC (the memory spike S-env, before P-1). A bill is one voucher, plus one Receipt for a mixed-tender or part-credit bill (about 15 to 20 per cent of bills [J]), plus the shift vouchers, so C vouchers is about 0.85 C bills. With a roll at most twice a year the sellable rate is **about 1,500 bills a day, all counters together** (Appendix B.9). The same store at the audit's own 800 bills a day per counter and three counters is 2,400 a day and **is outside v1** unless its owner accepts a roll every quarter and the memory gates pass on that customer's own PC: the sheet of 1.3.1 asks.

**What breaks above C, and the gates** (measured at C on the dev machine and on the low-end PC; a figure over 10 s on the low-end PC, or more than 3 times its 120,000-voucher figure on the dev machine, fails [J]): renderer heap on a 4 GB PC (the V8 limit scales with memory [J]; the audit's own reading is that 4 GB "cannot hold it comfortably" at 500,000 bills [AU 3.3]); **cold start, company open to first scan** (the read is unmeasured today [C PERF.md:325-330]); Accept to spool; scan to line; the Balance Sheet, Stock Summary, Trial Balance, the registers of 2.11 and the default dashboard; a month of GSTR-1; Table 13.

**Period roll: a one-sided Split Company Data.** The existing engine (`core/splitCompany.ts`) plans both halves, but its writer copies BOTH into new rows (about a quarter of a million puts for a half that is the whole old book) [C `splitWrite.ts:79-120`], which a POS cannot afford. The Period roll writes **only the later half** (`src/data/periodRoll.ts`, new): the earlier half **is the original company, untouched**, which is exact only while the original holds **no voucher dated on or after the split date**. So the wizard (a) asks for the split date S, a month start (a quarter start when the company files quarterly, which it asks and records), (b) requires every shift closed and no unsent outbox rows, (c) refuses if any voucher of the original is dated S or later ("this wizard cannot move vouchers: choose a later date"), (d) writes the later half in one transaction through restore-style change-log rows with the operator stamped and no user, (e) carries `numberFloor` per type so the Yearly series **continues inside one financial year** with no repeated number [C `voucherNumberFloor.ts`: a split on 1 October carries the running number through the rest of that year], and carries `Company.pos`, the terminals (same codes; the types remade by the split's id walk, the counters continuing), outlets, tenders, policy, customers and the **balances of `custEvents` as opening events**, and (f) leaves each terminal on the old company until **its own shift is closed**, then switches it (a terminal is bound by its **code**, which survives the roll). **Billing is never blocked** by the ceiling: at 80% of C a banner, at C a red preflight item that a manager acknowledges.

**What a mid-year split carries and does not** is the engine's own list: stock layers blend, and TDS, TCS and GST turnover aggregates, payroll year-to-date and leave balances do not carry; the year's profit and loss is split over two companies (the opening carries the position) [C `splitCompany.ts` header]. The annual return and the year-end books then span two companies; the app has no consolidated multi-company report (7.3), so the CA files GSTR-9 from both (11.2). The same wizard at 31 March is the Year roll (7.8).

---

## 3. Features worth copying, with the exact behaviour

The market study [RT 3, 4, 5; RS 3; PH 3] is the evidence for each row; "the leader" is whoever the study read doing it. Everything here is the behaviour **this** product ships, in the order a cashier meets it.

### 3.1 Finding an item: what is matched, in what order, and what is never done

One lookup function, `core/pos/lookup.ts`, over a **derived index** rebuilt only when the item masters, barcode lists or overlays change (never per keystroke, never per bill), in this order, stopping at the first stage that yields a result:

1. **Exact code** through the merged `resolveScan` over `buildBarcodeIndex` [C `scanResolve.ts:130, :373`]: a unit (an IMEI or serial, when the tracking lane has landed), a batch code, an item or pack or variant code, a weighing-scale label under the terminal's scale profile, a GS1 element string, then the item's **alias** and **part number** (so a Tally retailer's barcodes typed into the Alias keep working [RT 4.2]). A code on two items, or an item code with several live batches or MRPs, opens a picker instead of choosing silently; a duplicate across items is a Data Check row.
2. **Short code** (an item's `pos.shortCode`: 1 to 6 characters, unique, typed by the cashier who knows the shelf).
3. **Name tokens by prefix** ("par 5" matches "Paracetamol 500"), each token against the start of any word of the name or alias.
4. **Substring** of the name, alias or brand.

Ranking inside a stage: items sold on this terminal today (a counter built from the day's own bills), then recency, then name. At most 50 rows are drawn; the item's stock on hand (from the running index) and price show on each row. **An unknown code is said, never guessed, never appended anywhere** (the Sales screen's silent failure [AU 1.3]); a cashier with the Masters right gets "Create item with this code" and "Attach to an item", others "Ask the manager". **Customers** are found by exact card code, then phone (exact or the last 4 digits); **bills** (for a return) by number, scan or customer and date. No fuzzy matching is done at the counter: a wrong item that sounds right is worse than a visible miss.

### 3.2 Quantity and unit shortcuts

| Input | Result |
|---|---|
| A second scan of the same item (non-serial) | quantity + 1 on that line (a line flash, no new row); a serial unit already on a line is a duplicate, never an increment (the tracking rule) |
| `3*` then a scan or Enter on a result | the next item takes quantity 3 (the multiplier is cleared after one item) |
| `+` or `-` (numpad) with a line selected | quantity +1 or -1 (never below 1; Delete removes) |
| A weighed label | quantity from the label (weight) or price from a price-embedded label under the profile the setup scan proved; an unknown prefix is said [C `scale.ts`] |
| `2` or `5t` on a pharmacy line | strips or tablets (unit suffix entry exists: `VoucherScreen.tsx:3244-3265`) |
| Digits typed in the scan field, with or without a line selected | **always a code**: PLU or short code, then the registry; a 13-digit EAN never lands in a quantity box and a 3-digit PLU is never a quantity (RV24) |
| `*` pressed on an empty scan field with a line selected | opens that line's quantity edit; **eight or more bare digits there are refused** ("a barcode, not a quantity") because a 13-digit EAN taken as a quantity is a 100,000-fold stock corruption [C CLAUDE.md "Never let magnitude shorthand near a QUANTITY"]; 100 units or more, or a line of Rs 50,000 or more, asks "Is this right?" first |
| Magnitude shorthand (`5 L`, `2k`) | **never read** in a quantity (`parseQty` shares the arithmetic, not the magnitudes) |

### 3.3 Hold and recall

Held bills are `posDrafts` rows (PD14): `{ id, terminalId, label, cashierId, at, cart, customerId?, total }`, written on **every cart change** (so a power cut or a crash returns the cart: the draft with `label = 'auto'` is the cart itself), never numbered, never a stock reservation, never synced, never in the backup. **F7** holds the cart and clears it; **F8** lists holds with age, cashier, item count and total; recall **re-prices at today's price and flags every change** (price, stock, a promotion that has ended); a hold is recalled only at the terminal that made it (as at GoFrugal [RT 2.2]); holds older than `holdHours` (default 8) are shown in red; **open holds are listed at shift close** and must be discarded (an event) or carried by name. A held bill with a serial unit or a batch does not reserve it: on recall the allocation is proposed again (the parked-bill rule the tracking spec leaves out [PH 6.4 A2]). Restaurant tables are not holds; they are orders (4.2).

### 3.4 Discounts, price overrides, open items, free items

**Rights and caps** live in `Role.pos` (9.2) and are read at the moment of the action; the company's `policy` supplies the defaults. A cashier's defaults (all owner-editable): line discount cap 5%, bill discount cap 3%, price override none, no free items.

| Action | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| **Line discount** (F9 on a line) | percent or paise; clamped to the line; above the cap the approval prompt opens (a full-PIN approval by a person who holds the right and is not the requester); a reason from the owner's list is always asked above 0 when `policy.requireReason` is on (default on) |
| **Bill discount** (F9 with no line selected) | percent or paise; allocated across eligible lines before tax (2.3 step 3); above the cap the same approval; stored in `pos.discount` with its parts |
| **Price override** | `none` (default for a cashier), `down` (to a lower price, with a reason), `any` (manager); an override of a packaged item upward is **blocked by the MRP guard** whatever the right (a manager override of the guard needs the `mrp` approval and a reason and is an events row) |
| **Open item** | 2.10 |
| **Free item or gift** | a zero-amount line, only through the promotion engine or the `gift` reason with an approval; flagged "gift (no consideration)" for the credit-block note [RT 6 row 5] |
| **Discount after the sale** | **not offered**: a credit note is the only lawful route (2.6); a "price correction" button does not exist |
| **Discount abuse controls** | a per-cashier discount rate (discount value over sales value), the approver's name, and the "beneficiary must not approve" rule (the person who benefits from a void or a discount, such as a cashier ringing a relative, cannot be the approver; the system can only enforce that approver and requester differ) |

### 3.5 Promotions, schemes, coupons, happy hour: a small rule model

A promotion is **data** (the `promos` table, P-3a; the `bundle` and `combo` shapes are in it but **off** until the company has recorded its CA's attestation, P-3b), evaluated by the pure `core/pos/promos.ts`, producing **discount allocations per line before tax**. The shapes are fixed; there is no scripting [RT 10.4].

```ts
export interface PromoRule {
  id: string; cid: string; name: string; active: boolean; priority: number;               // lower first
  window: { from?: string; to?: string; days?: number[]; hours?: [number, number][] };    // ISO dates; 0 = Sunday; minutes from midnight
  audience: { all: true } | { group: string } | { coupon: string };                       // a customer tag, or a code the cashier types or scans
  scope: { all?: true; items?: string[]; groups?: string[]; brands?: string[] };
  stack: 'exclusive' | 'with-others';
  budget?: { maxUses?: number; maxAmountP?: number; onLimit: 'stop' | 'warn' };
  shape:
    | { kind: 'pctOff'; pct: number }
    | { kind: 'amtOff'; amountP: number; per: 'unit' | 'line' }
    | { kind: 'priceAt'; unitP: number }
    | { kind: 'bundle'; buy: number; get: number }                                         // buy 2 get 1 of the SAME item: priced once
    | { kind: 'combo'; items: { itemId: string; qty: number }[]; priceP: number }          // buy A get B free = a combo at A's price
    | { kind: 'slab'; basis: 'qty' | 'value'; slabs: { from: number; pct?: number; amountP?: number }[] }
    | { kind: 'billPct' | 'billAmt'; min?: number; value: number };
  apply: 'auto' | 'prompt';                                                                // auto = silent and shown; prompt = the cashier chooses
  reason?: string;
}
```

**Evaluation (the order is the contract).** (1) Take the rules whose window, audience and scope match the cart on the bill's date and time (the terminal clock). (2) Sort by `priority`. (3) For each line, an `exclusive` rule that matches ends the line's evaluation; `with-others` rules add. (4) `bundle` on the same item: `n` units, `f = floor(n / (buy + get)) x get` free; the line's discount is `f x unit gross`, **never a zero-rate line**, and the per-unit net is by allocation. A different-item "buy A get B free" is a `combo` priced at A's price and **allocated across A's and B's lines by list value**, so both lines carry tax on a share (the circular's "two supplies at one price" [RT 6 row 5, [W]]; the rate where the items differ in rate is a composite or mixed supply question, CA). (5) `slab` takes the highest slab reached. (6) A `budget` that is reached either stops the rule or warns, per `onLimit`. (7) All results are summed per line, then added to the bill discount `D` of 2.3 step 3, and allocated **once**. (8) The cart shows each applied rule's name under the line and in the totals; a rule that was available and not applied (a coupon not entered) says so. Happy-hour pricing is a `pctOff` or `priceAt` rule with `hours`; **no Indian vendor page documents time-of-day pricing, so it is [unverified] as an Indian expectation and ships with the engine as just another window** [RS 3.2].

### 3.6 Loyalty, store credit, gift vouchers (P-3c; a CA first)

All balances are **derived from an append-only `custEvents` table** (PD13): `{ id, cid, customerId, kind: 'earn' | 'burn' | 'expire' | 'reverse' | 'credit-issue' | 'credit-redeem' | 'voucher-issue' | 'voucher-redeem', amountP | points, billId?, at, terminalId, expiresOn? }`. A balance is the fold of the customer's events (points expire first-in first-out by `expiresOn`). **Earn** on commit for a bill with a customer: `floor(eligibleNetP / earnPerP) x points` (eligible = the net payable of lines not excluded, owner-set; welcome points on the first bill). **Burn** at tender, as part of the bill discount `D` (so it reduces taxable value before tax; it is never a tender): at most `maxRedeemPctOfBill`, at least `minRedeemPoints`, one point is worth `pointValueP`. **A cancel or return writes `reverse` events** pro rata and re-credits burned points. **Redemption confirmation**: `cashier` (default), `manager above N points`, or an OTP to the customer's phone (LogicERP's control [RT 2.5]) **which needs an SMS or WhatsApp provider and is not in v1**. **Gift vouchers and store credit** are closed-system stored value: a Receipt to a liability ledger at issue (2.4 case 9), a tender at redemption (case 8); a wallet that can be topped up by the customer, transferred or cashed out is **not built** (it is stored value under RBI's prepaid-instrument rules, which nobody here read) [PY 1.4, 1.7]. Whether points are a discount or a payment, and the GST timing of vouchers, are CA questions that gate P-3c (11.2). **Across terminals:** a balance is a fold, so two offline terminals can each burn the same points or redeem the same single-use code; redemption is therefore an online check, or an offline redemption capped per terminal per day and re-checked at sync, and a burn that takes a balance below zero after sync writes an exception row (PD41, RV5).

### 3.7 Customers and consent (the DPDP duties of about May 2027)

A `customers` row is `{ id, cid, phone (normalised: digits, 10, a leading +91 stripped), name?, cardCode?, ledgerId?, group?, createdAt }` (non-contested fields only: **consent and its withdrawal are append-only `custEvents` rows of kind `consent` and `withdraw`, `{ purpose: 'bill' | 'offers', at, by, terminalId, text }` with the notice words the shopper saw, and the current consent is their fold, withdrawal winning**, so two counters adding the same phone at once with different ticks cannot overwrite each other; `lastSeen` is derived from the customer's events and bills, RV17). **Rules:** phone and name are optional at the counter and never required to bill; the phone is the key (two "Ramesh" are two rows with two phones; a duplicate name is never refused [RT 2.1]); "to send you this bill" and "to send you offers" are two purposes and two ticks, offers off unless the shopper says yes; a number typed only to open one WhatsApp link is **not stored** unless the shopper ticks the bill consent; **Withdraw and erase** (an administrator action with a reason) removes the phone, name and card code from the row and from `custEvents`' display and **keeps the bill** (a bill is a tax record kept 72 months [PY 3.8]; how erasure meets retention was not read [PY 6 item 3, unverified]); the bill's `pos.buyer` block is the one place buyer details live on a voucher, written only when Rule 46(e) or (f) needs it or the shopper asked. A processing agreement per shop (Comhard is a processor, the shop the fiduciary), a breach plan and the notice wording are in 11.2; the product claims "DPDP-ready" nowhere [PY 4.5].

### 3.8 Price lists and the order in which a price is chosen

1. **A typed price** with the override right (or an open item).
2. **The batch's sale price** when the item's tracking says the price follows the batch (`batchPrice`) [C `trackTypes.ts:42`].
3. **The terminal's or customer's price level** (the voucher's Alt+Z level, then the party's default, then none: `priceLevel.ts:18-41`; a listed rate of 0 reads as "no rate for this level"); a level list per outlet is v2.
4. **MRP**, when the item keeps one (`mrpAt`), in inclusive mode.
5. **The item's standard selling rate.**

The MRP guard then judges the result against the effective MRP. A promotion is not a price list: it acts after the price is chosen (3.5). **Two MRPs for one batch** (the printed one that identifies it and a lower revised one that caps the price, which is the ordinary case after the 22 Sep 2025 rate cuts) are carried apart: the printed MRP stays in the cell key and is what the line copies; the effective price comes from a dated overlay; this is the tracking lane's finding F1 and its fix is a precondition of P-6 [PH 6.4 F1].

### 3.9 Cashier and salesperson tracking

Every bill carries `pos.cashierId` (the operator who accepted) and an optional `salesperson` (a name from the employee list or typed, per bill or per line from the customer panel, for stores with floor staff). Reports by cashier, terminal and salesperson are over `pos` (8). Commission (a percent or a rupee amount by salesperson, item group or target) is v2 and a report over the same fields; it posts nothing.

### 3.10 The receipt (a data model first, then a layout)

`core/pos/receipt.ts` builds a **receipt model** (header, title, document block, buyer block, lines, tax summary, tenders, payment reference, loyalty line, footer, codes, language) from the saved vouchers, so a reprint is built from the data and says **DUPLICATE** with the reprint number from `posEvents`; two renderers read the model: a **text layout** (32 columns at 58 mm, 48 at 80 mm) and a **raster layout** (384 or 576 dots) for the logo, the codes and any line the printer's code page cannot print [PY 2.2].
