| Section | Content (only what the owner entered or the law asks) |
|---|---|
| Header | shop name, address, phone, **GSTIN**; the 14-digit **FSSAI** number (every food business puts its licence or registration number on its receipts, invoices, cash memos and bills: FSSAI's order of 30 Sep 2021, effective 1 Jan 2022, after the order of 8 Jun 2021 [W fssai.gov.in]) and the **drug sale licence number** (a retail cash or credit memo carries the dealer's name, address and sale licence number, a serial number and the name and quantity of the drug: Rule 65(4)(3)(i) as the CDSCO consolidated text reads [W, page 43 of 874]) **are required to enable the restaurant, pharmacy and food-retail modes, are a red item in the shift-open preflight when blank and a hard stop at Accept** (RV25); the product still **never invents a number**: it prints exactly what the owner entered, with a warn-only shape check like `companyIds.ts`; a logo (raster); the item's optional `localName` through the raster path (below) |
| Title | TAX INVOICE, BILL OF SUPPLY (composition or exempt), INVOICE-CUM-BILL OF SUPPLY (a mixed basket), CREDIT NOTE, BILL (NOT YET PAID) (a provisional restaurant bill, no invoice number), TRAINING - NOT A BILL |
| Document | the bill number, date and time, terminal, cashier; a credit note names the **original's number and date** (Rule 53(1A)) |
| Buyer | GSTIN and name for a B2B bill; name, address and state with code when the bill is Rs 50,000 or more [PY 3.2] |
| Lines | name (wrapped), quantity and unit, rate, amount; under it the MRP, the discount, the batch and expiry, the manufacturer on a scheduled drug, the weight, the IMEI; the HSN (a Data Check keeps it complete; the printed digit count follows the company's band) |
| Tax | **by rate** (taxable, CGST, SGST or IGST, cess), never a blended rate; round-off |
| Total | the payable amount, large |
| Tenders | each with its kind and reference (UPI: UTR tail; card: issuer and last four; cash: tendered and change), **the payment cross-reference** (id, date, time, amount, mode) which satisfies Circular 146 for the B2C QR question without a QR [PY 3.4] |
| Loyalty | points earned and balance, only with consent |
| Footer | the owner's text (return policy, thanks); **never** default legal wording or bank details the owner did not choose [C CLAUDE.md Phase 20 to 21] |
| Codes | the **bill number as a Code 128 and a QR** (so a return starts with a scan); a credit bill adds a UPI QR whose `tr` is the bill number |

The receipt exists **only after the tender is recorded** (a bill sent before payment is a complaint about a competitor [RT 2.1]); the printed copy is the customer's, the saved voucher is the supplier's record, and a reprint is a duplicate [PY 3.1]. **Golden receipts** for five sample bills at 58 and 80 mm in text and in raster are release gates (a half-page print after an update is a complaint about Marg [RT 7]); a 40-line text receipt is under 4 KB [PY 2.2, M].

**Regional-language names (RV38).** An item (and a dish) has an optional `localName`; where the printer link supports raster (USB or LAN) the receipt and the kitchen ticket print it through the raster path (the app's own text layout, so a script the printer's code page lacks still prints); a text-only printer (serial or Bluetooth) prints the English name, and the pilot terms say so.

### 3.11 E-bill and WhatsApp (P-2 for the free paths, later for the paid)

- **`wa.me`**: the app builds `https://wa.me/<digits>?text=...` and **opens the person's own WhatsApp**; nothing is sent by this app, the text is capped at 3,500 characters and the wording says "Open WhatsApp", never "sent", and the product is sold as "share by WhatsApp", never as a "WhatsApp bill": each send costs the cashier a browser or app launch and needs WhatsApp Web or Desktop signed in on the counter PC, so cashiers skip it in a rush, and the provider-based send is the real feature (RV44) [C `share.ts:127-131`, `:108`; CLAUDE.md Phase 29]. The text is an **itemised short bill** (shop, bill number and date, up to the first lines, the total, the payment reference, the return policy line) and, once a hosted page exists, its link. It is offered **only after the tender is recorded** and only for a phone the shopper gave.
- **PDF**: an 80 mm or A5 PDF of the bill through the existing PDF bridge, for the person to attach (a `wa.me` link cannot carry a file) [PY 4.1].
- **Hosted bill page** (PY-7: a random 128-bit token, an expiry, no listing endpoint, one customer's bill and nothing else) waits for TLS and the backend lane; **a provider-based WhatsApp bill** (the Business Platform, templates, opt-in, a per-message price near Rs 0.115 for a utility message on third-party figures, utility-in-window treatment conflicting between two Meta pages) waits for an account and the owner's decision on who pays [PY 4.3].

### 3.12 Customer-facing display, offline cache, preflight, training, peak mode

- **Customer display** (P-5): section 1.5.
- **The offline contract in one sentence a cashier can read** (shown on the offline banner): "You can still sell for cash, credit and recorded UPI or card; stock, prices and bill numbers are this machine's own; UPI and card are marked unconfirmed until the bank statement or the provider says so" [RS 3.13; RT 0.2 item 6].
- **Shift-open preflight**: a green, amber or red list before the first bill: receipt printer reachable and a test line printed on request, drawer test, scanner setup scan on file for this connection, the clock (PD9), free disk over 1 GB, the last backup under 24 hours, sync state, the terminal's series next number **checked against the server's when online (a restored backup or a reset PC is lifted before its first bill; offline it reads "not verified" in amber and a manager acknowledges)**, open holds from yesterday, **the licence and FSSAI numbers where the mode needs them, the vertical's dated lists, the company's size against the ceiling C (PD38), the running index against the engines (PD43), unreconciled journal entries (PD39) and the installed version against the server's minimum (PD44)**; a red item needs the manager.
- **Training mode** (PD34): the counter runs on a throw-away in-memory book copy of the masters; nothing is written; receipts say TRAINING.
- **Peak mode** is not a mode: it is the hot path (PD21), a second billing device, and the rule that the add-item path never touches the voucher array.

### 3.13 What the market does that this product deliberately does not copy

A WhatsApp invoice sent before payment is recorded (a Vyapar complaint); predefined-only roles; refusing a second customer with the same name; a buy-X-get-Y that the cashier must add by hand (slow and a fraud surface; Odoo); deferring invoices while offline; "do not reload the browser" as the offline rule; a licence check that can stop a counter mid-day (the field's most-complained design); tender-rule changes sold by the day (policy is owner data, PD19); a tier that puts shift and multi-location behind a fourfold price step; a margin editor on a bill and incentive-ranked substitutes (pharmacy, 5.7); a default service charge (restaurant, 4.6); a "UPI charge" on a bill; summary posting as the default.

---

## 4. Restaurant mode (slice P-7; v1 is sold as counter service; small dine-in on one device is built but not sold)

**Scope in one sentence.** A restaurant bill is a GST Sales voucher and the order is not; the books can already post the bill, and everything before it (tables, an open check that lives for hours across several kitchen tickets, modifiers, void control) is new surface that nothing else in the app exercises [RS 0.2 item 1]. Everything the retail root provides (the cart engine, the tender, the shift, the rights, the events, the receipt, the bridge) is reused; what follows is only what differs. **Evidence is [RS] unless tagged; statutory points are the study's readings and every one needs a CA (4.6, 11.2). v1 is sold as counter service only (bakery, sweet shop, QSR, canteen; token numbers, pay first); the table, captain-tablet and aggregator pieces are designed here and built, but not sold to a dine-in or aggregator-heavy outlet until captain devices and the aggregator middleware exist: the Petpooja pages read list unlimited users and terminals and aggregator integrations in the Base plan and the captain app, KDS and Zomato or Swiggy reconciliation in a dearer Operations Manager tier [W petpooja.com/pricing, indicative], and re-keying every aggregator order by hand is the first-week comparison a restaurant would make (RV33).**

### 4.1 What is different from retail, and the v1 boundary

| Area | Retail | Restaurant | v1 decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unit of work | a cart that lives a minute | an **order** that lives for hours, takes several tickets, moves tables and splits [RS 7.3] | an order is an event stream with **no voucher number until tender** (an abandoned check burns nothing, which keeps Rule 46 gap-free) |
| What is sold | stock items with barcode, batch, MRP | menu items with variants, option groups, combos; some resale goods | menu items are item lines on **stockless** items (PD27); resale goods stay ordinary stock items |
| Stock effect | at sale | none at sale in v1; ingredients by recipe at day end in v2 | **no ingredient stock in v1** |
| Kitchen | none | stations, tickets, change and cancel tickets | immutable ticket events, printed per station from the counter PC |
| Charges | rare | service charge, packaging, delivery, tip, each with its own tax and consumer-law treatment | all lines or Receipts, **service charge off by default** |
| Splitting | rare | routine; each paid bill is its own tax invoice | by item or equally |
| Channels | online store | aggregators (tax paid by the operator), QR, own site, phone | manual aggregator order in v1 (a reconciliation record split per line class, never the order path); the cloud receiver in P-8 |
| Offline unit | one till | a room of devices that must agree on every table | **one counter device per outlet**; a second tablet only while online; an outlet hub is v2 |
| Compliance | Legal Metrology, MRP | 5% with no credit, composition, 9(5), service-charge law, FSSAI | the profile of 4.6 |

### 4.2 The data model (tables added in P-7's own additive Dexie version)

```ts
// the outlet lives in Company.pos.outlets[]: { id, name, code, godownId, fssai?, address?, gstinOverride?, stations: { id, name, printerProfile }[] }
export interface PosTable { id: string; cid: string; outletId: string; floor: string; name: string; seats: number; x?: number; y?: number }   // name unique per outlet, up to 6 characters
export interface MenuItem {
  id: string; cid: string; name: string; category: string; station: string; veg: 'veg' | 'nonveg' | 'egg' | 'none'; shortCode?: string; photo?: string;
  channels: { dine: boolean; take: boolean; delivery: boolean; agg?: Record<string, { name?: string; off?: boolean }> };     // per-channel names and switches
  variants: { id: string; label: string; stockItemId: string }[];                       // Half, Full: each points at its own stockless item (rate, HSN, tax snapshot)
  optionGroups: { id: string; name: string; min: number; max: number; options: { id: string; label: string; deltaP: number }[] }[];
  combo?: { slots: { name: string; choices: { menuItemId: string; variantId?: string }[] }[]; priceP: number };
}
export interface PosOrder { id: string; cid: string; outletId: string; channel: 'dine' | 'take' | 'delivery' | 'aggregator'; tableIds: string[]; guests?: number; captainId: string; token?: number; ext?: { platform: string; id: string }; openedAt: number; status: 'open' | 'billed' | 'settled' | 'cancelled'; settlementIds: string[] }
export interface KotEvent {                  // append-only; never edited; the order's lines are the FOLD of its events
  id: string; cid: string; orderId: string; seq: number; deviceId: string; at: number; by: string;
  kind: 'add' | 'edit' | 'cancel' | 'transfer' | 'note' | 'printed' | 'bill' | 'settle';
  station?: string; lines?: { lineId: string; menuItemId: string; variantId: string; options: string[]; qty: number; note?: string; course?: number }[];
  from?: string; to?: string;                // transfer: a table move or merge
  reason?: string; approvedBy?: string; kitchen?: 'not-sent' | 'sent' | 'cooked';   // cancel: where the line was
}
export interface ChargeRule { id: string; kind: 'service' | 'packaging' | 'delivery'; mode: 'pct' | 'fixed'; value: number; orderTypes: string[];
  gst: { treatment: 'supply-rate' | 'rate' | 'non-gst'; rate?: number; sac?: string }; beneficiary: 'house' | 'staff'; defaultOn: false | { attestedBy: string; at: number; text: string }; effectiveFrom: string }
export interface PendingOrder { id: string; cid: string; channel: string; extId: string; state: 'placed' | 'acknowledged' | 'accepted' | 'rejected' | 'held'; body: string; liability: Record<string, 'aggregator' | 'merchant'>; receivedAt: number }   // P-8
```

**Why an event stream.** Two captains adding to table 5 on two devices create two ticket documents and the check is their union: no field-level conflict, no lost edit; a voided line is another event; it fits "a pull must never overwrite an unsent local edit" and the outbox [RS 7.3]. KOT events must **not** ride the voucher outbox at full payload for ever: `kotEvents` is its own table, shipped compactly and archived after the day closes and reconciles (an unpruned outbox already grows about 1.2 MB a day at 800 bills a day [RS 7.4; AU 3.1]). `posTables`, `menuItems`, `posOrders`, `kotEvents` join `ALL_TABLES` and the server's `DATA_TABLES`; `pendingOrders` is the cloud's table first.

### 4.3 The order, the kitchen ticket, and who may undo what

- **A ticket is an immutable document**: station, lines, notes, who, when. A change is a **new** ticket of kind `add`, `edit`, `cancel` or `transfer` that prints with a loud banner; a cancelled line stays on the screen struck through; a reprint prints **DUPLICATE** (and writes an event) [RS 3.3]. Item-to-station routing is on the menu item. Courses and fire are v2 (the first screen is one station per item); KDS is v2 (the same events, a screen instead of a printer).
- **Printing.** A ticket event written on a second device reaches the counter PC by sync (0.5 to 4 s) and **the counter PC prints it and writes a `printed` event**; the captain's device shows a line as "sent" only when that event has arrived, so "kitchen notified" is never claimed on a guess. A ticket is an ESC/POS job (3.10's text layout; a kitchen ticket is ASCII or, for a regional language, a raster line) to a **network** printer on TCP 9100 from the counter PC through the device bridge (6.2); a tablet cannot print (the mixed-content trap, 6.4), so in v1 only the counter PC prints. **A printer that is off never drops a ticket silently**: the event is written, the job is queued, and the station shows "kitchen not notified" until it is sent.
- **The undo matrix** (the least that may undo each state, from Toast, Lightspeed and Petpooja's own glossary [RS 3.4]):

| State of the line | Who may remove it | What is recorded |
|---|---|---|
| Before the kitchen ticket | anyone | nothing |
| After the ticket, inside a short grace window (default 2 minutes) | the captain, with a reason | `cancel` event |
| After the grace window or after serving | a manager (full PIN, a different person from the requester), reason | `cancel` event with `approvedBy`; the line is tagged **comp** or **wastage** |
| After the bill is printed | a manager; the invoice, if one was issued, is **cancelled, not deleted** (a shell keeps its number) | `void` event; Cancel of 2.6 |
| After payment, or after the day is closed | a **credit note** | Return of 2.6 |

 The reason list is the owner's; the approver is recorded and must differ from the person who benefits; an order cancellation and a bill void are two different events; **there is no Undo on a printed bill** (PD10). The day-close screen lists the day's voids, comps, discounts and reprints for the manager before the Z report is accepted. **KOT-versus-bill reconciliation** (every ticket line is billed, voided with a reason, or flagged) is a report and a day-close row. **Honest limit:** rights are client-side and the model has no discount, cancel or reprint right today, so v1 says "detective" on the screen (9.4) [RS 3.4].
- **Move, merge, split.** A move or merge is a `transfer` event (both tables show the combined order; status is derived). **Split** is by item or equally, N settlements from one order; each paid bill is its own tax invoice with its own number from the terminal's series; the order's discount, charges and round-off are apportioned in paise by `allocateP` and round-off is per bill [RS 3.5].

### 4.4 The menu

A menu is the part leaders get wrong (complaints: cumbersome, manual updates [RS 5]), so v1 ships a **grid editor**, **Excel and CSV import** (the wave-3 mapped importer with a preview) and a **diff before publish**. Variants (Half and Full) are separate priced sellables, each a stockless item carrying its rate, HSN and tax snapshot; option groups carry a minimum and maximum pick count and a price delta; **a delta folds into the dish line's price** (one line per dish, so a 5%-rate option does not become a second line), and an option with a different tax rate is **refused in v1** (so is a mixed-rate combo: how one price is apportioned across two rates is a legal question nobody researched [RS 3.2]). Channel price lists reuse `PriceLevel` and `StockItem.prices` (dine-in, takeaway, delivery, aggregator); a per-channel validator runs **before** a menu is published to an aggregator and says, for example, "this name differs from another only by digits" (Zomato ignores digits, so "Chicken 65" and "Chicken 95" collide) or "more than four variant groups" (Swiggy) [RS 3.2]. The tracking lane's variant (a quantity split with pooled value) is for "stock by colour" and is **not** the menu variant [RS 3.2; CF AD.3].

### 4.5 Order types, tokens, settlement, charges, tips, the aggregator order

- **Order types.** Dine-in (a table); takeaway (a **token number**, a per-outlet per-day counter, shown on the customer display and the ticket); own delivery (address, phone, rider name, cash to collect); aggregator (channel, external id; Flow D). Takeaway and door delivery are restaurant service at the same rate (Circular 164) [RS 4.1]; what differs by type is the packaging and delivery charge.
- **Counter service pays first.** The cashier takes the order, the tender and issues the invoice at once (retail's flow with menu tiles and a ticket); table service pays later.
- **The provisional bill** (BILL, NOT YET PAID) carries no invoice number; the tax invoice is issued at settlement. (A printed number is an issued document; the siblings' reading is that a pre-settlement bill without the number is the safe design, which a CA confirms [PY 1.3].)
- **Settlement** posts per 2.4 (cases 1 to 3, 10, 11): one tender, S1; mixed, S2; credit to a corporate account, the customer's ledger; every charge a service line; a tip a Receipt to **Tips payable**.
- **Charge rules.** A `ChargeRule` has a kind, a percent or fixed value, the order types it applies to, its GST treatment, **who the money belongs to** (the house or the staff: the distinction the consumer-law dispute turns on [RS 3.6]) and a default state that is **off** until the owner attests in writing (`attestedBy`, date, the wording shown to the diner) and each change is dated and audited. A charge that is on is a **visible line the cashier removes with one key** and the bill says it is voluntary; relabelling "optional" does not cure an earlier default (the CCPA's Chaayos order of 14 Jul 2026 told a chain to change what its billing software does by default) [RS 4.4]. Packaging is on for takeaway and delivery by default (the owner chooses), in the supply's value at the supply's rate (s.15(2)(c); a CA confirms).
- **Discounts and comps** use 3.4's engine with the restaurant reasons; a **comp** is a zero-value line with a reason and an approver, reported, never deleted; staff meals and wastage are CA questions (GST on a free meal) [RS 4.10 item 13].
- **Flow D: an aggregator order, in v1 by hand, per line class.** The cashier opens "Aggregator order", picks the channel, types the platform's order id and, **per line, the class: restaurant service or goods** (item classes default packaged goods and ice cream to goods; the manual screen splits the order or asks). **Restaurant-service lines** post as 2.4 case 12 (a Sales voucher in the aggregator's own type, to the aggregator's ledger, ECO95, no output tax, **no tax invoice printed to the diner**: the operator issues it, Circular 167 Sl. 10 [W]). **Goods and other supplies on the same order** post as the restaurant's own taxable supply at their own rate, with an ordinary tax invoice from the terminal's POS Sales series and the operator's TCS expected (Sl. 8 and 9 [W]; the circular advises the operator to bill the restaurant service separately). The payout reconciliation screen clears the receivable bill-wise against the platform's payout sheet, with the commission and its GST as the restaurant's own expense (a 5%-no-credit restaurant takes no credit on it) [RS 4.3]. Golden cases come from Circular 167 questions 8 to 11 and Circular 164 section 4. The mixed aggregator order is on the CA's list (11.2) (RV11, RV45).

### 4.6 GST profile of a restaurant company (dated data; every row a CA signs)

`Company.pos.restaurant` holds: the scheme (Regular or Composition), the **premises class** (standard 5% without credit, or specified premises 18% with credit, with an `effectiveFrom`), the rate history, the service-charge posture, the packaging default, the **FSSAI number per outlet** (printed on every bill: the 14-digit licence or registration number is required on cash receipts, invoices and bills [RS 4.7]), GSTIN capture on, and the aggregator class on. Changing any is a dated, audited change. The study's readings [RS 4.1 to 4.4, 4.7, 4.11; all with their own evidence tags]:

- **5% (2.5 + 2.5), a mandatory rate with the condition that input credit on goods and services used in the supply is not taken**, for restaurant service other than at "specified premises", takeaway, door delivery and cloud or central kitchens included [RS 4.1, read in the Council's rate table and Circular 164]. **A stand-alone restaurant cannot declare itself "specified premises"** (the CBIC FAQ and the declaration form say only a registered person supplying hotel accommodation can) [RS 4.1]. From 1 Apr 2025 the status depends on the previous year's room values or a declaration [RS 4.1, Notification 05/2025]. **Pending, not law:** the 57th Council's recommendations of 8 Oct 2026 (same-line-of-business credit for restaurant services, 5% without credit for platform delivery services, an amendment of section 9(5)): none is notified, and they might change the no-credit condition; so **"5% means no credit" lives only in the rate history as data** [RS 4.11].
- **Composition**: up to 5% of turnover, a **bill of supply**, no input credit, tax not collected, no inter-state supply, alcohol breaks eligibility; the turnover limit read was Rs 1.5 crore (Rs 75 lakh in eight named states) in Notification 14/2019, **unverified after March 2019**; whether a composition restaurant may sell through an aggregator is the study's inference, not a statement in the circular [RS 4.2].
- **The operator-pays class (ECO95).** From 1 Jan 2022 the operator pays the GST on restaurant service supplied through it and issues the invoice; the restaurant reports the supply and counts it in its turnover; the circular says GSTR-1 Table 8 and GSTR-3B 3.1(c) "for the time being" and the portal now has Table 14(b) and 3.1.1(ii) according to search summaries that **do not name restaurants** [RS 4.3]. **There is no code for this anywhere in `src` today** (a grep finds none) [AU 1.5, Appendix B]. **The class is per line, restaurant service only** (goods on the same order are the restaurant's own supply at their own rate, 2.4 case 12), and an aggregator order is its own voucher type with `docs: 'not-issued'`, so the restaurant's printed series stays a pure series of its own tax invoices and Table 13 does not count the operator's invoices as its own unless the CA says so (a third hunk in `gstr1.ts docsIssued`, P-7a). P-7a adds the taxability, the builders, the type flag and a Data Check, and **does not ship until a CA confirms the table layout and the Table 13 treatment**; until then an aggregator order is entered in the Sales screen's ordinary way outside the POS with the same caution.
- **Mixed bills** (packaged goods on a restaurant bill) are a separate class with their own rate and credit and a separate inventory; a v1 bill that mixes classes uses the separate-lines rule; the common-input credit reversal "as if exempt" is the FAQ's reading and the Rules 42 and 43 arithmetic is the CA's [RS 4.1, 4.6].
- **Alcohol** is outside GST and outside v1 (a Non-GST line type exists; excise and VAT are state rules) [RS 4.6].
- **Small bills**: the Rs 200 consolidated daily invoice is not offered (PD3).

### 4.7 Recipes and ingredient depletion (v2, a day-end journal)

`StockItem.bom` exists and is read only by the Manufacturing screen, Data Check and the Tally exporter; nothing consumes a recipe at sale time [AU 6; C grep]. v1 sells dishes with no ingredient stock. **v2:** a **day-end backflush**: one Stock Journal per outlet per day computed as the sold quantity of each dish times its recipe (kg to gram by compound units), then a Physical Stock count prices the variance; wastage is a Stock Journal to a wastage head; purchases are ordinary Purchase vouchers; sub-recipes need the **multi-level BOM that is not built** [C CLAUDE.md "Not built"]. Live depletion on every sale is v3 (it makes every bill touch many positions). A restaurant item has a **class** (dish, ingredient, resale goods with a rate and an MRP) because the tax side asks for separate inventories [RS 3.10].

### 4.8 Aggregators: the integration plan (slice P-8)

**Decision PD32: a cloud receiver, a non-posting intake table, and a middleware first.** What the contract forces [RS 3.7, 0.2 items 6 and 15, UrbanPiper's public docs]: an order arrives as an **unsigned webhook** (put your own secret in a header) that must be answered with a 2xx within about five seconds, is tried four times over a few minutes and then dropped, and an endpoint that keeps failing is switched off (a circuit breaker after more than 15 connection failures in a minute, re-pushing the missed orders when it re-enables); statuses move forward only; a cancel carries a reason from a fixed list; each tax entry says whether the aggregator or the merchant owes it (`liability_on`) and discounts say whether the merchant bears them. Therefore:

1. **The receiver is the cloud backend** on a public HTTPS hostname (TLS first, PD23); an outlet PC cannot be one. It checks a shared secret header, writes `pendingOrders` keyed on `(platform, extId)` (**idempotent: a repeat is accepted and ignored**), answers inside the window, and **never writes a voucher**. Its request line is logged by a per-route serializer that prints method, path and peer only (the repo's logger redacts only a few key names) [C CLAUDE.md MCP gotchas].
2. **The counter accepts, rejects or holds** a pending order (accept after a late-orders check: "3 orders arrived after the retry window"), pushes the status back, and only then does the POS writer make the bill (2.4 case 12), with the aggregator's order id as `pos.order.ext`.
3. **The REST API is not the door:** it writes raw vouchers with no numbering or balance check [AU 1.5]; POS types are refused there (PD33).
4. **Menu publish** goes through the channel validator of 4.4; stock toggles and store snooze are explicit controls on the counter; a void on the platform must also be done on the platform and the screen says so (a known fragility with one POS and UrbanPiper) [RS 5].
5. **Reconciliation:** the payout sheet matches receivables bill-wise; commission per platform is a report.
6. **Direct Zomato, Swiggy and ONDC APIs are partner programmes** (an NDA, an IP whitelist, partner-only docs; no public Swiggy page was found); the middleware conversation and the partner applications start **now** because calendars, not engineering, are the long pole [RS 3.7]. Effort: 30 to 45 builder-days of ours (6 to 9 engineer-weeks [RS 7.2]) after TLS.

### 4.9 The outlet hub for several devices (v2, designed only as far as the risk)

Cloud-only sync cannot keep two offline tablets agreeing on a table: pulls reload the whole book per page and a nudge arrives every 0.5 to 4 s [AU 5]; Petpooja gates multi-device offline behind an on-premises server and Toast says orders do not cross devices offline [RS 2.6, 7.4]. **The v2 shape** is a hub that is a role of the counter PC's Electron process: the KOT-event log, the print server for every ticket and bill, the day close and the offline queue, the app shell served to tablets over the LAN, syncing to the cloud as a **relay and a cache, not a fork** [RS 7.4]. **What must be spiked before anyone promises it** (none was run): a tablet loading the shell from a plain-HTTP hub without mixed-content blocks (service workers, install and camera are off on plain HTTP); a hub whose PC sleeps, restarts or is replaced (the replacement pulls the log before it serves); two hubs started by mistake in one outlet (one lease, held in the cloud and cached); clock skew between devices; the Electron app's missing kiosk and power-save settings [RS 7.4; AU 7]. v1 sells the one-device restaurant and says so.

### 4.10 Restaurant reports (added to the Reports Center in P-7 and P-9)

Item-wise, category-wise, hourly, day-wise, order-type and channel-wise, payment mode, captain-wise, table turn, void and comp and discount and reprint audit by person, **KOT-versus-bill reconciliation**, no-sale drawer opens, GST summary through the existing return builders, and the day-end report of 2.7 with the voids-and-comps review; v2 adds food cost and variance, aggregator reconciliation, outlet comparison [RS 3.12].

---

## 5. Pharmacy mode (slice P-6; the retail chemist first, the stockist later)

**Scope in one sentence.** A pharmacy bill is a GST Sales voucher written by the same counter writer, with six additions that together make a pharmacy and not a billing app: the batch engine's sale-time pick, per-batch MRP with a tax-inclusive line and a price cap, strip and loose, the schedule gate with the prescription and Schedule H1 registers as reports, licence numbers and the pharmacist on the memo, and free-quantity schemes and the expiry-return worksheet where the trade's money moves [PH 6.7]. **Evidence is [PH] and [TS]; the Drugs Rules were read by the pharmacy study in CDSCO's consolidated PDF (its edition date unstated), and its readings are carried with its tags. Compliance wording is cautious on purpose (5.8).**

### 5.1 What it depends on, and who owns each ask

The batch spec gives a pharmacy "a correct batch engine and a sale-time pick, not a pharmacy" [PH 0 item 10]. Of the pharmacy study's 14 Tier 0 items, 5 cannot work without the tracking engine, 6 work in part and 3 do not depend on it; the spec designs 3, designs 6 in part and **does not design 5** (strip and loose, the schedule gate, the registers, licence numbers, free quantity) [PH 6.2]. The mapping of the study's asks (A1 to A12) and findings (F1, F2) to owners:

| Ask or finding | What it is | Owner | POS gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F1** | a revised (lower) MRP written through the batch overlay moves the next sale off its stock cell; carry the printed MRP (identity, in the key) apart from an effective price with a from-date | the tracking lane (before its allocation panel exists) | **P-6 cannot start until fixed** (11.1 asks the owner to pass it on) |
| **F2** | the spec's dispense label is Rule 94(2) (a medicine made up for a patient); a cut strip is Rule 65(19): a separate `loose` label kind | tracking lane (template data and a rules row) | before the loose-sale step |
| **A1** | a **no-stop FEFO pick** when one batch covers the need and is neither short-dated nor in a warning tier (the chip still shows batch, expiry, MRP) | tracking panel's caller; the POS implements the same rule in its own component | P-6 |
| **A2** | the allocation panel as a self-contained component (cells, need, policy in; lines out) and parked-bill semantics | the POS builds its own over `pickCellsOf`, `proposeAllocation`, `splitToLines`; the tracking lane keeps the pure functions stable | P-6 |
| **A3, A9** | the loose-supply label; a rack field on the item | tracking lane (labels); the item master (POS) | P-6 |
| **A4** | the tax-inclusive MRP sale line | **this spec** (2.3), shared with retail | P-0b1 |
| **A5** | free quantity on the line and in the inward grid | this spec for the sale (5.3); the tracking lane's inward grid for the Free column | P-6 |
| **A6** | PTR and PTS at batch grain; an effective price applied in bulk by company | tracking lane (overlay) with POS reading | P-6 optional |
| **A7, A8, A10** | the schedule derived from a dated list; the `rx` capture and registers; licence numbers and the pharmacist | **this spec** (5.2, 5.4, 5.9) | P-6 |
| **A11** | the expiry-return worksheet (challan, pending credit) | **this spec** on top of the tracking report | P-6 |
| **A12** | batch holds from a regulator's alert list | **this spec** on top of the tracking hold | P-6 optional |

**Sequencing.** The pure parts (the dated schedule list and its derivation from a composition, the register and alert builders as pure functions over vouchers) start now as new files; the fields and screens that touch `types.ts` and the item form queue behind the one-writer files [PH 6.6].

### 5.2 The item master and the dated schedule list

Pharmacy mode switches on (`features.pos` plus `Company.pos.verticals` includes `pharmacy`) a tail group on the stock item, **after the last existing field** (the frozen-walk rule) or in an Alt-key grid: `composition: { substance, strength, unit }[]`, `form`, `route`, `manufacturer` (the tracking spec's typed field, T34), `schedule` (`none | C | H | H1 | X`, plus the NRx marker; **derived, stored with a reasoned override**), `rack`, the pack hierarchy through compound units (box > strip > unit, base unit the smallest sold unit), `noSub` (no substitute offered). A text "Salt" field is **not** the design: it can be searched but cannot be matched safely ("Paracetamol 500" against "Paracetamol 500 + Caffeine 30") [PH 3.1].

**The schedule list is dated data, not code** (the `drugSchedules` table, P-6's version): `{ id, cid, source, asOf, entries: [{ substance, schedule, effectiveFrom, covers: 'salts' | 'exact', route?: string, notes? }] }`, editable, with its **source and date shown on screen** and a Data Check "list dated more than 12 months ago". The derivation takes an item's composition, matches each substance (normalised) against the entries in force **on the bill's date** and stores the strictest schedule with `derived: true`; a person may override with a reason. **The list ships loaded, not empty** ("an engine with no input path is not a feature" [C CLAUDE.md Phase 29]): enabling the mode requires loading the Comhard-supplied dated file (a CSV prepared from CDSCO's schedules and **signed off by a pharmacist and an advocate before P-6 releases**), because Schedule H has 551 numbered entries that extend to salts, esters and derivatives with carve-outs for topical preparations, Schedule H1 has 48 in the copy the study read, and **pregabalin was added to H1 by G.S.R. 377(E) of 13 May 2026, in force 180 days after Gazette publication: about 9 November 2026 if the Gazette date is 13 May and about 16 November if it is 20 May, as one report gives** (the pharmacy study carries both; `effectiveFrom` is loaded `SCHEMA?` with both candidates noted, and the Gazette text is read before the file ships, RV40) [PH 3.9, 5 T1]. No drug database is claimed; none is bundled; an interaction checker is out of v1 (a silent all-clear is read as "safe"; a duplicate-therapy check, the same substance twice on a bill, comes free from the composition data) [PH 3.11].

### 5.3 The counter in pharmacy mode

- **One search box** over brand, salt, alias, barcode, strip code and rack (3.1's lookup plus composition tokens); each row shows maker, pack, MRP, stock and, for a batch item, the earliest sellable batch and its expiry.
- **The batch pick** is the tracking engine's: FEFO proposal, expired blocked (the default for a new item, with an Inventory-right override and a reason), held and recalled batches excluded, a minimum remaining life at issue, a `warnDays` ladder (90, 60, 30), a short batch rolling into the next with a visible split; **A1: when one batch covers the need and none of those fire, the proposal is taken without opening the panel**, and the chip still shows batch, expiry and MRP so nothing is silent. The panel opens on an exception or a key. A new batch cannot be typed at a sale (R27) [TS 4.4, 5, 6.3]. A marked **sample or scheme-stock** batch cannot be sold (65(18)) [PH 3.9].
- **Price.** A tax-inclusive line whose base is the MRP; the unit rate is the strip price over the units per strip, never above the pro-rata MRP; a discount comes off the MRP; **a hard stop above the effective MRP**, a soft stop below the batch's cost (needs the batch purchase rate, which the tracking report carries), and a retail margin shown on the line **for a role that may see margin** only. Never an editor of the recorded margin of a bill (a Marg line the study would not copy) [PH 2.2, 3.7].
- **Strip and loose.** Base unit = the smallest sold unit; `2` is strips, `5t` tablets; **whole strips plus loose units per batch** is shown in stock and asked in a count; **a loose sale needs the pharmacist on duty and queues a loose-supply label** (Rule 65(19): drug, quantity, the dealer's name and address; batch, expiry and unit MRP are the shop's own additions, labelled so); the **rounding rule of a per-unit price is recorded and never above the pro-rata MRP** [PH 3.2].
- **Free-quantity schemes** (A5): a purchase line carries paid and free units (the inward grid's Free column, the tracking lane's); a sale line `freeQty` follows the **net-rate method**: `amount` = paid units x rate, `qty` = paid plus free, `rate` = amount over total units, the free units shown separately and moving real stock in a named batch; tax is on the consideration (Circular 92/11/2019 as read through secondary summaries); **a free line is never a zero-amount line** (the voucher screen refuses it, and the e-invoice schema has a `FreeQty` that Comsky does not send); free units of a different item or batch is a CA question [PH 3.6]. Dated group schemes by company with slabs are Tier 1, on the promotion engine of 3.5.
- **Margin-loss warning** and a **short-list key** (an unavailable medicine and the customer's phone, with consent, feeds reorder) [PH 3.15].
- **No stop for an OTC bill.** The schedule gate (5.4) appears only when a scheduled line is on the bill, which alone is a speed feature [PH 3.15].

### 5.4 The prescription record, the gate, the registers

```ts
export interface PosRx {
  v: 1; sched: ('C' | 'H' | 'H1' | 'X' | 'NRx')[];                      // from the LINE snapshots, not today's list
  prescriber: { name: string; regNo?: string; address?: string };
  patient: { name: string; address?: string; phone?: string; age?: number; sex?: string };
  rxNo?: string; rxDate?: string; timesAllowed?: number; interval?: string;
  imageRef?: string;                                                     // a local store key; never a public link; retention is a setting
  pharmacist: { id: string; name: string; regNo?: string };             // the shift's pharmacist
  regSerial: string;                                                     // 'RX-T1-000123': written on the prescription (65(3)(1)); a per-terminal register series
  h1Serial?: string;                                                     // the separate Schedule H1 register's serial
  repeatOf?: string;                                                     // the earlier voucher this repeats (65(11))
}
// InventoryLine gains, snapshotted at sale: sched?: 'C' | 'H' | 'H1' | 'X' | 'NRx', mfr?: string   (the register line needs the manufacturer, batch and expiry as they were)
```

- **The gate keys on the line's schedule snapshot** (C, H, H1, X). A bill with such a line cannot commit without `pos.rx` carrying the prescriber's name, the patient's name and the pharmacist on duty; H, H1 and X are supplied **only on a practitioner's prescription** (65(9)(a)), so an OTC sale of them is a **Block with an Administration-right override and a reason**, an events row, in the pattern of the credit-limit override; Schedule X needs the duplicate-prescription reference and is Tier 2 as a register (rare in a chemist's shop) [PH 3.9]. A Schedule C line needs manufacturer, batch and expiry even on an OTC bill (65(4)(1)).
- **The prescription itself must be endorsed.** Rule 65(11)(c) requires that, at the time of dispensing, the seller's name and address and the date of dispensing be noted on the prescription above the prescriber's signature [W CDSCO text, page 46 of 874]. **The software does not do this**: the bill prints a line "Endorse the prescription: name, address, date" for every H, H1 or X line, and the register never claims the endorsement was made (RV40).
- **A repeat dispensing** of an H, H1 or X prescription is not allowed unless the prescriber allowed it (65(11)): `timesAllowed` and `interval` are tracked and **there is no automatic refill nudge**; **a reminder, if a shop sends one, is a `wa.me` link the staff opens, worded "your medicine is due, please bring or send a current prescription", never an offer, and only while `timesAllowed` has not run out** [PH 3.17].
- **The register serial** is assigned at commit by the writer from a per-terminal counter derived from the vouchers (like `posSeries`); the Rule says the entry's serial is written on the prescription (the printout has a line for it). Whether several per-terminal series are acceptable to an inspector is an advocate question (V1 in 11.2).
- **The registers are reports over vouchers** (one source of truth): the **prescription register** (serial, date, prescriber, patient, drug and quantity, and for C, H and H1 the manufacturer, batch, expiry and the pharmacist), the **Schedule H1 register kept separately** (prescriber's name and address, the **name** of the patient, the drug, the quantity; **kept three years and never pruned**), the Schedule C register entry; the memo-book option declared to the licensing authority at licence time (65(3)(2)) is a company setting that decides which the report prints [PH 3.9]. **Because POS bills are alteration-locked, "lock after print" is automatic**; a correction is a dated `rxAmend` event (`field`, `from`, `to`, `reason`, `by`) shown in the register as "amended" with its history [PH 3.8, 3.19].
- **Doctors and patients are not ledgers.** The pick-lists are derived from the vouchers' own `rx` records and keyed on the phone number or registration number, so a mis-spelt name is not a second person; promote to a stored master only when something needs one [PH 3.8, 5 T23].
- **Electronic prescriptions.** The pharmacist may attach any image or file; **the product never states that an e-prescription is valid** (unsettled in commentary) [PH 3.8].
- **NDPS.** A drug that is also under the NDPS Act (the NRx label) is treated as Schedule H1 for the gate and the register. The NDPS Rules' own record for a retail seller was read only through a case-law site's copy: it sends a retail chemist back to Rule 65, and for a psychotropic **consignment** wants both licence numbers, the batch and the transport details on the invoice (a wholesale matter); **the pharmacy study found no separate NDPS register that a retail chemist keeps, and that stays an advocate question** [PH 3.9, 5 T13].
- **Patient data is health data.** The `rx` block is role-gated, never part of a public link, never in the MCP read (PD33), has a retention setting, and an access log; the DPDP duties bind from about May 2027 [PH 5 T10, T27].
- **The year-roll caveat.** The H1 register must be inspectable for three years but a new financial year is a new company (PD35): the register screen has a company-year switcher and an Excel export per company, and **there is no consolidated cross-company register in v1** (the app has no multi-company report). The Year roll wizard carries the `rx` pick-lists, not the history.

### 5.5 Substitutes: a neutral panel, off where the law says

Three uses, three behaviours [PH 3.10]. (a) **Same-salt alternatives for stock the shop holds**, from the shop's own master (batch, expiry, MRP, stock), matched on **identical substances, strengths, dosage form and route**, **sorted by price to the customer, ascending, with no incentive sort**; the choice is logged on the line (who, what was replaced). (b) On a **scheduled prescription line** the panel is replaced by "No substitution on this prescription" (65(11A): no other preparation may be supplied in lieu, the same substance or not) and an action "ask the prescriber" that starts a fresh prescription line. (c) An **out-of-stock capture** (the short-list). A list of banned fixed-dose combinations is a blocker flag, not a suggestion filter. **An incentive-ranked substitute programme is not built.**

### 5.6 Near-expiry, expiry returns, the regulator's alert list

- **Near-expiry and expired reports** are the tracking lane's (Expiry Ageing, Expiring and Expired), by supplier and window (30, 60, 90, 120 days, per company, default 90).
- **The Not-for-sale godown** (a `quarantine` godown the pharmacy preset creates) receives an expired batch by a Stock Journal, so the 65(17) proviso is met by a location, not a deletion [PH 3.5].
- **The supplier-return worksheet** (A11): pick a supplier, list its batches inside the return window, value at MRP, purchase rate or landed cost, print a **delivery challan** (route (b) of Circular 72/2018 as read) and track the return **pending** until the supplier's credit note arrives; a pending-claims report by supplier and age is the money screen. The Act's s.34(2) limit applies to the credit note's tax effect [PH 3.5].
- **A batch hold from a regulator's list** (A12, Tier 1): a screen where a person pastes rows from a CDSCO Not-of-Standard-Quality or spurious-drug alert (product, batch, maker) or loads a cleaned CSV; the app lists every batch in the company whose number matches under the tracking engine's own normalisation and whose item or maker looks alike, with the quantity held, whom it was sold to (from the POS bill's customer if any) and which supplier it came from; **the pharmacist ticks which to hold**; the hold records its source. **Never automatic and never a verdict**: batch numbers are free text and collide across makers, so the wording is "your stock has a batch with the same number as one on the alert", never "this batch is unsafe"; the PDF is never parsed silently [PH 3.20]. A synced pharmacy must not trust a hold until the tracking lane's server rule (hold wins, capability `trk1`) is deployed [PH 6.4].

### 5.7 What pharmacy mode deliberately does not build

Incentive-ranked substitution (the pharmacist's margin between the patient and the prescriber's choice); doctor commission tracking (the physician's receipt of a commission is barred by clause 6.4.1 of the 2002 Medical Council regulations; the chemist's side is unsettled; a neutral doctor-wise sales report is fine); automatic refill reminders on scheduled drugs; any marketing message that names a scheduled drug (the draft Rule 65(22), G.S.R. 861(E) of 28 Sep 2026, is in its comment window; Rule 74(p) already binds manufacturers); an "adjust margins of bills" feature (it edits the recorded margin; an audit trail's opposite); a bundled drug database; "no interaction" all-clears; insurance and loan resale; marketplace or ONDC work; the distributor segment (salesman app, route, e-invoice through a GSP, party licence control), which waits behind the mobile and GSP tracks [PH 6.1, 6.2, 5 T4 to T9].

### 5.8 Compliance wording

**The software may say:** "prints the prescription register in the columns Rule 65(3) lists"; "keeps Schedule H1 entries for three years and will not prune them"; "warns before an expired batch is sold"; "shows the schedule list's date and source"; "the licensee is responsible for the register and for keeping the list current". **It may not say:** "Drugs and Cosmetics Act compliant", "inspector-ready", "audit-proof", "approved by CDSCO", "legal in every state", "no interaction", "safe substitute", "this invoice is your prescription register", "a valid e-prescription", "this batch is unsafe", "DPDP-ready" [PH 6.3]. **A gate that blocks names the rule in the sentence that blocks** ("Schedule H item: prescriber and patient are required by Rule 65(9) and 65(3)"), with the rule as a citation the owner can read and an administrator override logged with a reason. **Print only what the owner entered, and require it before the mode is enabled:** the licence number prints exactly as entered, is **required to enable pharmacy mode** and blocks Accept when blank (RV25), with a warn-only shape check [C CLAUDE.md Phase 20 to 21; `companyIds.ts:80`]. **Rules watched, nothing built:** the draft Rule 65(2A) (G.S.R. 791(E), 8 Sep 2026: CCTV for premises that supply prescription drugs, recordings kept three months) is hardware and footage, not software [PH T7]; the draft Rule 65(22) (G.S.R. 861(E), 28 Sep 2026) is in 5.7; both are on the advocate's list (11.2).

### 5.9 Licences and the pharmacist on duty

`Company.pos.identity` holds the shop's drug licences (Forms 20 and 21, or 20B and 21B), the FSSAI number (restaurant), and per outlet where they differ; **required before pharmacy or restaurant mode can be enabled (3.10)**; **there is no such field today** (`companyIds.ts` holds only CIN, Udyam and IEC lines) [PH 3.9]. The pharmacist's registration number is a custom field on the operator (`User.udf`, which the custom-fields branch supplies) or a field of the shift; the **pharmacist on duty is chosen at shift open** and shown in the header (a carried value must always be visible); a loose sale and a scheduled line need one. A wholesale buyer's drug licence number and validity (a warning on a scheduled line to a party with none or a lapsed one) belongs to the distributor segment, not v1.

### 5.10 Testing a pharmacy mode, and what stays open

The test is real counters, not this document: six chemists (observe a morning, time ten real bills on their software and on ours), one registered pharmacist reading the register formats line by line, an advocate reading 5.8's lists, a CA on free goods and expiry returns [PH 6.6]; the number to beat is **our own Sales-screen time for the same bill**, not a vendor's "40 to 50% faster" (the target hypothesis, with no evidence: a five-line mixed bill with two scheduled lines and one loose in under 30 seconds with a scanner and under 60 without) [PH 3.15]. **Open and unresolved in the study:** whether a software printout satisfies a register written for paper, what the NDPS Rules ask of a retailer beyond Rule 65, whether an e-prescription satisfies Rule 65(10), the chemist's side of commissions, GST on free units of another batch, real strips' codes (a URL carries no batch) [PH 7].

---

## 6. Payments and hardware: what is built in-house, what needs a partner, how the two builds reach devices

### 6.1 The split

| Capability | In-house (no partner, no hardware beyond a bench printer) | Needs a PSP, acquirer or other partner | Needs TLS first | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cash, change, credit (khata), store gift voucher and credit | yes | no | no | P-2, P-3c |
| UPI as a **recorded** tender with a typed reference, cashier-attested, matched to the bank statement | yes (`bankStatement.ts`, `brsMatch.ts` already import and match statements [C]) | no | no | P-2 |
| UPI **static QR for the exact amount** (the owner's VPA link; `tr` and merchant fields when configured) | yes (`upi.ts`, `qr.ts`) | no | no | P-2 |
| UPI **dynamic QR with a confirmation** (a PSP callback or poll) | the interface and the manual provider | **a PSP merchant account** (Razorpay, PhonePe, Paytm, Cashfree were read from public docs only) | **yes** (a callback endpoint) | P-8 era, 11.1 |
| Card on a standalone machine (typed approval code and last four) | yes | the shop's own acquirer | no | P-2 |
| Card or UPI on an **integrated terminal** (the amount pushed, the status returned) | the interface | **an acquirer relationship** (Pine Labs cloud API, Paytm EDC request, Razorpay POS Bridge: read from public docs, second hand for the last) | yes for the cloud variants | later |
| Soundbox confirmation | none (the soundbox is the cashier's own ear, not a data path) | the PSP's soundbox API (not found on the page read) | yes | later |
| Bank-feed settlement matching | the statement file path exists | a bank partnership (ICICI first per the live-bank-feeds research) | yes | later |
| Receipt, kitchen ticket, drawer, display | yes (the bridge, the emitter, the model) | a printer maker's certified list | no | P-5 |
| Hosted e-bill, provider WhatsApp, SMS | the link and template text | a WhatsApp Business provider (Meta account, templates, opt-in, per-message price); an SMS aggregator with DLT registration | **yes** | P-8 era |
| E-invoice (IRN) for a B2B bill over the threshold | none (the app never generates an IRN) | a GSP per `einvoice-gsp.md` | yes | later |

### 6.2 The device bridge (`electron/deviceBridge.cjs`): one pattern for the receipt, the drawer, the display and the scale

**Pattern (the Phase 29 rule, exported from `exportBridge.cjs`):** the renderer is not trusted; a handler is a pure factory with its collaborators injected so a suite runs the same function against a fake `electron`; a request is **validated, not repaired**; one job at a time (`busy`); the answer says "sent to the printer", never "printed" [C `electron/exportBridge.cjs:8-16`; PY 2.6]. **One bridge, one allow-list, one printer list, one Windows helper, shared with the tracking lane's labels** (PD22): the file is `deviceBridge.cjs`; `labelLang.cjs` (merged, TSPL only) and a new `escposLang.cjs` are its emitters; the tracking spec's `labels.*` IPC names stay and POS adds `receipt.*`, `drawer.*`, `display.*`, `scale.*` (the T-7a builder adopts the file name; a one-line change in their slice, flagged in 11.1).

| IPC | The renderer sends | Main does | Refuses |
|---|---|---|---|
| `devices.printers()` | nothing | the live `getPrintersAsync()` list as `sys:<name>`, the allow-listed network printers as `net:<uuid>`, enumerated serial ports as `ser:<id>` | any host, path or port not in main's own list |
| `devices.addNetwork` / `removeNetwork` | nothing (main opens its own small window) | stores a private-address host and a port from 9100 to 9109 in `userData/printers.json`, written only by main; a public address is refused at connect time as well | everything else |
| `receipt.print(job)` | a **structured** `ReceiptJob` (below) | builds the ESC/POS bytes itself, writes them in one spooler write, TCP stream or serial write, polls status before and after where the printer reports it | unknown keys, wrong types, a raster whose length is not `widthBytes x heightDots`, widths other than the profile's, `copies` over 3, a job over its size ceiling, any command string |
| `drawer.open(printerId, reason)` | a printer id and a reason (`sale`, `noSale`, `shift`, `test`) | sends the profile's **fixed** pulse (pin and times from a short allow-list), writes a **drawer event** to `userData/drawer.log` with a time and the reason, returns it | any timing from the renderer; more than one pulse inside 2 s |
| `display.open()` / `display.update(state)` | the customer view's state (items, total, QR payload, payment state) | opens a window on the second display and relays the state | a URL, a script, Node integration |
| `scale.open(portId)` / `scale.onWeight` | a port id | opens the Web Serial port main selected (`select-serial-port` and the grant kept by main), parses by the model's profile, emits `{ kg, stable }` | a baud or a parser from the renderer |
| `kiosk.status()` | nothing | answers the flags main read from its own config | any write (the flags come from a launch argument or `userData/terminal.json`, never the renderer) |

```ts
export interface ReceiptJob {
  jobId: string; printerId: string; reason: 'sale' | 'reprint' | 'kot' | 'z' | 'x' | 'label-test';
  kind: 'text' | 'raster'; copies: 1 | 2 | 3; cut: 'none' | 'partial' | 'full'; drawer: boolean;
  lines: ReceiptLine[];                                                 // 'raster' kind: one raster element per band
}
export type ReceiptLine =
  | { t: 'text'; s: string; a?: 'l' | 'c' | 'r'; b?: boolean; size?: 1 | 2 | 3 }     // printable ASCII plus the profile's extra characters; 'Rs.' in text mode
  | { t: 'rule' } | { t: 'feed'; n: number }
  | { t: 'qr'; payload: string } | { t: 'code128'; value: string }
  | { t: 'raster'; widthBytes: number; heightDots: number; dataB64: string };
```

**The emitter allow-list** (`escposLang.cjs`, a pure CJS twin of a renderer-side `core/receiptJob.ts` builder, asserted equal on one corpus as `labelJob.ts` is [C `electron/labelLang.cjs:26-27`]): initialise, text in the profile's code page, line feed, alignment, bold, size, a raster block, a QR block **only if the profile says it is native** (otherwise a raster), a cut, a drawer pulse, a feed. **Never** the printer's memory-programming or macro commands, a logo-download command, a baud or code-page persisting setting, or any byte the renderer supplied outside a text run or a bitmap. The profile carries `columns` (32 or 48), `codepage`, `rupee: 'text' | 'raster'`, `cut`, `drawerPin`, `link`, `baud`, `rasterOk`. **Ceilings:** 400 lines and 256 KB per text job, 512 KB per raster job, a ticket of 80 lines [M, sized from PY 2.2: a 150 mm 80 mm raster is 84 KiB, a 40-line text receipt about 2 KB].

**What a receipt costs that a label does not** [PY 2.6]: it prints on every bill, so (1) the Windows spooler write uses a **long-lived helper process** (a bundled, hash-checked PowerShell script reading jobs from stdin, started once, restarted on failure) and **not one process per job** (hundreds of milliseconds each, unmeasured); (2) the first byte should reach paper under a second after Accept on USB, a figure gated on real hardware; (3) **printing never blocks Accept**: the sale is committed first and the receipt is a queued job in a renderer-side **print queue** (persisted per device: queued, sent, failed, retry count) with a visible lane message ("Receipt not printed: Retry, Reprint later"); (4) a reprint is built from the saved voucher, marked DUPLICATE, and writes an event. A serial or Bluetooth printer defaults to **text-only with "Rs."** (a raster of 84 KiB takes 45 s at 19,200 baud) and USB or LAN may use raster for the logo, codes and unprintable lines [PY 2.2, M].

**Packaging, honestly.** Only a **rebuilt installer** carries any of this; the Phase 29 PDF bridge **has never run on a real Electron binary**, the Windows installer is unsigned and was never run on Windows [C CLAUDE.md Phase 29], and `package.json` pins Electron `^31.4.0` (Chromium 126) with an NSIS target and no updater [C `package.json`; a grep finds no `electron-updater`], which is outside Electron's policy of supporting the latest three majors [W electronjs.org timelines]. So: **P-5p upgrades Electron first** (re-running `webContents.print`, Web Serial, the single-instance lock, silent print and the PDF bridge on the new Chromium) before the packaging gate; the bridge's first real run on a packaged build, with a real 58 mm and a real 80 mm printer, is a **gate** of P-5, not an assumption (11.1 asks the owner for the bench kit); and **P-5o is the operations kit** (6.8). Two writers share `electron/main.cjs` and `preload.cjs` (Back's optional B-6 and the label bridge's T-7a); whichever goes second rebases and neither starts while the other is open [TS 15.1]. **Windows 10 or 11, 64-bit, is the floor** (a reviewer read Electron's README as Windows 10 and later with x64 and arm64 binaries only; not re-fetched here), and the pitch says so (RV29).

### 6.3 How a receipt reaches a printer, by platform

| Path [PY 2.5] | Where | Silent | v1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R1 browser dialog** with an injected `@page { size: 80mm auto }` | the HTTP site, Electron, any browser | no (a dialog per bill) | the universal **fallback**, unproven until the one-day printed spike (one receipt per page at 100% and whether a blank page follows); the settings that worked are shown beside the Print button |
| **R3 Electron `webContents.print({ silent: true, deviceName })`** | Electron | yes | tried first on a driver printer; Electron 31 does not state the units of `pageSize` or whether `print()` honours `@page` [TS 13.6], so the P-5 gate runs both on a packaged binary; failing that, the fallback is "save a PDF and print it from a viewer" |
| **R4 Electron raw ESC/POS**: TCP 9100 to a network printer; the Windows spooler's RAW data type through the long-lived helper; Web Serial to a COM or paired Bluetooth-serial printer | Electron on **Windows** | yes, with status | **the default for a counter**; macOS raw (CUPS) is out of scope for v1 |
| R5 WebUSB, Web Serial, Web Bluetooth | Chromium on HTTPS | no (a chooser per device) | not in v1 (HTTPS first; WebUSB on Windows needs the WinUSB driver bound to the device; the CDC-ACM class is unavailable to WebUSB on Android) |
| R6 a local agent (QZ Tray) | any browser with a per-PC install | yes, signed | not in v1 (one more thing to install and support; a paid support tier on the vendor's store) |
| R7 CloudPRNT and Server Direct Print | the printer pulls its jobs over HTTP | yes | not in v1 (printer families that support it only; needs internet and a server) |
| R8 Epson ePOS SDK | an HTTPS page to a printer with a certificate | yes | not in v1 (the mixed-content rule) |
| R9 an Android native shell or a print-service app (RawBT) | Android | dialog or silent by plugin | v2, with the mobile track |

### 6.4 The tablet and browser builds, and the mixed-content trap

**The counter is a Windows machine running the Electron app** (PD23): it prints silently, sends raw bytes, drives a second screen and a serial scale, and can act as a store hub [PY 2.5 feasibility table, J]. **A tablet browser or an Android Chrome page** can run the POS UI, take a **keyboard-wedge scanner** (a scanner is HID keys; no device API), show the UPI QR and print through the **system print dialog** only; it cannot print silently, open a drawer, read a serial scale or drive a second screen. **The trap that kills most "a tablet prints through the counter PC" ideas:** a page served over HTTPS may not call an `http://` address, including a LAN address, so a tablet that loads the app from `https://books.example` cannot call `http://192.168.1.20`. The routes out: (a) the tablet loads the app from the hub itself over plain HTTP on the LAN (then service workers, install and the camera, which need a secure context, are off), (b) the hub serves HTTPS with a certificate the tablets trust (a real hostname, as Epson's secure-hostname trick does), (c) every device talks outbound only to the cloud and a printer or hub pulls its work (fails offline), (d) a native shell not subject to browser rules. **Decision for v1: no tablet printing; the counter PC is the only printing machine**; the choice among (a) to (d) is the owner's and is needed before the restaurant hub (11.1) [PY 2.5, 5.5 item 2]. The camera and any PSP callback, hosted bill or aggregator webhook need **HTTPS**, and **the live site is plain HTTP on one VM**, so TLS and a hostname are started first and in parallel with the pure slices [PY 0 item 14].

### 6.5 Payments: the tender record, the confirmation state, the providers

**The tender record** is `PosTender` (2.5). **Confirmation states and who may move them** (a state machine `core/pos/payState.ts`, every illegal transition refused and tested): `pending` (a request is out) to `confirmed` (source `psp`, `terminal` or `statement`), `failed` or `expired` (a timer: the QR or request expires, ten minutes in one vendor's flow); `unconfirmed` is the **cashier-attested** state (a typed UTR or a "mark received" with no provider) and moves to `confirmed` only through the bank statement or a provider. **Cash and credit are `confirmed` at once. A bill whose UPI or card tender is `unconfirmed` is complete and printed, appears in the shift-close list, and is cleared when the imported statement carries its UTR** (the existing one-to-one matcher learns to recognise a UPI UTR; a net settlement of gross minus the merchant discount is the **settlement voucher** of 2.4 case 16, whose single bank entry the matcher matches, so no many-tenders-to-one-line rule is added; every change of state is a `tender` event, never a write to the bill (2.5); a tender still unmatched after the owner's wait is written off or converted to a receivable, 2.4 case 17) [PY 1.3, 5.2 PI-5].

**Pay first, issue second.** Where a provider exists, the QR or the terminal request is created for the **draft**, with the draft id as `tr`; the invoice number is taken only after `confirmed`; the printed invoice carries the payment cross-reference (id, date, time, amount, mode), which is what Circular 146 accepts for a B2C bill without a QR, so an abandoned QR burns no number [PY 0 item 4, 1.3, 3.4]. A restaurant pays after eating, so the provisional bill carries no number (4.5).

**The provider interface** (`core/pos/payProvider.ts`): `start({ billRef, amountP, mode }) -> { ref, qrPayload?, expiresAt? }`, `poll(ref) -> { state, utr?, rrn? }`, `cancel(ref)` (a **two-step** act on a terminal: cancel on the machine, then in the POS), and `recover()` (a payment taken but not recorded: the machine charged, the PC crashed). **The manual provider ships first**, so the tender screen does not wait for any partner; one PSP QR provider and one integrated terminal follow the owner's choice (11.1). Split tender with a provider is one request per part.

**The UPI link.** `upi.ts` extends with optional `tr`, `mc`, `mid`, `msid`, `mtid`, `url`, **written only when configured**, so with nothing configured the bytes are unchanged and existing invoices print identically; the NPCI text the repo cites is a **third-party copy of the November 2017 draft** and is re-checked before launch [C `upi.ts:3-8`; PY 1.2]. A merchant-created signed QR needs the acquiring bank's registered key; the repo's unsigned link makes a payer's app warn that the source could not be verified, so the owner's own-VPA link suits a micro-merchant (the 2026 framework's "P2PM" tier up to Rs 1 lakh a month) and a PSP QR suits the rest [PY 0 item 2, 1.2].

**Money rules, as data (never in code):**
- **A dated merchant-discount table**: NPCI's FAQ of 15 Sep 2026 introduces 0.4% on person-to-merchant UPI payments above Rs 2,000, capped at Rs 300 from Rs 75,000, nothing up to Rs 2,000 or for small merchants taking up to Rs 1 lakh a month on a QR into their own accounts, a flat Rs 5 for named sectors, and **merchants may not pass it on to customers**; **the start date is unverified** (15 Oct 2026 on paper; one page says after Diwali), the rates are read from a broker's copy of the FAQ [PY 0 item 3]. The POS ships a **cost-per-tender view** from the table (each UPI payment is one payment for the Rs 2,000 line) and **no surcharge field exists on any UPI tender**.
- **Cash cap**: a running total per **known** customer per day with a warning at Rs 2 lakh (section 269ST as the study read it through commentary; the Income-tax Act 2025 renumbers it, reportedly section 186, unconfirmed) [PY 1.6].
- **Over-tender**: a non-cash tender is capped at the balance; only cash gives change.
- **A digital-acceptance duty**: a business above Rs 50 crore must offer a RuPay debit card, UPI and UPI QR (section 269SU); the POS offers UPI at every counter by default [PY 1.6].
- **Aggregator-ness.** Comsky never collects, holds or settles a customer's payment, never runs a pooled account; the customer is the merchant of record at a PSP or an authorised acquirer and Comsky passes references and statuses; **whether merely generating a QR for a partner's merchant account is a payment-aggregator activity is not answered by anything the study read** and is a question for advice before any partner integration [PY 1.4, 11.2].

### 6.6 Scanner, scale, display, drawer: what exists and what is a bench test

- **Scanner.** A wedge scanner needs nothing; the burst detector, resolver and setup screen are the tracking lane's merged pure engines, wired by T-5b; **the POS does not wait for them** (PD27). The POS reads the scanner profile (`scanDevice`) once T-5b exists and a **scan target** from `registerScanTarget`. A 1-D scanner cannot read a QR (`reads2D` is a setup-scan result).
- **Scale.** A price-embedded or weight label read by the scanner (a profile from the setup scan; no Indian scale maker's own layout was found, so the shop's own printed label is the test [C `scale.ts` header]); a scale on a serial port is `scale.*` (Web Serial through main, a per-model profile, **a simulator for tests because no maker's serial protocol was found**) [PY 2.4]. The POS records the scale's **verification due date** and warns near it (Legal Metrology: an instrument used in trade is verified and stamped; the reverification period is unconfirmed) [PY 2.4].
- **Drawer.** Opened by the printer's pulse after a cash tender, a no-sale (a right and an event) and a test; **a drawer open is not a data write, so it is an event with the operator** (PD16).
- **Display.** 1.5.
- **The bench kit** (the owner supplies; nothing in any study substitutes for it): one Windows PC with the packaged Electron app; one 80 mm USB printer with a drawer port (the study names the TVS RP 3160 Gold or an Epson TM-T82 class unit) and a drawer; one 58 mm Bluetooth printer; one network ESC/POS printer; one serial or USB scale with its manual; a wedge scanner and a 2-D imager; a card machine in test mode; a soundbox. **For each:** the exact driver settings that give one clean receipt, time from Accept to first paper, whether the cutter and drawer fire, how `Rs.`, the rupee glyph and a Hindi line print, and what a power cut mid-job leaves [PY 2.7]. **A "hardware we tested" list ships in the product and nothing is promised beyond it** [RT 4.17].

### 6.7 What the app must never store, log, sync or send

A card number, expiry, CVV, PIN or track data (a **PAN sentinel**, a 13 to 19 digit run that passes the Luhn check, is refused or masked in the free-text fields where a cashier types a reference, narration or note, **with the reason in the message, and is never applied to a scan target or to a serial or IMEI field** (an IMEI is 15 digits with a Luhn check digit by design [J], and about one 13-digit string in ten passes Luhn by chance), and is asserted absent from the outbox and every backup); the payer's UPI VPA or phone beyond the bank reference (UTR or RRN) and a status; an Aadhaar number; an operator's PIN or a customer's OTP in any log; a prescription image or a patient's details in a public link, an MCP read or the REST API; a hosted-bill token without an expiry; a scanner's raw debug output containing a shopper's data; and **any statutory number a government system issues** (an IRN, an e-way bill number) that was not typed or imported (Phase 29) [PY 1.5, 1.8; RT 6 row 14; C CLAUDE.md]. Where the VM sits against the RBI's payment-data localisation FAQ (payment data in systems in India for payment providers and their vendors) is **unchecked** [PY 1.5, 6 item 13].

### 6.8 Operations: installer, updates, diagnostics and the lapse contract (PD44, slice P-5o)

- **A signed installer.** The owner buys a code-signing certificate (11.1 Q25) and the build signs the NSIS installer; SmartScreen warns a shopkeeper off an unsigned one. The first install on Windows 10 and on Windows 11 is a gate.
- **Updates.** An update channel (a pinned `electron-updater` feed or an equivalent served from the owner's host, chosen in P-5o) that **downloads at any time and applies only when the terminal's shift is closed** (never mid-shift, never during a held bill), with a **minimum version** the server states through the capability handshake (the mobile spec's `X-Client` and HTTP 426 pattern, mobile-app-spec 3.5): a terminal below the minimum may close its shift and back up but may not open a new one. Switching POS on stays refused while any device seen in 30 days lacks `pos1` (7.5), so a release is staged: backend first, then terminals at shift close, one counter at a time.
- **Diagnostics.** A page (Settings, POS) and an **exportable support bundle**: app, Electron and Chromium versions, last sync times and queue depth, the print queue and printer test results, the drawer log tail, free disk, last backup, series state per type, the journal's unreconciled entries, the running-index check and the last 200 events. It contains no card data, no customer phone or name and no PIN (6.7), and the owner can read it before sending it.
- **The lapse contract** (a 402 means cloud writes are locked and local data is safe [C `sync.ts:647`]). **Never gated: billing, the Z close, local backup, reprint.** Gated, and said so on screen in one sentence each: sync, a new terminal, the Replace terminal wizard, a remote close, the batched number claim (numbers keep being issued locally and are claimed after the plan is restored), hosted bills and a PSP callback.
- **The restore run.** A backup is restored on a scratch machine that never signs in (10.4 item 3) before the pilot, and again each quarter by the partner (RV30).

---

## 7. Terminals, stores, sync, roles and the server

### 7.1 Terminals, outlets, and the Add terminal wizard

```ts
export interface PosTerminal {                // table posTerminals (synced; written by an administrator)
  id: string; cid: string; name: string; code: string;                    // code: 2 to 6 upper-case letters or digits, unique in the company
  outletId: string; mode: PosMode;
  salesTypeId: string; returnTypeId: string; receiptTypeId: string;       // the five voucher types of 2.5 (the aggregator and adjustment types are made on first use)
  paymentTypeId: string; contraTypeId: string; replacedFrom?: string;     // a replacement terminal names the one it replaces (below)
  cashLedgerId: string; safeLedgerId: string; godownId: string;
  walkInLedgerId: string; salesLedgerId: string;
  createdAt: number; retiredAt?: number;
}
export interface Outlet { id: string; name: string; code: string; godownId: string; fssai?: string; address?: string; gstinOverride?: string;
  stations?: { id: string; name: string; printerProfile: string }[]; upiLedgerId?: string; cardLedgerId?: string }
export interface TenderDef { id: string; kind: TenderKind; label: string; ledgerId?: string; confirmable: boolean; key: string }   // key: the F-key row it takes
export interface PosPolicy {                  // the numbers a cashier's caps default from; every one has a default and a reason (3.4, 2.6, 2.7)
  roundTo: 'none' | '100' | '50' | '10' | '5'; roundDir: 'nearest' | 'down';
  stockPolicy: 'allow' | 'warn' | 'block'; requireReason: boolean; reasonCodes: Record<string, string[]>;       // per action
  lineDiscCapPct: number; billDiscCapPct: number; priceOverride: 'none' | 'down' | 'any'; mrpGuard: 'warn' | 'block';
  refundCashMaxP: number; returnWindowDays: number; creditNoteCutoff: { rule: string; source: string; asOf: string };   // SCHEMA? (2.6)
  holdHours: number; maxDifferenceP: number; closeCarry: 'all-to-safe' | 'keep-float'; cashCapP: number;
  clock: { warnMinutes: number; blockDays: number }; reprintFree: number; noSaleNeedsApproval: boolean;
}
export interface CompanyPos {                 // Company.pos (synced; Phase 27 duty: rev, per-device mirror, restore, server keep rule; written only by writeCompanyPos)
  v: 1; rev: number; verticals: ('retail' | 'pharmacy' | 'restaurant')[]; scheme: 'regular' | 'composition';
  outlets: Outlet[]; tenders: TenderDef[]; policy: PosPolicy; salesByGroup?: boolean;
  identity?: { drugLicences?: string[]; fssai?: string };                    // printed only when entered
  loyalty?: unknown; charges?: ChargeRule[]; restaurant?: unknown; pharmacy?: unknown;   // later slices own their shapes
}
// Role.pos?: RolePos is section 9.2.   Device: TW_DEVICE.pos = { terminalCode, mode, kiosk }   (per device, never synced)
```

**The Add terminal wizard (P-1/P-2; a bulk writer, so it tags `origin: 'flag'` and is one undo step, and it ends in a Data Check).** Steps: (1) name, code, outlet, mode; (2) **ledgers**, each created or picked: `Cash - <code>` under Cash in hand; the safe (`Cash - Main`); per outlet `UPI Collections`, `Card Collections` (Bank Accounts) and `Walk-in customers` (Sundry Debtors, not bill-wise); `Gift vouchers payable`, `Store credit payable` and `Tips payable` (Current Liabilities); `Cash short and excess`, `Goodwill refunds`, `Merchant discount` and a loss ledger for written-off tenders (Indirect Expenses); `Round Off` (found by name); the **output tax ledgers** by head (and rate where the company keeps rate-specific ones), checked to exist; the sales ledger; (3) the godown (**the outlet's single godown for a retail terminal**, so two counters on one shelf do not split the stock; a new `Counter <code>` only where the stock is physically separate, with an explicit transfer step, RV18); (4) the five **voucher types** through the template validator of 2.5; (5) the tenders and their F-keys; (6) on this device: printer, scanner, drawer; (7) **a test bill in training mode**. **Retire terminal** keeps every number and ledger and refuses new bills; **Replace terminal** is a separate wizard: the new device **pulls the history from the cloud** (lane A, never a restore, PD29), asks the server for each series' next number and starts above both that and the last number the manager declares was issued; it names the terminal it replaces (`replacedFrom`), the old device's open shift is closed remotely (2.7), and **a declared range of bills that never synced is handled by 2.4 case 18, never by cancelled shells**. A Data Check verifies each terminal has every ledger and type, no template can overflow 16 characters, no two terminals share a code, and the clearing debtor is zero.

### 7.2 Several terminals in one store

Shared: the company, the masters, the price lists, the customers, the promotions, the policy. Per terminal: three series, a cash ledger, a godown (or a share of the outlet's), a shift, drafts, a printer profile, a scanner profile. **Sales of one terminal are visible to the others** after sync (a return of a bill made on another counter works once that bill has synced; the return screen says when the machine last synced). A held bill is the terminal's own (3.3). **The stock a terminal shows is its own copy** and can be behind another terminal's by the sync lag; that is PD15's policy and 2.12's report, not a bug. A second billing device is the queue-breaker on a festival day.

### 7.3 Several stores

**One company per GSTIN; outlets in one state share a company** with a godown, a terminal series and a cash ledger each (PD35) [AU 10.1; RT 4.16].
