Stock moves between outlets by a **Stock Journal with a transit godown** (the tracking spec's godown kind `transit`, with the batch, MRP, expiry and units mirrored [TS 7.5]). A central item master is the company's; a **per-outlet price list** is a price level per outlet (v2). **Reports by outlet** come from `pos.terminalId` mapped to the terminal's outlet (no dependency on any other build), and **profit by outlet** from the custom fields' "Income and expense by field" (merged in `1d22e84`), with an Outlet header field on the POS types (its report computes its own tie to the Profit and Loss and must show 0.00 [CF CF2, CF22]; the change in stock cannot be attributed by a header field and stays one line, so per-outlet cost of goods still comes from godown stock reports) [AU 10.1]. **Several GSTINs and consolidation across companies are not built** (the app has no consolidated multi-company report) and the device pulls the whole tenant (every company) [AU 10.1, 3.3].

### 7.4 Sync: volume, partial sync, conflicts per table, onboarding a terminal

**Volume.** At 3,000 bills a day a terminal writes about 3.3 MB of vouchers and 3.7 MB of change-log rows a day [M, 2.11]; the cloud keeps one `change_log` row per put with full payload and no retention rule [AU 3.1]; `voucher_number_issue` is not used by device-owned series (PD8). What POS adds to the outbox: at most one extra voucher (the Receipt) for the minority of mixed or credit bills, a handful of event rows per shift, and customers; **`posDrafts`, the print queue and the drawer log are local and never synced**. **Lane A** removes the whole-book reload per pulled page, makes the duplicate-number check incremental, bounds the outbox reads of the Audit Log and the Cancelled register, and streams the backup (PD21, P-0g). **A cost model belongs in the price (11.1 Q6):** a bill writes about 1.1 KB of voucher and 1.2 KB of change-log row [AU 3.1], so about 1.9 MB a day and 0.69 GB a year per terminal at 800 bills a day [M: 800 x 2,354 B x 365], `/sync/pull` sends every document to every device, and the cloud's `change_log` has no retention rule; fifty three-counter stores add roughly 100 GB a year to one VM (RV42).

**Conflict model, table by table (decided here so the server's keep list and the client's merge agree):**

| Table | Writers | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `posTerminals`, `promos`, `Company.pos` | an administrator | last write wins with a revision; `Company.pos` has the Phase 27 machinery (a revision, a per-device mirror, a restore path, a server keep rule that keeps the current value against a write that states no base) [C CLAUDE.md Phase 27] |
| `posShifts` | the terminal that opened it; a manager's **remote close** is a `posEvents` row the terminal applies, never a second writer of the shift document | single writer; a stale write to a **closed** shift is refused by the server in tier 2 (7.6) |
| `posEvents`, `custEvents`, `kotEvents` | any device | **append-only with unique ids**: a union; no conflict is possible |
| `customers` | any terminal | the **id is deterministic from the company and the normalised phone** (a hash), so two terminals that add the same phone at the same time write the same row; **the row holds only non-contested fields** (phone, name, card code, ledger link): the server's policy is last write wins with a recorded mismatch and the client never overwrites an unsent edit, and neither merges fields, so **consent, withdrawal and `lastSeen` are `custEvents` rows (append-only, a union) and their current value is a fold** (RV17) |
| vouchers (POS bills) | the terminal that numbered them | single writer, never altered (**the server refuses a change to a POS voucher's financial content, 7.5**); a duplicate number is, first, the claim's `taken`, then a Series integrity row and the incremental `checkDuplicates` mismatch (P-0g1; today it stops looking above 5,000 arriving vouchers [C `sync.ts:678-680`]); **two credit notes beyond the sold quantity are an Over-returned bill row (PD41)** |
| `posOrders`, `menuItems`, `posTables` (restaurant) | captain devices, administrators | orders are the fold of `kotEvents`; menus last-write with a revision |

**Onboarding a new or replacement terminal in a company that already has a large book (PD29).** A pull replays every revision of every document of the whole tenant in pages of 500 (502 pages and 502 full reloads for a 250,000-voucher company today) [AU 0.6, 3.3]; lane A (P-0g1) applies a page without a reload, and **that is the path**: the terminal signs in and pulls. **A backup file is not a seed.** The existing restore is a replace: it queues an outbox put for every row in the file and a delete for every row the device holds that the file lacks, the backup header carries no pull cursor, and a restore write skips the cloud's cancelled-voucher guard [C `db.ts:505-571`; `backupGuard.ts:77-97`; `db.ts:449-472` with `backupMigrate.ts:239-249`; `conflict.js:68`, where `needsCancelCheck` excludes any change with a `restoreId`]. Restoring yesterday's file onto a third terminal of a 250,000-voucher company would push about 310 MB in 834 batches of 300 under the tenant lock as new revisions that every other terminal then pulls, replace every document edited since the backup (item prices, `Company.pos`, a shift) with yesterday's version on the cloud and on every device, bring a bill cancelled today back to life (Table 13 and GSTR-1 change), and, on a terminal that already holds today's bills, delete them everywhere [C; M arithmetic: 250,000 x 1,238 B]. So for a POS company **a restore that would delete or replace any voucher carrying `pos` is refused** (the client's `restoreImpactOf` already measures what a restore deletes and replaces, so the refusal is one rule over it) and the server refuses a delete of a POS voucher even when it carries a `restoreId` (7.5). A disaster restore is a partner procedure into a fresh company on a scratch machine, not over the live one. **Seed-from-backup (P-2c, optional, built only if the lane-A pull at the ceiling C is too slow on the low-end PC):** a separate import that (1) writes the tables only and queues **no** outbox rows, (2) refuses a device holding unsent rows or any data of that company, (3) requires a backup written with an **empty outbox** (a new header field `outboxEmpty`, backup schema 5), (4) takes the exporter's pull cursor and its namespace from new header fields (`pullCursor`, `cursorKey`) so the first pull starts there, (5) writes the baselines `stampBase` needs [C `sync/baseline.ts`], and (6) is gated by a test that seeds a second device on a tenant that has a bill cancelled after the backup and proves nothing is re-pushed, un-cancelled or deleted (RV1).

### 7.5 Older clients, backups, the server's keep list

- **An older app version re-saving a POS bill** (a narration edit) would erase `Voucher.pos`, because the save rebuilds the voucher field by field [AU 0.3]. **Four layers, in the pattern of the custom-fields and tracking work:** (1) `pos` joins CF's carry funnel and is classified in `voucher-resave` (which fails on any `Voucher` key nobody classified); (2) the server keeps `pos` for a writer lacking the capability marker **`pos1`** (read from `sync_device.caps`) the way it restores `udf2` [CF 9.3]; (3) a POS voucher is also `cancelled`-protected: the cloud already keeps a cancelled voucher cancelled [C `conflict.js:51-62`]; (4) **backup schema 5, written only when the company holds POS data**, so an older app refuses a backup it would damage (schema 3 is custom data, 4 is tracking data) [AU 12]; (5) **immutability, pulled forward from tier 2 into P-0c (RV12):** the server **refuses, and records as a mismatch, a put that changes the financial content of a stored voucher that carries `pos` or a POS type** (lines, entries, party, amount, date, number, type) **and a delete of one, whatever the writer and even under a `restoreId`**, except a put that is a cancel shell (the cancelled-voucher rule already governs it; the shell keeps its `pos` stamp) and an identical re-put (a retry). The server cannot judge who is authorised to cancel (no role is resolved server-side until tier 3), so this rule narrows what a stale or older client can do and does not replace the client's rights. The Sales screen refuses a voucher with `pos` **by capability, not by build** (an older build that cannot read `pos` is refused at open when the cloud says the company has POS), and a `syncconflict` case covers an older writer's put and delete.
- **Company switch-on is refused in a synced company while a device seen in the last 30 days lacks `pos1`** (the tracking lane's rule T33 for `trk1`), and says which device and when it was last seen.
- **REST and webhooks.** `REST_DENIED` gains `posTerminals`, `posShifts`, `posEvents`, `customers`, `custEvents`, `posDrafts`; the REST writer refuses a voucher that carries `pos` or names a POS type; outbound webhooks fire only from REST writes, never the sync path, so **a POS bill never fires `voucher.created`** (an integration gap, said in the release notes) [AU 12; mobile-backend-readiness].
- **The MCP bundle** drops `pos` from every read (it carries buyer addresses, phones and approvals) except `{ role, terminalId, shiftId, at }`, never loads `customers`, `custEvents`, `posEvents`, and its write planner refuses a POS type; **every edit of a locked engine file ends with `cd mcp && npm run drift` and a re-lock** (PD33) [C CLAUDE.md MCP gotchas].

### 7.6 Roles, rights, and the server-side enforcement the counter will need

**What exists, and its limit.** A role is areas plus create, alter and delete; cancel counts as alter; there is no discount, price-override, refund, reprint or no-sale right; a cashier needs the Accounting area, which also opens the Day Book, the cash book and the outstandings; the server accepts any table name of the right shape and any payload from any member of the tenant; the cashier terminal holds a **tenant-wide write token**, and `/sync/pull` hands every member every document, **including operator PIN hashes and the company passcode vault** (the REST API strips them) [AU 9, 0 item 10; mobile-backend-readiness]. So a cashier terminal carries every PIN hash of the company (PBKDF2, 310,000 iterations: a four-digit PIN falls to an offline guess in seconds). **v1's controls are therefore detective and deterrent, and the screen says so;** the app's own text calls the model "a client-side control for people sharing a screen, not a data-security boundary" (`access.ts:12-14`).

**What v1 adds (client-side):** the area `pos` and the `Role.pos` block (9.2); a voucher carrying `pos` is writable by an operator holding `pos` rights only through the POS writer's actions (create sale, create settlement, return within limits, cancel within the shift, shift events) and **never altered or deleted**; `voucherDenial` and `writeDenial` get one added rule for it; every refusal is an event.

**The four tiers of server enforcement (PD36), each with its own exit test, none in v1:**

| Tier | What the server enforces | Needs |
|---|---|---|
| **T1 credential allow-list** | a **terminal credential** (a `device_session` of the mobile spec's BE-2 with kind `terminal` and role `counter`, **not a second identity model**) can write only its own voucher types, the POS tables it owns and nothing else; cannot delete a voucher; is excluded from the pull of `users`, `roles` and the vault | **BE-2's member accounts, device grants and edge gate** (the mobile spec's `device_grant`, `device_request`, `device_session` and the per-request membership check, mobile-app-spec 2.6, 3.2) and the invites work (assessed, not built: no way to add a second account to a tenant, no e-mail sender, plain HTTP, `max_users` display-only; a token's tenant is trusted without a membership check) [HANDOFF "Team members and invites"; AU 9]; TLS first |
| **T2 immutability** | a write to a closed shift, to a cancelled shell, or that reuses or goes below a terminal's series is refused; a voucher dated more than a day from the server's time is refused. **The first piece, a POS voucher's financial content and its deletion, is in v1 (P-0c, 7.5 layer 5)**; the rest waits | `conflict.js` already keeps a cancelled voucher cancelled; the rest is new |
| **T3 value policy** | discount over the role's cap, a sale below the batch's cost beyond a floor, no-sales over a count, refunds over a cap are refused or flagged server-side, reading `Company.pos.policy` and `Role.pos` from the stored documents | a role-resolving read of the stored `users`, `roles`, company documents in the push path (the server reads payloads only for keep rules today [C `conflict.js`]) |
| **T4 second-identity approval** | an approval is **signed by the approver's own token** (not a PIN typed on the cashier's machine), so a cashier cannot forge one | T1 and a per-user session |

New files: `backend/src/posPolicy.js`, a migration numbered **after** the tracking lane's `009_unit_events.sql` and allocated together with the mobile spec's BE-2 tables at build (the POS adds only what BE-2's tables lack: a terminal kind and a role, and the claim route), `scripts/pg/posPolicy-pg.mjs`; the **backend half deploys before the SPA** and the owner runs the smoke on the VM (the custom-fields branch's own release order) [CF notes 6].

### 7.7 The mobile and tablet tie-in

The mobile spec now exists (`docs/design/mobile-app-spec.md`, 25 Oct; revision 1 of this file said it did not, wrongly): **React Native on Expo, Android first, one app with an Owner shell and a Field shell, engines shared as a built bundle** (mobile D-02 and D-04), a device-bound credential per person (D-15 and D-16), device-owned series with a batched claim (D-10, 2.5.6), and **counter billing, invoices from a phone and Bluetooth printing are v2 there**, waiting for per-device invoice series and a CA decision (mobile D-24, 1.3, section 9 item 16). This POS design is that decision's other half: PD8's device-owned series with the batched claim is the protocol mobile v2 would use, one server function serves both (PD40), and a phone counter shell later reuses `core/pos/*` unchanged. The design costs the mobile app nothing: **`core/pos/*` imports no DOM, no Dexie, no React and no store** (a purity scan that must prove it examined files) so the cart, pricing, tender, shift, receipt and report engines run under Hermes; the **counter PIN is a fast salted hash** (the phone has no WebCrypto, and a pure-JS PBKDF2 takes about 22 s in an interpreter-only engine [mobile-app-spec 2.3]); and the "counter staff" persona S3 is a later **phone shell over the same engines**. **Until then a tablet is a lookup, order-taking and owner device** (6.4). **Engine locks:** mobile's `engines/entry.ts` bundles `gstRates`, `upi`, `voucherCancel`, `accounting`, `billwise`, `singleEntry`, `creditLimit`, `scanResolve`, `money` and `dates` among others and carries its own `engine-lock.json` with a drift check (mobile-app-spec 2.3); the POS edits `gstRates.ts` (P-7a), `upi.ts` (P-2) and `voucherCancel.ts` (P-4), and `types.ts` (P-0c) is an input of both packages, so each of those edits trips **both** locks (the lock map in 10.2). `access.ts` is in neither the MCP lock nor mobile's first-cut entry [C `mcp/engine-lock.json`; mobile-app-spec 2.3, which lists it under "not in it"], so P-0c's `access.ts` edit trips no lock. The prerequisites a POS terminal shares with the mobile app are the mobile backend plan's: HTTPS, a second identity per tenant (BE-2), the server-side gate (7.6), a pull a phone can afford, and push.

### 7.8 The Year roll and the Period roll

A new financial year is a new company (series restart, Table 13 per year); a **Period roll** (2.15) does the same inside a year when a company nears its ceiling (PD38). Both use the **one-sided Split Company Data writer** of 2.15 (`src/data/periodRoll.ts`): it writes only the later half, carries closing balances, stock and open bills (the engine's carry [C `splitCompany.ts`]) and `numberFloor` per voucher type (a split on 1 April starts a new Yearly window and sets no floor; a split inside a year carries the running number so no number repeats), then **copies `Company.pos`, the terminals (same codes), the outlets, the tenders and the policy**, carries `customers` and the **balances of `custEvents` as opening events**, keeps shifts and events as history in the earlier company, and switches each terminal's active company **only after its shift is closed** (an automatic switch at midnight on 31 March would split a night shift). The roll is run **after the last shift of the old period has closed and before the first bill of the new one** (the night of 31 March, or of the last day of the month chosen), because it cannot move vouchers: the wizard refuses when the original holds a voucher dated on or after S. Variants already carry across a split (the split engine changed with `1d22e84`); batch cells wait for the tracking lane's T-0b, and the roll says what ends at the split. **Risk:** a kiosk terminal that must change company at a boundary is a procedure the partner rehearses with the owner (in March for the Year roll, at the first 80% banner for a Period roll); a company rolled late simply stays past C until the next boundary and the red preflight item keeps asking.

---

## 8. Reports and dashboard widgets

Every report is a **pure engine plus a thin screen**, a row in the Reports Center catalogue (`ui/reportsCenter/catalogue.ts`), reads through the Phase 26 row filter where the report is a register, and **ties to the report it stands beside to the paisa** (the Phase 28 rule). The grid (`core/listQuery.ts`) is not on `main` (a `report-grid` worktree, `.wt-grid`, is building it and was not read here); the reports use the existing report sheet and `reportSheet.ts` text columns until it lands.

| Report | Question it answers | Source | Tie |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Z report, X report** (per terminal and shift) | what the drawer should hold and how the day went | `pos.shiftId` vouchers, `posEvents`, the cash ledger | 2.7's four ties |
| **Day summary** (all terminals, one outlet) | the owner's end-of-day page | the Z reports of the day | sum of the Z reports = the day's Sales, tax and tender ledger movements |
| **Sales by tender**, **cost per tender** | takings by cash, UPI, card, credit; what the merchant discount costs | `pos.tenders`, the dated discount table | tender totals = the ledger movements (cash per terminal; UPI and card per outlet) |
| **Sales by cashier, terminal, outlet, hour, salesperson** | who sold what and when | `pos` stamp | = Sales register total |
| **Sales by item, category, brand; margin** | what sells and at what margin | item lines, cost at issue | = Stock Summary movement and Top-N |
| **Discounts given** | line, bill, promotion, loyalty, by cashier and approver | `pos.discount`, `posEvents` | discount value = gross less net |
| **Exceptions** (voids and cancels, returns and refunds, no-sales, price overrides, open items, reprints, refused approvals) **per cashier against peers** | who is out of line | `posEvents` over a window | counts equal the events |
| **Unconfirmed and unmatched tenders** | UPI and card taken but not yet matched to the bank statement | `pos.tenders` against `brsMatch` | listed items = unconfirmed tender total |
| **Oversell** | items or cells below zero after sync, with the sales that took them there | the running index and the vouchers | = negative positions in Stock Summary |
| **Held bills** | holds older than the limit, per terminal | `posDrafts` (device-local: reported by the terminal at shift close) | n/a |
| **Cash movements** (float, drops, pay-ins, pay-outs, over and short) | where the cash went | `pos.role` vouchers | = the cash ledgers' movements |
| **Series integrity** | gaps, duplicates, a date running backwards, a template that could overflow | `docsIssued` and the terminal series | = Table 13 |
| **Customers**: top, dues, loyalty liability | who buys and what is owed | `customers`, `custEvents`, bill-wise | liability = the sum of balances |
| **Restaurant**: KOT-versus-bill reconciliation, table turn, channel-wise, captain-wise | see 4.10 | `kotEvents`, settlements | every ticket line is billed, voided with a reason or flagged |
| **Pharmacy**: prescription register, Schedule H1 register, Schedule C entries, near-expiry, expiry-return pending | see 5.4, 5.6 | `pos.rx`, the tracking reports | register rows = the scheduled lines sold |
| **Over-returned bills**, **lost ranges** | credit notes beyond the sold quantity across terminals; declared lost ranges and how each was handled | the fold of `pos.originalId` credit notes; the Replace terminal wizard's records in `posEvents` | the listed quantities equal the credit notes' lines |
| **Settlements and unsettled tenders** | what each Collections ledger still holds, by age, and what was settled in which batch | `pos.tenders`, the `tender` events, the settlement vouchers | each Collections ledger equals its unsettled tenders (zero after a full cycle) |
| **Volume views** | a day-wise roll-up of a ledger or register, with a drill to one day | the same vouchers | the roll-up total equals the ledger's movement |

**Dashboard widgets** (rows in `ui/dash/widgets/registry.ts`, each through the one door `runWidget`, a rights check before the compute, a lazy pass named in `meta.engine`, reconciled to its report): Today's takings by tender; Bills and average bill by hour; Top items today; Open shifts and drawer expected; Unconfirmed UPI; Exceptions today; Cash short or excess this month; Held bills older than the limit. **The existing "Top customers" widget ranks the party leg**, which for a POS company is "Cash" or "UPI Collections" (`rankParties` reads `partyOf`) [C `dashboardData.ts:106-116`], so a POS company's board uses a POS "Top customers" widget over `customers` and credit ledgers instead, chosen by the company's feature; the existing widget is not changed.

---

## 9. Security, audit and fraud controls

### 9.1 The threats and the control for each (detective until the server enforces)

| Threat (how a till is robbed [RT 9; RS 3.4]) | Prevent (client-side, a deterrent) | Detect (the evidence that cannot be switched off) | Where it shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| A bill rung, cash taken, the bill cancelled | cancel is a manager right with a reason, only while the shift is open, never Undo or Delete | `void` event with the approver; a cancelled shell keeps its number; the shift's cancelled count | Exceptions, Z report, Series integrity |
| **Sweethearting** (a discount or a cheap item for a friend) | caps by role; approval by a different person | discount rate per cashier against peers; the approver's name; the discount reasons | Discounts given, Exceptions |
| A price override or an open item to dodge a list | override and open item are rights; the MRP guard blocks upward | `override` and `openItem` events, the receipt says OPEN ITEM | Exceptions |
| A return without the bill or to a different tender | the original is required; the refund goes to the original tender by default; cash refunds capped | `return` and `refund` events; returns per cashier | Exceptions |
| The same bill returned at two counters (offline), or one gift code redeemed twice | a return of another terminal's bill is online-only; a redemption is an online check or capped offline (PD41) | **Over-returned bill** rows and the sync-time re-check | Exceptions, the Z close list, the Sync Mismatch Report |
| No-sale drawer opens | a right and an event; the pulse is a fixed profile constant | the drawer log and `noSale` events | Exceptions, Cash movements |
| **Fake UPI confirmation** (a spoofed app, a played-back tone, an edited screenshot) | a typed reference; `unconfirmed` until the statement or a provider confirms; the cashier's phone or soundbox is not proof | the unmatched-tenders list against the imported statement, at shift close and the next day | Unconfirmed tenders |
| A cashier engineers a zero variance at close | **blind count**; a maximum difference with a manager; a manager can close remotely | over and short vouchers per cashier and shift; repeated small shorts | Cash movements |
| Back-dating, or a clock set wrong | the date is the device clock, shown; skew warns and blocks (PD9) | `clock` events | Exceptions |
| A reprinted bill reused | a reprint prints DUPLICATE; the Nth reprint needs approval | `reprint` events with a count | Exceptions |
| Unrung sales (the goods leave with no bill) | for a restaurant, KOT-versus-bill; for retail, stock variance | the Oversell and stock-count reports; Physical Stock differences by reason | Reconciliation |
| Deleting evidence | no cashier delete; the event log is append-only and cannot be disabled (the audit trail rule for company customers [PY 3.8]) | the change log stamps every write with the operator | Audit Log (windowed by lane A) |
| A restored old backup or a shared terminal code | the single-instance lock; the Replace terminal wizard | duplicate numbers and gaps | Series integrity |
| **A cashier terminal's own token and PIN hashes** | none in v1 | none: this is the server's job (7.6 T1) | stated on screen |
| A stolen shop PC | the counter PIN is a fast hash for switching only; the full PIN protects approvals; the company passcode if the owner set one | none | n/a |

### 9.2 `Role.pos` and the approval protocol

```ts
export interface RolePos {                    // optional on Role; absent = the area-based rights of today
  lineDiscPct?: number; billDiscPct?: number; override?: 'none' | 'down' | 'any'; void?: boolean; refundCashMaxP?: number;
  noSale?: boolean; reprint?: boolean; closeShift?: boolean; openItem?: boolean; returnsWithoutBill?: boolean;
  seeExpected?: boolean; seeMargin?: boolean;                              // a blind cashier never sees expected cash; margin only where granted
  approve?: ('discount' | 'void' | 'refund' | 'override' | 'noSale' | 'reprint' | 'mrp' | 'clock' | 'creditLimit' | 'stock')[];
}
```
Built-in role templates (editable, never predefined-only, a complaint about Vyapar [RT 7]): **Cashier** (area `pos`, caps 5% and 3%, no override, no void), **Manager** (all of POS plus approve), **Owner** (full). **The approval protocol:** a cashier action that exceeds a limit opens the approval prompt; an eligible approver (holds `approve` for the kind) is picked from a list and enters their **full PIN** (the PBKDF2 check, 260 ms or more, once per rare approval); the approver **must differ from the requester** unless the requester holds the right (then the event is flagged `self`); the approval is `{ kind, by, reason, at, amountP }` on the voucher and an event; three wrong PINs lock the prompt for 60 seconds and write `approvalFail`. **The counter PIN** (a 4 to 6 digit PIN per operator per device, stored as a salted fast hash in the device's local storage, never synced) is used **only** to switch cashier on an unlocked terminal; five wrong tries need a manager's full PIN.

### 9.3 Audit trail, retention, privacy

- **Every event is an operator-stamped row** (`posEvents`) plus the change-log stamp on every write; **the log cannot be switched off**; a company's accounting software must keep an audit trail and an edit log that cannot be disabled for financial years from 1 April 2023 (the Companies (Accounts) Rules as the study read them through a search summary) [PY 3.8, [Ws]].
- **Retention**: GST records 72 months after the annual return's due date (section 36), a restorable electronic back-up (Rule 57), the Schedule H1 register three years; the data is the record (thermal paper fades), so a restore of a backup is rehearsed (never tested today) and nothing in POS prunes vouchers, events or `rx` [PY 3.8; PH 3.9; RT 5 row 18].
- **Privacy**: 3.7. The buyer block and the `rx` block are the two places a shopper's data lives on a voucher; both are classified for every reader (print, MCP, REST, backup, split).

### 9.4 What the screen says about itself

"Controls on this counter record who did what and flag it; they are not a lock against someone who controls this computer. Server-side limits are planned." (The honest-limit sentence of 7.6, shown in the POS settings and on the shift-open screen for the manager.) A fraud-control feature that claims more than it does is a liability.

---

## 10. The build: slices, exit gates, effort, order, lanes, assertions, recipes

**Rules for every slice** (from CLAUDE.md and the audit): new files wherever possible; a shared file is touched only by the lane that owns it, in a small additive hunk and only in its turn (10.3); every changed shared file gets its existing guards (the resave suites for `Voucher` and masters, the sidenav row table, the registry rule for chords, the MCP drift check); **with POS off the app is byte-for-byte what it is** (the `pos-identity` suite); a slice ends with its suites green, **every mutation of its lists killed**, `tsc` 0, the build green and the harness sweeps unchanged except for the new screens. Effort is **builder-days [J]**, tests and screenshots included, **before the review and fix round the project runs on every wave** (this document does not size it) and before the elapsed-time effect of the order in 10.3.

Nothing is promised in calendar terms; the elapsed time is set by the order of 10.3, not by the sum.

### 10.2 File ownership, one writer at a time

The shared files a POS touches and who is already in front of it (the audit's table, updated for the decisions here) [AU 14]. **Since `1d22e84` the `CF-` entries in the middle column are history (their hunks are on `main`); the tracking lane's `T-` entries and Back's optional B-6 are still ahead:**

| File | Writers already ahead | POS slice and hunk |
|---|---|---|
| `src/types.ts` | CF-0, then the tracking lane's T-0b | P-0c: the members of 2.5, 7.1, 9.2 |
| `src/data/db.ts` | the tracking lane's optional T-7b `version(9)` | P-0c: one additive version (numbered after or merged with it) **and the one-line `db.use(posDurability)` install**; later slices their own |
| `src/state/store.ts` | CF-0, CF-4a, T-4 (the `Screen` union once) | P-0a: `commitPosBill`; P-0c: `COLLECTIONS`, state arrays; P-1: the `Screen` union once |
| `src/ui/VoucherScreen.tsx` | Back's overlay edits, CF-2, CF-3, T-2a, CF-9c, T-2b | P-0c: the type picker hides POS types (revision 1's memo hunk is dropped: the POS root never mounts the voucher screen's memos); **the POS never edits it otherwise** |
| `src/sync/sync.ts`, `backend/src/{sync,pushRun,conflict,restPolicy}.js` | CF-6a, T-9a | P-0g1 and P-0g2: pull apply, the duplicate map and the outbox read; P-0c: `DATA_TABLES`, the `pos1` keep list and the immutability rule (7.5 layer 5), `REST_DENIED`; P-2: the terminal claim route (one function with mobile's, `numbering.js`); lane F: `posPolicy.js` |
| `electron/main.cjs`, `preload.cjs` | Back's optional B-6, T-7a | P-5p (the Electron upgrade), P-5 (the device flags and handlers), P-5j (the journal) and P-5o (the updater), each after or with them |
| `src/core/access.ts` | CF (`SCREEN_AREA` rows), T-4 | P-0c and P-4: `SCREEN_AREA`, `TABLE_AREA`, the `voucherDenial` and `writeDenial` rule |
| `src/ui/CompanyCreate.tsx` (the F11 literal) | the tracking lane's T-1 | P-0c: the `pos` key in the same hunk |
| `src/core/backupMigrate.ts`, `splitCompany.ts`, `companyAlter.ts` | CF-0, T-0b | P-0c: schema 5, the carry, the alter |
| `src/core/inventory.ts`, `valuation.ts` (hash-locked) | CF-9a to CF-9d, T-0b, T-3 | P-0c: `noStock`; P-4b: `costValue` |
| `src/core/gstr1.ts` (hash-locked) | none | P-0g3: a month window; P-0c: `originalInvoice` reads `pos.originalId`; P-7a: `docsIssued` skips `docs: 'not-issued'` types |
| `src/core/gstRates.ts`, `gstNil.ts`, `gstr3b.ts` (hash-locked) | none | P-7a only |
| `src/core/upi.ts`, `bankStatement.ts`, `brsMatch.ts` | none | P-2: the optional link fields (bytes unchanged when none are set), UTR matching |

| `src/ui/router.tsx`, `MenuScreen.tsx`, `modern/CommandPalette.tsx`, `overlays.tsx`, `navTree.ts` + `sidenav.assert.ts`, `quickCreate.ts`, `shortcutRegistry.ts`, `reportsCenter/catalogue.ts`, `dash/widgets/registry.ts`, `settings/tabs.ts` | CF-4a once, T-4 once | P-1 once, P-9 once |
| `mcp/engine-lock.json`, `mcp/src/data/fields.ts` | every lane that edits a locked file | last, by whoever edits a locked file (lane M) |

**Lanes** (the audit's letters kept so the sibling documents still read): A hot path (P-0a, POS-only, and P-0g1 to P-0g4, global); B the pure engine (P-0b1 and P-0b2, and the pure halves of P-3, P-4, P-6, P-7); C contracts (P-0c); D the counter UI (P-1 to P-4); E devices and print (P-5); F server and rights; G reports (P-9); H the vertical packs (P-6, P-7, P-8); M the MCP seam. **One person owns the integration lane for the registration hunks** in `store.ts`, `types.ts`, `db.ts`, `access.ts` and the router files [AU 14].

**The lock map (PD33, RV28).** A file in a lock needs that package's drift run and re-lock after an edit. Three packages matter: the root `tsc` and `assert` (no lock), `mcp/engine-lock.json` (52 files [C]) and the mobile `engines/engine-lock.json` (planned: the inputs of `engines/entry.ts`, mobile-app-spec 2.3). A slice that edits a row with a "yes" names the checks in its exit gate. **The MCP lock is already stale:** 9 of its 52 files changed in `1d22e84` and the re-lock was not done [C CLAUDE.md Phase 30 GOTCHAs: `types.ts`, `backupMigrate`, `integrity`, `inventory`, `openingStock`, `splitCompany`, `syncGuard`, `valuation`, `voucherCancel`], so `cd mcp && npm run drift` is red before any POS edit; the first POS slice that touches a locked file starts by getting that to green (lane M), not by adding to the pile.

| File the POS edits | MCP lock | Mobile entry | POS slice |
|---|---|---|---|
| `src/types.ts` | yes | yes (an input of the type declarations) | P-0c |
| `src/core/backupMigrate.ts`, `src/core/splitCompany.ts` | yes | no | P-0c |
| `src/core/gstr1.ts`, `gstr3b.ts`, `gstNil.ts` | yes | no | P-0g3 and P-0c (gstr1), P-7a |
| `src/core/gstRates.ts` | yes | yes | P-7a |
| `src/core/inventory.ts`, `valuation.ts` | yes | no | P-0c (`noStock`), P-4b (`costValue`) |
| `src/core/voucherCancel.ts` | yes | yes | P-4 |
| `src/core/syncGuard.ts` | yes | no | P-0g1, only if the incremental map lives there |
| `src/core/vchNumbering.ts`, `voucherNumberFloor.ts` | yes | `voucherNumberFloor` only | none planned (the claim batching is in `src/sync/numbering.ts` and the server) |
| `src/core/upi.ts` | no | yes | P-2 (optional link fields, bytes unchanged when unset) |
| `src/core/access.ts` | no | no | P-0c, P-4 |
| `src/data/defaults.ts` | yes | no | none (avoid adding a default ledger) |

### 10.3 The order against everything else

**Facts as read on 2026-10-25.** Parity wave 3 is committed (`2cee555`) and deployed (`687c062`); the task text that asked for this spec said the main tree was gating about 160 uncommitted paths, but the tree I read at `5aee765` shows only research documents modified, so I wrote against `HEAD`. The custom-fields, variants and Back build is **built and gated in `.wt-cf`, then fast-forwarded into `main` as `1d22e84` on 2026-10-25 and NOT deployed** (the VM still runs `687c062`; `main` is now `d959028`, one docs commit later) [C `git log`, CLAUDE.md], with a backend deploy that must precede the SPA [CF notes 0, 6]. The tracking lane's engines are merged and unwired; **T-0b's gate, CF-9a on a rebased `main`, is met by `1d22e84`** [C CLAUDE.md], and on `VoucherScreen.tsx` its order is Back's overlays, CF-2, CF-3, T-2a, CF-9c, T-2b [TS 15.1, 15.3]. The report grid (`core/listQuery.ts`) is not on `main`: a `report-grid` worktree (`.wt-grid`, cut at `1d22e84`, with untracked goldens named `grid.big.daybook` and `grid.big.bills.*`) is building it and was not read, so P-9a coordinates with it instead of building a second windowing; **the mobile spec exists** (`docs/design/mobile-app-spec.md`, 25 Oct 20:59) with its own backend lanes BE-0 to BE-8 and its own engine lock; the MCP bundle locks 52 files and **is already stale** (9 of them changed in `1d22e84` and the re-lock was not done [C CLAUDE.md Phase 30 GOTCHAs]).

```
 NOW (new files, no collision; a worktree cut from main: .wt-pos0)
   P-0b1 pure engine  ||  P-5 pure half (escposLang, receiptJob, the bridge suite)  ||  P-9 pure report engines
   ||  S-dur (strict-commit cost; the power-pull bench)  ||  S-env (heap and cold start at the ceiling C on a 4 GB PC: it sets C)
   ||  the bench spike: a 58 mm and an 80 mm printer, Chrome and the packaged Electron, a drawer pulse (beside the tracking lane's label spike T-6s)
   ||  P-5p Electron upgrade (its own branch; it touches package.json and main.cjs, so it is sequenced with Back's B-6 and T-7a as agreed in 11.1 Q11)
   ||  TLS and a hostname (the owner's DNS action), an e-mail provider decision, the code-signing certificate (11.1)
   ||  the CA, advocate and pharmacist sign-offs of 11.2 begin (their lead time is the long pole of P-3b, P-3c, P-6 and P-7)
   ||  Q2's numbers and the screening sheet of 1.3.1 (they decide the envelope before P-1)
 -- GATE: met. `main` contains the CF branch since `1d22e84`; the POS worktree `.wt-pos` is cut from `main` (11.1 Q10) --
 PHASE 1  P-0c contracts (one Dexie bump, one editor of db.ts)  ->  P-0a POS hot path  ->  P-1 counter  ||  P-1i importer  ||  P-0b2 (`bulkPlan.ts` is on `main`)
          || lane F tier 1 and 2 after the CF backend deploy, with the tracking lane's T-9a (same files) and on top of the mobile spec's BE-2
          || P-0g1 to P-0g4 (global lane A): after the CF and tracking merges, first for a POS company only, each with its equivalence suite; earlier only if S-env says the pilot needs them
 PHASE 2  P-2 payments, day end, receipts, claim, settlement  ->  P-3a promotions  ->  P-4 returns, approvals  ->  P-4b  ||  P-2d roll writer
          ||  P-5 wiring (after T-7a and Back's B-6 on main.cjs), P-5j, P-5o  ||  P-9 screens, P-9a volume views
          =>  PILOT: one store (a barcoded general store inside the envelope), one to three counters, Windows 10 or 11, a real Tally company (10.4)
 PHASE 3  P-3b bundle and combo (after the owner's CA)  ||  P-3c loyalty, store credit, gift vouchers (after the CA)
          P-2b tracked items at the counter (needs T-0b and T-2)  ->  P-6 pharmacy (needs P-2b, the tracking lane's T-2 and the RV1 fix)
          ||  P-7 restaurant counter service (needs P-7a and the CA); swap rule: whichever of P-6 and P-7 has its dependencies first
 PHASE 4  P-8 aggregators (needs TLS and the partner applications)  ||  dine-in tables, captain devices and the outlet hub (v2)
 LATER    lane S or a hybrid, only if the owner's Q2 numbers say so; P-2c seed from backup, only if the lane-A pull is too slow; an Android shell with the mobile track
```

**Why the pure slices can start now:** they import only `src/core` engines (read-only) and the tracking lane's merged files; their only shared dependency is `src/trackTypes.ts` (new, types only) and the `types.ts` members, which a new `src/core/pos/types.ts` carries until P-0c re-exports them. **Why P-0c and P-0a wait:** `types.ts`, `db.ts`, `store.ts`, `sync.ts` and `VoucherScreen.tsx` are one-writer files for three other builds; a stale full-file write silently drops another builder's edit [TS 15.3]. **A rule for the voucher screen:** the POS builds nothing in it except the type-picker filter.

### 10.4 Pilot-ready, defined

Pilot-ready (the end of Phase 2) means: (1) a single scanned item with exact cash is **two actions** with `exactCashOnKey` on (three with it off), a UPI bill is **three** (scan, F3, Enter), a 10-line basket with a split tender is a scan per line plus about five keys, **and the select-then-scan, select-then-PLU and touch-only runs of 1.5 pass**; Accept to spool is 300 ms or under at 250,000 vouchers **including the strict commit**, with no per-scan growth; (2) GSTR-1, 3B, HSN and Table 13 equal the Sales-screen build for 10,000 baskets (the value-slab rule, the bill-discount split and an MRP basket each have a golden); (3) **a killed process mid-bill returns the cart; a real power pull on the bench kit in the middle of a shift loses no saved bill and issues no number twice (a process kill proves nothing: it does not lose OS-buffered data); a backup is restored on a scratch machine that never signs in and its totals equal the live company's (never over a live POS company); a replacement terminal joins a 250,000-voucher test tenant by pull and its first number is above the issue ledger's maximum, with nothing re-pushed, un-cancelled or deleted**; (4) a shift closes with a blind count, a difference above the limit waits for a manager and the Z report ties to the cash ledger; (5) every void, discount over cap, override, refund, no-sale and reprint is an event with operator, terminal and time and the exceptions report shows them per cashier; (6) **a real 58 mm and a real 80 mm printer, a USB scanner, a drawer and a scale label were used by a person, on a packaged and signed build, on Windows 10 and 11** (nothing in any study was); (7) **one pilot retailer with a real Tally company**: alias barcodes and masters imported, **a real store's masters (about 4,000 items) loaded in under half a day**, a week of real bills, the CA's GSTR-1 compared with their usual one [RT 10.5]; (8) **the envelope holds**: renderer heap, cold start and the report gates of 2.15 pass at the ceiling C on the 4 GB test PC and the low-end PC, and the pilot customer was screened against 1.3.1 (turnover against Rs 5 crore, B2B share, bills a day); (9) **a support bundle can be exported and a restore has been run** (6.8). The test for "held must not mean forgotten": a shift cannot close with an open hold until it is discarded or carried.

### 10.5 The assertion plan

**Suites** (`scripts/assert/pos-*.assert.ts`, each with a mutation list in `scripts/assert/mutations/<suite>.mjs`; **every mutation must make the suite fail, and a stale pattern is a failure; the runner is run on the unmutated tree first** [C CLAUDE.md MCP gotchas]):

| Suite | What it pins |
|---|---|
| `pos-money` | **`allocateP` equals an exact BigInt largest remainder (ties by index) over a million random inputs**; `allocateP` and `gst.ts allocate` agree on the sum, never differ by more than one paisa in any part, and agree except at flagged near-ties (4 in a million on the seeded run of Appendix B.6); half-up rounding; paise to rupee conversion exact |
| `pos-pricing` | exclusive and inclusive splits on the vectors of Appendix B; the residual over T = 1 to 200,000 at every slab: **0 to 2 paise without cess and 0 to 3 with it** (the 28% + 22%, 28% + 60% and 18% + 1% vectors of Appendix B.5); discount allocation before tax; the rate rule's three bases and its tier edges, **its off-by-default refusal, and the Rs 2,626 to Rs 2,950 band with Rs 2,799 and Rs 2,899 refused with the three results shown**; the MRP guard; the round-off policy (down only with an MRP item) |
| `pos-bill` | every case of 2.4 builds the right vouchers; every voucher balances; `voucherGstSummary` reads back what was posted; the stamp is complete; S1 versus S2 selection; the clearing debtor nets to zero |
| `pos-reconcile` | **the golden**: 10,000 random baskets and tender mixes, GSTR-1 (B2CS, B2B, HSN, Table 13), GSTR-3B 3.1 and the trial balance equal the Sales-screen builder (exclusive; **needs a base that contains `bulkPlan.ts`, which `main` has since `1d22e84`**) or the independent integer oracle (inclusive; always) |
| `pos-tender` | the state machine (every illegal transition refused); the PAN sentinel (typed reference, narration and note fields only; an IMEI or a valid-Luhn EAN in a scan target or serial field is accepted, RV39); over-tender; the cash cap (**a single cash tender at or above the cap needs a manager for any customer**, RV27); the dated discount table vectors (Rs 2,000 pays 0, Rs 3,000 pays Rs 12, Rs 50,000 pays Rs 200, Rs 75,000 and Rs 1,00,000 pay Rs 300) [PY 1.2, M] |
| `pos-shift` | open, close, blind count, over and short, drop, remote close, the Z ties to the paisa |
| `pos-returns` | returnable quantity; per-unit returns sum to the sale; a whole-bill return copies its legs; the cut-off as data; exchange atomicity; the return at issue cost (P-4b); a serial return needs its own bill; **a rate change between sale and return, and a tier crossed by a discount, credit at the original's rate; GSTR-1 rows by rate net to zero for a full return; a cash refund credits the refunding terminal's own cash ledger; the same bill returned on two terminals raises an Over-returned bill row** (RV4, RV5, RV19) |
| `pos-cancel` | the composite shell; allowed only while the shift is open; a confirmed UPI payment must be refunded first; the number is kept |
| `pos-numbering` | the template validator on the budget table (15 accepted, 19 refused), Monthly without `{mm}` refused, upper case, overflow at width plus one, **`posSeries.next` is the maximum of the counter, a window scan, `numberFloor` and the burned numbers, and the counter equals a scan after a split; POS types are refused by Bulk Entry, import, REST, MCP and `writeDenial`; the batched claim (at most 50) returns `taken` for a clone and lifts a stale counter; no gap-fill shells exist** (RV14, RV6) |
| `pos-lookup` | the four stages in order; an unknown code is said and never appended; ranking; the debounced results; an exact code in under 50 ms at 30,000 items |
| `pos-hold` | the draft is the cart on every change; recall re-prices and flags; holds are listed at close |
| `pos-promos`, `pos-loyalty` | each shape, priority, exclusivity, windows, budgets; a bundle priced once; the events fold, expiry first in first out, reversal on cancel |
| `pos-receipt` | golden receipts for five sample bills at 58 and 80 mm in text and raster; a 40-line text receipt under 4 KB; DUPLICATE and the reprint count; the title logic (tax invoice, bill of supply, invoice-cum-bill, credit note, provisional, training) |
| `pos-bridge` | against a fake `electron`: hostile jobs, ceilings, one job at a time, a structured job only, no host or path from the renderer, the drawer rate limit and fixed pulse, the emitter allow-list, the renderer builder equals the CJS validator on one corpus |
| `pos-events`, `pos-rights` | append-only; cannot be disabled; caps by role; the approval protocol (the approver differs from the requester, a lockout after three wrong PINs, the counter PIN's own lockout) |
| `pos-sync` | the conflict table of 7.4 table by table; the deterministic customer id; a remote close against a concurrent commit; the `pos1` keep rule |
| `pos-identity` | **with the feature off** (and no POS data): `Company` has no `pos`; the five tables are empty and absent from the backup; backup schema unchanged; the menu rows, quick-create list and Settings tabs are deep-equal; the Go To golden of 103 queries is unchanged; the F11 object has no `pos` key; the dispatcher hands every handler the same event object; the Sales save's `Voucher` literal is unchanged; the Sales screen's type picker is the same list |
| `pos-purity` | `core/pos` imports no DOM, Dexie, React or store (**a scan that must prove it examined files**), contains no literal tax rate, no `* rate / 200` and no `round2` over tax |
| `pos-durable` | the commit transaction asks for strict durability through the middleware and no other transaction does; nothing prints before the commit's promise resolves; the high-water mark is in the same transaction; the journal reconciliation lists a printed number IndexedDB lacks (against a fake `electron`); a **bench test** (not a suite) pulls the power |
| `pos-index` | the hooks fire for put, bulkPut, bulkDelete, a restore, an undo, a pull apply, Bulk Entry, a cancel and a split; the index equals the engines after each; live order equals reloaded order over random arrival orders including cross-type ties; the oracle's expected cash equals the index's on clean books and overrules it when they differ |
| `pos-settle` | every Collections ledger returns to zero after a simulated statement cycle; gross, discount, GST and net must add up; the matcher matches the one Dr Bank entry one-to-one; a write-off needs an approver and a reason; an unmatched tender after the wait appears on the close list |
| `pos-claim` | the batched claim: at most 50, idempotent, a clone gets `taken`, the series answer lifts a stale counter, a replacement terminal starts above the issue ledger's maximum and the declared last number; one server function serves the mobile route and the terminal route |
| `pos-envelope` | the ceiling banner at 80% and the red item at C never block a bill; the Period roll writes only the later half, refuses when a voucher is dated on or after S, carries `numberFloor`, the openings tie to the engine, a Yearly series continues with no repeated number |
| `pos-importer` | barcodes, aliases, MRP, prices, pack, short code, opening stock by godown and customer openings load with a preview; duplicate barcodes are listed, never merged; a hostile sheet (the Phase 29 shapes) is capped |
| `pos-global-golden` | **for a company without POS, byte-equal to HEAD:** GSTR-1 and Table 13 JSON on the sample and a 50,000-voucher book for every month; the Audit Log and Cancelled register row sets with "load more"; a backup the HEAD restore reads; pull-apply semantics (a pull never overwrites an unsent edit; Split Company Data's held pulls); the `syncconflict` and pull suites unchanged |
| `pos-restore-guard` | a restore that would delete or replace a voucher carrying `pos` is refused on the client and the server; a delete of a POS voucher carrying a `restoreId` is refused; a cancelled bill stays cancelled |
| `voucher-resave`, `master-resave`, `syncconflict`, `backupmigrate`, `security-access`, `sidenav`, `reportscenter`, `quickcreate` | **extended** with the new keys, tables, rows and rights |
| Restaurant (`pos-kot`, `pos-charges`, `pos-eco95`, `pos-menu`, `pos-aggregator`) | the event fold and immutability; a transfer; a split apportioned in paise; charges off by default and the attestation; the ECO95 reporting cases **including a mixed aggregator order split by line class (Circular 167 Sl. 8 to 11, Circular 164 s.4)**; the channel validator; idempotent intake and the late-orders list |
| Pharmacy (`pos-rx`, `pos-schedule`, `pos-fefo`, `pos-loose`, `pos-freeqty`, `pos-subst`) | the gate on the line snapshot; the registers over vouchers; the H1 register never pruned; the dated list and the derivation (a future `effectiveFrom` is not in force before its date); the no-stop pick; strip and loose arithmetic and the label rule; the net-rate free quantity; the neutral substitute order, disabled on a scheduled line |

**Performance rows** (added to `scripts/perf`, reference rows first and **gated once measured on a quiet machine**; the audit measured at load 14 to 20, so every figure there is an upper bound for that machine and a lower bound for a low-end PC [AU 3]): `pos.scan` (scan to line), `pos.accept` (Accept to spool, **including the strict commit**), `pos.series`, `pos.search30k`, `pos.z` (a Z close for 3,000 bills), `pos.gstr1.month`, `pos.backup`, `pos.pull100` (the cart stays responsive), **`pos.pull.dupcheck`, `pos.open` (company open to first scan), `pos.heap`, `pos.dayview` (the volume views and registers of 2.11), and the existing Balance Sheet, Stock Summary and default dashboard rows at C**; each at 120,000, 250,000 and 500,000 synthetic POS bills (the audit's shape: 1 to 8 lines, mean 4.13) **and again with a 3 to 5 times slowdown factor for a low-end PC**; targets in 2.11. No figure above 500,000 bills is claimed [AU 3.2].

**Hostile and degenerate inputs** (each a named test): quantity 1e9, NaN, negative, ten decimals; price 0, negative, 1e12; a 1,000-line cart; 300-character names; zero-width and bidirectional characters in names and narration; a 13 to 19 digit PAN-like string in every typed field; a reference with CRLF or a formula prefix (`=`, `+`, `-`, `@`) through `csvOut`; a duplicate `billId` (commit is idempotent); a commit interrupted after the Sales write (fault injection: none or all); a clock jump back a day; two terminals with one code; a template that can overflow 16 characters; a scanner burst of 13 and 15 characters at 3 ms spacing, 1,000 in a row, against a 30,000-item master and a 120,000-voucher book; `999999*`; a discount percent over 100 or negative; a tender above the balance; 200 tenders; 10,000 drafts; an `rx` image of 50 MB (refused); an address of 20,000 characters; a phone with letters; a promotion whose combo references itself [C CLAUDE.md Phase 24 and 29: cap length before any pattern test, scan linearly].

**Race simulations** (deterministic, over the real writer and a fake sync; the harness runs both orders of every pair): two terminals offline sell the last unit (the Oversell report lists both bills); the same serial on two terminals (the tracking lane's mismatch row); two terminals with one code (duplicate numbers); a replaced terminal continues the series; a pull page applied while a cart is open; a crash after Accept and before print (the queue is restored); a power cut inside the transaction (none or all of the Sales and the Receipt) **and a power cut after it completes (a bench test, 10.4 item 3)**; two windows on one machine (the single-instance lock and `ensureFresh`); a remote close against a concurrent commit; a shift closed with unsynced bills; the same phone added on two terminals; **the same bill returned on two offline terminals; one gift code redeemed on two; a restore over a live POS company (refused); a replaced terminal with a lost range; a claim answered `taken`; a stale writer's put to a POS voucher (refused)**.

### 10.6 Harness recipes (`scripts/shots/recipes/pos-*.mjs`; real keys, light and dark, compact and comfortable, 150% text size, 1280 x 800 and 1024 x 768 with touch events; never Accept on a live company)

`pos-counter-empty`, `pos-scan-five`, `pos-select-then-scan`, `pos-select-then-plu`, `pos-touch-only`, `pos-tender-cash-exact`, `pos-tender-split`, `pos-tender-upi-qr`, `pos-tender-upi-flow`, `pos-customer-find`, `pos-customer-add-consent`, `pos-discount-approval`, `pos-hold-recall`, `pos-return`, `pos-exchange`, `pos-cancel-bill`, `pos-shift-open`, `pos-shift-close-blind`, `pos-z-report`, `pos-offline-banner`, `pos-unknown-code`, `pos-error-save-failed`, `pos-print-failed`, `pos-training`, `pos-add-terminal-wizard`, `pos-replace-terminal`, `pos-year-roll`, `pos-period-roll`, `pos-settle-tenders`, `pos-lost-range`, `pos-einvoice-handoff`, `pos-importer`, `pos-diagnostics`, `pos-ceiling-banner`, `pos-settings-tab`, `pos-reports-*` (one per report), `pos-widgets`, plus flows (a real-key walk of Flow A, a killed-process cart return) and the **text-drawn-over-text and text-cut-off probes** compared with the 100% layout at 125% and 150% (the Phase 27 lesson that overflow alone does not prove a size fits). Restaurant and pharmacy recipes (`pos-table-map`, `pos-kot-print`, `pos-split-bill`, `pos-rx-gate`, `pos-fefo-pick`, `pos-loose-label`, `pos-register`) ship with their slices.

### 10.7 Where this spec departs from its inputs (so two documents never disagree silently)

1. **S2's shape.** The audit's S2 made the Sales voucher's party the Cash ledger and the Receipt a transfer (Dr UPI, Cr Cash) [AU 2.3]; I use a **Walk-in debtor as the party and one Receipt with a Dr line per tender** (2.4 case 3): the cash ledger then equals the cash tenders exactly, a Receipt keeps its ordinary meaning (Dr cash or bank, Cr party), a bill with any number of tenders costs one Sales and one Receipt, and the debtor nets to zero as a testable invariant.
2. **Numbering** was left to lanes A and C as an open decision [AU 4, 15 item 4]; decided here: device-owned (PD8).
3. **Order of the verticals.** The retail study listed pharmacy "per its own doc" [RT D6] and the restaurant study put a hub and captain tablets in v2; the order here (retail, pharmacy, restaurant) with a swap rule is new.
4. **The bridge name**: the audit's `deviceBridge.cjs`, the tracking spec's `labelBridge.cjs`, resolved as `deviceBridge.cjs` (PD22).
5. **Effort**: the retail study's weeks against the audit's lane days (10.1).
6. **P-1 does not wait for the scan layer**, which the audit's finding 15 and the tracking spec's order make a precondition of the scan budget [AU 1.3]; the POS meets the budget by construction (an uncontrolled field, no bare-letter hotkey, debounced results) and adopts T-5b when it lands (PD27).
7. **The restaurant study** proposed `Voucher.pos` including a restaurant `order`; here the order table and events are P-7's and the voucher carries only a reference.
8. **PD29 reversed.** Revision 1 offered a backup file plus delta as the way to onboard a terminal; the restore is a replace (7.4), so the ordinary pull is the path and a seed import is optional.
9. **PD8 aligned with the mobile spec** (a batched claim, the enrolment continuation, `taken` as the clone flag); the gap-fill shells of revision 1 are removed (2.4 case 18).
10. **Lane A is split** into POS-only P-0a and global P-0g1 to P-0g4 with HEAD goldens; the voucher-screen memo hunk is dropped.
11. **New in revision 2:** strict durability (PD39), the envelope and Period roll (PD38), the settlement voucher (PD42), the running-index choke point (PD43), operations (PD44), the importer (PD45), the cross-terminal return fold (PD41), spikes S-dur and S-env.
12. **Changed defaults:** the rate rule ships off; an e-invoice-liable GSTIN bill is a hand-off, not a dead end; a licence or FSSAI number is required to enable a mode, not print-if-entered; a single cash tender is checked; restaurant v1 is sold as counter service; promotions move into Phase 2 (P-3a) with loyalty after the CA (P-3c).

## 11. Questions that genuinely need the owner or an adviser

### 11.1 For the owner (each with the default taken so work can proceed)

| # | Question | Default taken | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Which vertical first?** | Retail (P-1 to P-5), then pharmacy, then restaurant, with the swap rule of PD1 | decides who the first pilot is |
| 2 | **Voucher per bill or summary posting?** Please give, for the target customers, **bills a day per outlet and per GSTIN, annual turnover (against Rs 5 crore for e-invoice, Rs 1.5 crore for composition and Rs 50 crore for the digital-acceptance duty), the B2B share, the credit share and the share under Rs 200** | voucher per bill; summary posting not built; **a customer above about 1,500 bills a day per GSTIN is not sold the POS in v1 (PD38)** | the only inputs that could change PD3 [AU 15 item 1] |
| 3 | **Which hardware do the target customers own?** Printers (58 or 80 mm, make and model, USB, serial, LAN or Bluetooth), drawers, scanners (1-D or 2-D), scales, card machines and soundboxes; and **who buys the bench kit** of 6.6 | ESC/POS printers only; two or three certified models after the bench; the bench kit bought by Comhard | the bridge's first real run is a gate, and nothing in any study touched hardware [PY 5.5 items 5, 15] |
| 4 | **Aggregator priority.** Is restaurant mode and the aggregator work wanted this year, and should the **partner applications and the middleware conversation start now** (calendars are the long pole)? | yes, start now; manual aggregator entry in v1; middleware in P-8 | [RS 3.7, D3] |
| 5 | **Payment partner(s) and the commercial model.** Which PSP for a dynamic QR with a confirmation and which acquirer for an integrated terminal (Razorpay, PhonePe, Paytm, Cashfree, Pine Labs were read from public docs only; none was contacted)? Referral, never custody | the manual provider and a provider-neutral interface first; one PSP QR and one terminal chosen after two conversations | decides commission, TLS urgency and legal exposure [PY 5.5 item 3] |
| 6 | **Pricing and the seat model** | one published price per company, no per-terminal or per-user fee, every counter feature included, add-ons only for third-party costs (WhatsApp messages, a payment partner); **a fair-use envelope (the ceiling C and about 1,500 bills a day per GSTIN) and a `change_log` retention rule scheduled before launch volume: a terminal at 800 bills a day writes about 0.69 GB a year, so fifty three-counter stores add about 100 GB a year to one VM** | the field's complaints are add-on staircases and renewals [RT 7, 10.1] |
| 7 | **Pilot customers.** How many of the owner's Tally customers are retailers, chemists or restaurants, what do they use at the counter, and would two or three pilot? | two or three retailers with a Tally company, one chemist, one counter-service restaurant | the exit gate needs a real Tally company [RT 11.2] |
| 8 | **TLS, a hostname, an e-mail provider.** When? | start now, in parallel | gates invites, PSP callbacks, hosted bills, the camera, aggregator webhooks, the Android shell [PY 5.4] |
| 9 | **Device-owned numbering (PD8)** or cloud numbering for POS series | device-owned with the **batched claim** (one function with the mobile track), the Replace terminal wizard and the Data Check | the 4-second stall and claim storm against the replacement procedure |
| 10 | **The CF merge and the order on shared files.** The custom-fields, variants and Back work is on `main` (`1d22e84`) and not deployed. Does its backend deploy (JavaScript only, no migration) go before any POS backend change, and may the POS build in a worktree `.wt-pos` cut from `main`? | yes to both; the POS rebases onto `main` at the end | P-0c, P-0a and P-1 no longer wait on the merge, only on the deploy order [AU 15 item 11] |
| 11 | **One bridge and one Dexie editor.** Adopt `deviceBridge.cjs` as the shared bridge; agree one Dexie version number with the tracking lane's T-7b; who runs the printed spike and on which printers | as PD18 and PD22; **the tracking spec's T-7a is amended to `deviceBridge.cjs` before either slice starts**; the owner supplies the printers and PCs | two names and two version numbers collide otherwise |
| 12 | **Rights and the server.** Is v1 acceptable as **detective** ("records who did what; not a lock"), with member accounts, invites and server enforcement scheduled after the pilot? | yes, and the screen says so | a cashier terminal holds a tenant-wide token and every PIN hash [7.6] |
| 13 | **Loyalty and promotions in v1?** | promotions in v1 (P-3a); loyalty, store credit and gift vouchers not v1 (P-3c, after the CA); bundle and combo off until the CA attests (P-3b) | the DPDP duties and the GST of points are unsettled [RT 4.8] |
| 14 | **Service charge posture** | off by default; turning it on needs the owner's written attestation, a dated record | the CCPA's Chaayos order; a vendor default is a liability [RS 4.4] |
| 15 | **Stock policy at the counter** | allow below zero silently and report | a prompt per bill is wrong at a counter [AU 6] |

| 17 | **Platform order** | Windows desktop first; Android after HTTPS and the mobile track | [PY 5.5 item 1] |

| 19 | **Open item, returns without a bill, training mode** | open item for a manager role; no-bill returns as store credit only with a right; training mode on | [RT 4.3, 4.6] |
| 20 | **Who owns the support** the field's top complaint is about: is the partner channel ready to answer a counter during a rush? | the reseller channel plus in-app diagnostics, a status line and a restore path | a business decision no feature settles [RT 7, PH 7 item 6] |
| 21 | **The envelope.** Is v1 sold only to a GSTIN of about 1,500 bills a day or fewer (all counters together), with a Period roll at a month start when a company passes 80% of its ceiling? | yes; a customer above is not sold the POS in v1 and lane S or a hybrid is designed from Q2's numbers | PD38; a three-counter store at the audit's 800 a day each is 2,400 a day |
| 22 | **The runtime floor and the Electron upgrade.** 64-bit Windows 10 or 11, 4 GB (8 GB recommended), an SSD recommended; Electron upgraded before the packaging gate | yes | PD23; Electron 31 is outside the support policy |
| 23 | **Power.** Do the target counters have a UPS? Who supplies the PC and a switchable socket for the power-pull bench? | the owner supplies the bench PC and socket; a UPS is advised in the pitch, not required | PD39; power cuts are the named condition |

Nobody here has seen a real export file | saved column mappings for Excel and CSV first; a named product's mapping only after the partner supplies a real file | PD45; no export format was verified |

### 11.2 For a CA, an advocate and a pharmacist, before each vertical ships

**CA, retail (before P-1's pilot and P-3b, P-3c):** the **basis of "sale value" for the Rs 2,500 apparel test** (tax-exclusive? after discount? per piece on a multi-piece invoice? and the three bases of 2.3 step 4); the **bill-level discount split** across lines; **buy-one-get-one as a combo priced once**; GST on **gift vouchers, store credit and loyalty points** (the 2025 omission of sections 12(4) and 13(4); Circular 243/37/2024 as read through commentary); **round-off against Rule 18(2)** and whether it is judged per pack or per bill; an **invoice-cum-bill of supply** as the title of a mixed basket (Rule 46A); **composition counters** and the Rs 1.5 crore lapse; **the section 34(2) cut-off** (`SCHEMA?`: the audit's copy of the Act says September, the payments study reports 30 November from 1 Oct 2022); the Rs 200 consolidated invoice (not offered); the **Income-tax Act 2025 section numbers** for the cash cap and the electronic-modes duty; whether an **electronic bill** by WhatsApp satisfies Rule 48's copy marks and Rule 57's record duty; **the credit note's tax at the original's rate** (2.6), including the original's rate for a line that crossed a tier by a discount; **the apparel basis in the band between Rs 2,626 and Rs 2,950** (2.3 step 4); **the single-bill cash limit** (all three limbs of the cash-receipt section as commentary reads them, whether a PAN must be quoted on a large cash sale, the Income-tax Act 2025 section number); **the lost-bills adjustment and Table 13**: how a range of issued documents whose records were lost is filed (2.4 case 18); **a mid-year Period roll**: GSTR-1, 3B and Table 13 per company by month, GSTR-9 across two companies, and the turnover aggregates the split does not carry (2.15); **GST on the merchant discount** and the input credit on it (2.4 case 16); **round-off per pack or per bill** (PD7, with the estimated cost shown to the owner); whether an **e-invoice-liable seller's** counter hand-off (an Optional Sales voucher completed in the back office) meets the invoice-issue time limits [PY 5.6; RT 11.2].
**CA, restaurant (before P-7):** the list in [RS 4.10]: a voluntary service charge and tips, packaging and delivery at the supply's rate, a composition restaurant through an aggregator, the current composition limit, how a 5%-no-credit restaurant records input GST and the GST on aggregator commission, mixed bills, **the current table for 9(5) supplies** (Table 14(b) and 3.1.1(ii) per search summaries), **the mixed aggregator order** (restaurant service plus packaged goods plus manufactured ice cream: who invoices what, the operator's TCS, and where each line is reported, Circular 167 Sl. 8 to 11 and Circular 164 s.4), **whether the operator's invoices count in the restaurant's Table 13**, the ice-cream and packaged-goods rates after 22 Sep 2025 (Circular 164 read 18% for ice cream in 2021; the rate in force is dated data, not read here), comps, wastage and staff meals, and whether the dated defaults satisfy the CCPA's reading.
**Advocate, pharmacy (before P-6):** whether a software-printed register or memo satisfies Rule 65(3) and 65(4) given the option declared at licence time; **several per-terminal register series** for one shop; whether an e-prescription satisfies 65(10); the NDPS obligations of a retailer beyond Rule 65; the chemist's side of any commission; whether a reminder or a message naming a scheduled drug is an advertisement under the draft Rule 65(22) when final; the retailer's duty on a batch named in a CDSCO alert; the **wording lists of 5.8**; **the pregabalin effective date** (a 13 May or a 20 May Gazette, plus 180 days); the draft Rule 65(2A) CCTV rule and what a shop must do if it is finalised; whether the endorsement of the prescription (65(11)(c)) can be printed by the software or must be written by hand.
**Pharmacist and advocate:** the **dated schedule list** as shipped (Schedule H and H1 as of its date, pregabalin's effective date from the Gazette).
**Advocate, privacy and payments (before any phone number or payment partner):** the DPDP notice and consent wording, a processing agreement per shop, retention against erasure, a breach plan; whether showing a PSP's QR or **referring** a PSP makes Comhard a payment aggregator or imposes disclosures (the RBI's 2025 Directions), and the no-pass-through wording for any "convenience fee"; WhatsApp opt-in adequacy for "gave a number for a bill" and the DLT category of a bill SMS [PY 5.6].
