## Appendix B. Worked vectors [M] (an integer-arithmetic script run in the session scratchpad; not committed; the suites pin these)

**B.1 The residual of an inclusive bucket.** For every inclusive total T from 1 to 200,000 paise and each slab tested (0.25, 3, 5, 12, 18, 28 and 40 per cent, no cess), the largest base B with B + CGST(B) + SGST(B) <= T leaves `T - (B + CGST + SGST)` of 0, 1 or 2 paise and never 3. Counts of each (of 200,000): 0.25%: 199,502 / 249 / 249; 3%: 194,174 / 2,913 / 2,913; 5%: 190,476 / 4,762 / 4,762; 12%: 178,572 / 10,714 / 10,714; 18%: 169,492 / 15,254 / 15,254; 28%: 156,250 / 21,875 / 21,875; 40%: 142,857 / 28,572 / 28,571. The engine's CGST and SGST are each `round2(base x rate / 200)`.

**B.2 The bill of 2.3.** Gross 1,699.00; a 5% bill discount 84.95, allocated 4.50 / 15.50 / 64.95; net 85.50 (18%), 294.50 and 1,234.05 (5%). Bucket 5%: inclusive 1,528.55, base 1,455.77, CGST 36.39, SGST 36.39, leftover 0, line amounts 280.48 (rice) and 1,175.29 (T-shirt). Bucket 18%: inclusive 85.50, base 72.46, CGST 6.52, SGST 6.52, leftover 0. Totals: base 1,528.23, CGST 42.91, SGST 42.91, 1,614.05; party leg rounded down to the rupee 1,614.00; Round Off 0.05.

**B.3 Postings.** *S1, cash:* Dr Cash - T1 1,614.00; Dr Round Off 0.05; Cr Sales 1,528.23; Cr Output CGST 42.91; Cr Output SGST 42.91. *S2, cash 1,000.00 and UPI 614.00:* Sales: Dr Walk-in customers 1,614.00, Dr Round Off 0.05, Cr Sales 1,528.23, Cr CGST 42.91, Cr SGST 42.91; Receipt: Dr Cash - T1 1,000.00, Dr UPI Collections 614.00, Cr Walk-in customers 1,614.00; the debtor is 0.00. *Return of the rice (the whole line):* net inclusive 294.50 at 5%: base 280.48, CGST 7.01, SGST 7.01, leftover 0; Credit Note: Cr the refund tender (cash) 294.50, Dr Sales returns or Sales 280.48, Dr CGST 7.01, Dr SGST 7.01; it nets into the same B2CS row (intra, 5%) with sign -1.

**B.4 A Z report, by hand.** Float 2,000.00. Bills: #1 as B.3 (cash 1,000.00, UPI 614.00 unconfirmed); #2 cash 250.00; #3 UPI 400.00 confirmed. Cash refund 294.50 (B.3); pay-out 100.00. **Expected cash** = 2,000.00 + 1,000.00 + 250.00 - 294.50 - 100.00 = **2,855.50**. Counted 2,850.00: **short 5.50** (a Payment, Dr Cash short and excess, Cr Cash - T1); drop 2,850.00 to the safe if the float is not kept. Tender totals: cash 1,250.00 in, 294.50 out; UPI 1,014.00 (614.00 unconfirmed, 400.00 confirmed). The unconfirmed 614.00 is on the close list until the statement carries its UTR.

**B.5 The residual with cess (reproduced in revision 2).** Same method, T = 1 to 200,000 paise, the engine's rounding (CGST, SGST and cess each `round2` of its own base). Leftover of 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 paise: 28% + 22% cess: 133,333 / 45,656 / 18,667 / 2,344; 28% + 60%: 106,382 / 68,085 / 14,894 / 10,639; 18% + 1%: 168,067 / 15,313 / 15,126 / 1,494. The no-cess counts of B.1 reproduce exactly. The base search never needed more than 2 steps up or 1 step down over every case run (the slabs of B.1 plus 28% + 22%, 28% + 60%, 18% + 1% and 5% + 12%), so "at most six" is safe.

**B.6 `allocate` against the exact split (reproduced in revision 2).** 1,000,000 seeded random cases (2 to 7 weights from 1 paisa to Rs 5,000, totals from 1 paisa to Rs 4,000): 0 sums wrong and **4 cases where the engine's float largest remainder differs from the exact BigInt one by one paisa in two parts**. The first: total 117,621 over weights 402,493, 412,092, 74,624, 218,175, 16,728 and 166,114: engine 36,692 / 37,568 / 6,803 / 19,890 / 1,525 / 15,143, exact 36,693 / 37,568 / 6,803 / 19,889 / 1,525 / 15,143. A reviewer's separate run found the same four.

**B.7 The apparel band.** The tax-exclusive value of an inclusive tag P is P / 1.05 at 5% and P / 1.18 at 18%. At 5% it is above Rs 2,500 for P above Rs 2,625; at 18% it is at or below Rs 2,500 for P up to Rs 2,950. So for Rs 2,626 to Rs 2,950 the lowest-tier test sends the line up and the upper tier's own exclusive value would send it down: Rs 2,799 is 2,665.71 at 5% (over) and 2,372.03 at 18% (under); Rs 2,899 is 2,760.95 and 2,456.78; Rs 2,950 is 2,809.52 and 2,500.00; Rs 2,951 is 2,810.48 and 2,500.85 (over at both). GST on a Rs 2,799 tag: about Rs 133 on 2,666 at 5% against about Rs 427 on 2,372 at 18%, a Rs 294 difference a piece.

**B.8 The pull duplicate check, timed.** `findPullDuplicates` (copied from `syncGuard.ts`) over 3 voucher types and 10 arriving vouchers, median of 5 runs, this machine at load about 8: 120,000 vouchers 32 ms; 250,000 59 ms; 500,000 182 ms; 1,000,000 586 ms. A reviewer at load about 16 saw 113, 118, 498 and 912 ms. The shape is the finding's; the magnitude depends on the machine and a low-end PC is 3 to 5 times slower [J].

**B.9 The envelope, by arithmetic.** C = 300,000 vouchers; the roll is run at the next month start after the 80% banner, so allow up to 0.87 C = 261,000 vouchers in a period. One bill is 1.15 to 1.2 vouchers (the Receipt of a mixed or part-credit bill, plus shift vouchers) [J], so a period holds about 217,000 to 227,000 bills. Three periods a year (two rolls) is 650,000 to 680,000 bills, about 1,790 to 1,860 a day; the sellable figure is stated as 1,500 for margin and festival peaks. At 800 bills a day one terminal fills a period in 272 to 284 days: one financial year with at most one roll. At 2,400 a day (three counters at 800) a period lasts 90 to 95 days: a roll every quarter. At 3,000 a day, 72 to 76 days: a roll about every two and a half months, outside v1. The audit's own figures: 500,000 bills is day 167 at 3,000 a day and day 208 at 2,400 a day; 500,000 bills in a year is 1,370 a day. [M arithmetic]

## Appendix C. What this specification did not verify (read this before relying on any claim)

1. **Web pages and files read in revision 2 (fetched and read, not search summaries):** Chrome's IndexedDB durability post (the default became relaxed in Chrome 121; strict can be asked per transaction); Petpooja's pricing page (indicative, ex tax, platforms listed, offline billing not stated); Electron's timelines page (the three-major support policy only; it names no Chromium mapping); CBIC Circular 167/23/2021-GST and Circular 164/20/2021-GST (PDFs, text extracted locally with PDFKit: the clarifications cited in 2.4 case 12 and 4.5); the Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules 2011 book from the Department of Consumer Affairs (Rule 18(1) to (3) read); FSSAI's order on printing the licence number on bills; the CDSCO consolidated Drugs Rules PDF (Rule 65 cash memo particulars at page 43 of 874 and the prescription endorsement at page 46); Notification 10/2023-CT through taxheal (the Rs 5 crore e-invoice threshold from 1 August 2023; which preceding years count was not on the page). **Failed or not read:** the CBIC Rule 46 page failed its certificate check; the web-search budget was exhausted before any new search ran, so the s.34(2) cut-off, Rule 46 and 46A after 2021, the Rs 2,500 apparel wording and the portal tables for 9(5) supplies stay as the siblings left them: dated data with `SCHEMA?`. Everything else statutory or vendor is carried from the sibling documents with their tags [AU 16; RT 0.1; RS 0.1; PH 1; PY 6].
2. **No hardware and no live product.** Printers, drawers, scales, scanners, card terminals and soundboxes were not touched; ESC/POS facts are from a third-party list, a library and search summaries (Epson's reference refused the fetch); the printed spike, the bridge's first run on a packaged Electron, the Windows RAW helper and a scanner burst are all unrun [PY 6; TS 18].
3. **Nothing in the product was run.** No suite, build, screenshot or perf pass; the figures marked [M] are integer-arithmetic scripts (Appendix B; the revision-2 scripts are in the session scratch area, not committed) and the audit's measurements on a busy machine [AU 16], not time-to-paint in a browser; **the IndexedDB cost of a POS save, and of a strict one, is unmeasured** (slice S-dur), and so are heap and cold start at the ceiling (slice S-env).
4. **The numbers in 10.1 are judgement**, and the retail study's own tally is 2 to 3 times larger (10.1); the first slice resizes the plan.
5. **The CF branch and the tracking engines** were read, not run; their line numbers move when merged; the tracking lane's F1 and F2 findings are the pharmacy study's, pinned there by a scratch script, not by a suite in the repo [PH 7 V15].
6. **Unread statutory points the design leans on, by name:** the s.34(2) cut-off after 1 Oct 2022; Rule 46's text after 2021 and Rule 46A; Notification 14/2020's threshold after 2021; whether Rule 18(2) is judged per pack or per bill; the GST treatment of points and vouchers (commentary only); the portal table for 9(5) restaurant supplies; the composition limit after March 2019; the 15 Oct 2026 UPI merchant-discount start; the DPDP notice duties' exact date and how erasure meets retention; whether a software printout is a Rule 65 register; the Schedule H and H1 lists as of today (the study's copy lags the Gazette); how the existing Bill of Supply path treats a composition company (read at P-2's start).
7. **Not designed, said plainly:** summary posting; several GSTINs and consolidation; a wallet that can be topped up; the outlet hub beyond its risks; KDS, courses, QR ordering, recipes with live depletion; the distributor segment; OTP redemption; the Android shell; a consolidated cross-company register; the statutory dynamic QR for a taxpayer above Rs 500 crore (nobody in the target band is bound); liquor; PCI DSS (the design holds no card data); where the VM sits against the RBI's data-localisation FAQ.
8. **`docs/design/mobile-app-spec.md` exists** (revision 1 said it did not). Read for this revision: its decisions table (0.3), 2.3 (the engine entry and lock), 2.5.1, 2.5.6 (numbering), 2.6 (identity) and 3.2 to 3.6 (backend work, tables, versions). Not read: its sections on the phone UI, push, release and testing, and its 7.4 "against the desktop backlog" for collisions with the POS slices.
9. **New unverified points of revision 2:** Electron 31's Chromium version (the mapping to 126 is the known one; Electron's own page names none); the effect of a DBCore-level `durability: 'strict'` on a Dexie transaction in a packaged Electron (read in the library source, not run); that Dexie hooks fire for `bulkPut` and `bulkDelete` (a suite proves it, 2.14); Windows 7, 8 and 32-bit support in Electron (a reviewer's reading of its README, not re-fetched); the three limbs of the cash-receipt section (commentary, not the Act); whether a payer's app lets the payer edit the amount of an unsigned UPI link; which preceding years count for the Rs 5 crore e-invoice threshold; the pregabalin Gazette date; the cost of a strict commit on any disk; C = 300,000; the 1.15 to 1.2 vouchers per bill; that an IMEI carries a Luhn check digit (the IMEI standard, from memory).

## Review log (revision 2)

**Method.** Two reviewers read revision 1 (one on accounting, GST, sync and performance; one on product, compliance, hardware and UX) and returned 46 findings: 4 blockers, 23 majors, 19 minors. Each was re-checked here against the code, a script, or a page before any text changed. "Reproduced" means I saw the same thing; "narrowed" means the finding was right and its fix needed a different shape; "partly" means one part did not reproduce as stated. Six themes were found by both reviews (durability: RV2 and RV21; capacity: RV3 and RV23; the shared lane A paths: RV9 and RV35; the apparel basis: RV16 and RV26; aggregator orders: RV11, RV20 and RV45; the mobile spec: RV6 and RV28). No finding was rejected whole; the parts that did not reproduce are in the second table.

| # | Sev | Finding in a few words | Verdict | Changed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RV1 | blocker | A backup file as a terminal seed, the Replace terminal flow and the P-2 gate | Reproduced: `importCompany` queues a put per row and a delete per absent row, the header has no cursor, a `restoreId` skips the cancelled guard [C]. Narrowed: the mobile "pack" is an allowlisted projection for reps, so it cannot seed a full replica; the ordinary pull is the path and a tables-only seed is optional | PD29, 7.4, 2.5, 10.1 (P-2, P-2c), 10.4, 10.5 `pos-restore-guard` |
| RV2 | blocker | A power cut can lose a saved bill (relaxed IndexedDB durability) | Reproduced: no durability option anywhere [C]; Chrome's default is relaxed since 121 [W]. Narrowed: Dexie's public API has only a database-wide option, so a DBCore middleware makes the bill's transaction strict; the cost is unmeasured | PD39, 2.13, 1.4, 10.1 (S-dur, P-0a, P-5j), 10.4 |
| RV3 | blocker | 3,000 bills a day is outside everything the design certifies | Reproduced by arithmetic and by the audit's own table. Found while checking: `writeSplit` copies BOTH halves, so Period roll needs a one-sided writer. Decided: lane S is not built and a customer above about 1,500 bills a day is not sold in v1 (a veto item) | PD38, PD35, 2.1, 2.11, 2.15, 7.8, 1.3.1, 10.1 (S-env, P-2d), App B.9, Q21 |
| RV4 | major | A credit note's tax is computed at the return date's rate | Reproduced: a line's own rate wins, else the item's record on the voucher date [C `gst.ts:93-108`] | PD11, 2.6, 10.5 `pos-returns` |
| RV5 | major | The same bill returned on two offline terminals; stored value | Reproduced (no such race in revision 1) | PD41, 2.6, 3.6, 9.1, 10.5 |

**Where a finding's own fix was not taken as written, and why.**
- **RV1:** the finding offered a seed import or a scoped bootstrap. The mobile pack is an allowlist projection for field reps (it excludes purchases and other users' documents), so it cannot seed a counter's full replica. The pull is the path and the seed is optional (P-2c).
- **RV2:** "strict per transaction if the installed Dexie exposes it": Dexie 4.4.4 exposes only a database-wide option publicly [C `dexie.d.ts:1121`]; the per-transaction request exists at the DBCore layer [C `dexie.d.ts:565, 714`], which is where the middleware sits.
- **RV3:** "decide lane S or a hybrid before P-1": decided here as not built, with a customer above the envelope not sold; the owner may veto it with Q2's numbers.
- **RV6:** "post a dated lost-bills adjustment at the Z-report totals": adopted as one adjustment in its own voucher type, plus re-entry from copies first; Table 13 cannot be fixed in the builder, so the screen carries a note for the CA.
- **RV12:** "except a cancel by an authorised writer": the server resolves no roles, so the rule allows only a cancel shell and an identical re-put.
- **RV22:** "carry the cart into the Sales screen's e-invoice path": that screen has no prefill parameter and may not be edited by this build, so the cart becomes an Optional Sales voucher that the existing alteration route opens.

**Facts that moved while this revision was written.** Phase 30 (custom fields, reports and stock by field, the Back control) was fast-forwarded into `main` as `1d22e84` (the reviewers and revision 1 saw it as an unmerged branch), so `bulkPlan.ts`, `entryPolicy.ts`, `voucherFields.ts` and `sync/caps.ts` are on `main` and the tracking lane's T-0b gate is met; `main` is `d959028`; the MCP engine lock is stale (9 of 52 files changed in `1d22e84`); a `report-grid` worktree exists (`.wt-grid`). Line anchors I added were re-read at `d959028`.

**What this revision could not re-check.** The section 34(2) cut-off, Rule 46 and 46A after 2021, the Rs 2,500 apparel wording, the portal's 9(5) tables, the effect of a strict commit on any disk, Windows 7 and 8 support in Electron, and every competitor's behaviour beyond the pages named in Appendix C item 1. The web-search budget was exhausted; if more searching is wanted, the owner can ask in a follow-up.
